# Command Reference — File Map

Quick lookup: every CLI command → implementation file + domain client + test file.

Use this to jump straight to code without exploring the codebase.

## Naming Convention (Backend → CLI)

| Backend term | CLI term | Go package |
|---|---|---|
| dataset | table | `internal/datasets` |
| stored_item | file | `internal/stored_items` |
| data_source | cloud-source | `internal/cloud_sources` |
| alert_item | alert | `internal/alerts` |
| rule_family | rule (list) | `internal/rule_families` |
| rule_revision | rule (detail) | `internal/rule_versions` |
| dataset_rule | table-rule | `internal/dataset_rules` |
| problem_domain (formerly dataset_kind) | table-kind | `internal/dataset_kinds` |
| migration (`migration-details`) | table-schema (aliases `migration`, `table-migration`) | `internal/datasets` |
| comment_thread | comment-thread | `internal/comment_threads` |

### Table selectors

Every command a table *scopes* takes `--table <name>` and `--table-id <id>`,
except the four positional-only resource commands (`get table`, `download
data`, `undelete table`, and `share table`). Commands whose own positional slot
is free, including `describe table` and `get data`, accept both the positional
form and the flag pair.
`--table` is a name and is never parsed as an ID; `--table-id` is the ID.
Register both with `cmdutil.AddTableFlags`, read them with
`cmdutil.ResolveTableID` (required, table positional),
`cmdutil.ResolveTableIDFromFlags` (required, no table positional) or
`cmdutil.OptionalTableID` (a filter, or an org-wide default).
Before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, call the matching
`cmdutil.ValidateTableSelector`, `ValidateTableSelectorFromFlags`, or
`ValidateOptionalTableSelector` validator. These reject an explicitly empty
`--table`, non-positive `--table-id`, conflicting selector forms, and a missing
required selector as bad usage (exit 2) before config or credentials can hide
the command-line error. The lookup-facing resolvers defensively repeat the
same validation.
Both name paths share `datasets.Client.ResolveByName`: exact names only, with
one bounded same-org search for suggestions on a miss (exit 3 with the scope
note); an ambiguous exact name exits 2. A numeric `--table` miss suggests the
ID flag. Positional table arguments keep id-first, name-second resolution via
`datasets.ResolveID` — they are the convenience path, the flags are the
unambiguous one. `internal/catalog`'s `TestTableFlagIsAlwaysAName`,
`TestTableIDFlagIsAlwaysAnInt`, `TestTableSelectorFlagsComeInPairs`, and
`TestEveryTableSelectorRejectsLocalErrorsBeforeCredentials` enforce this on
the real tree; the pairing check is the one that prevents a lone String
`--table` from quietly meaning "name or ID".
`get alerts` / `get warnings` also accept `--dataset-id` as a hidden alias of
`--table-id`. `--filter table-id=<n>` is not a flag — it is a `--filter
key=value` payload key on `run`/`kill ingestion-job` and `run`/`kill
profiling-job`, and it stays ID-only.

## Discovery surfaces

| surface | source of truth | file |
|---|---|---|
| `qctl api-resources` | `catalog.Build(cmd.Root())` | `internal/commands/api_resources/api_resources.go` |
| `qctl explain <res>` | the resource page, then the live schema | `internal/commands/explain/explain.go` |
| `qctl guide <topic>` | `internal/commands/guide/topics/*.md` | `internal/commands/guide/topics.go` |

Existence is owned by the cobra tree: every command carries a `resource`
annotation (`catalog.For`, `catalog.Parent`, `catalog.Dispatcher`). Semantics
are owned by one markdown page per resource under
`internal/resources/pages/`. Adding a command without both fails CI —
see `internal/catalog/*_test.go`.

## Behavior changes

- `TableSeries` manifests export and accept `spec.period_column` and `spec.period_column_kind` (`period_key|date`) as a pair. Omit both to preserve the setting or supply both as null to clear it. Apply and diff plan a changed pair as a final PATCH after membership edits, including after a series create.
- `TableSeries` manifests export and accept `spec.deadline_schedule` (`internal/table_series/schedule.go` `DeadlineScheduleSpec`): the 23 keys of the schedule PATCH, flat — all eleven overrides, `null` where none is stored (no `omitempty`), and all twelve `*_enabled` switches. `get table-series <ref> -o json|yaml` also GETs `/table-series/{id}/deadline-schedule` and fails, with nothing on stdout, when that read does. An omitted block leaves the schedule unread and unwritten; inside a present block an omitted key, override or switch, keeps its stored value, and an override written as `null` resets it to its default (`Manifest.scheduleNulls` reads which keys were written as null, since a nil `*int` cannot tell). `deadline_schedule: null`, an empty block `{}` and a null switch exit 2 before authentication. Apply and diff plan one schedule PATCH of the changed keys as the last request (`Plan.planDeadlineSchedule`, `Request.Schedule`), after the period-column PATCH and on a create against `<created-series-id>`; apply stores its response in `Plan.Schedule`, and `apply -o json|yaml` carries `spec.deadline_schedule` only when the plan read or wrote it.
- `get data --anchor-row-id <id>` opens the row's page within the current filters and ordering, overriding `--page`. The Markdown summary and next-page hint use the resolved page; JSON/YAML retain the server's `page`. The hint tells callers to remove the anchor before continuing with `--page`. Anchored 404s preserve exit 3 and name the selected bucket and explicit filters; a non-positive anchor or limit exits 2 before any request.
- `qctl explain <resource>` no longer prints its GOTCHAS by default: the
  section is one line counting them. `--gotchas` prints them in full, as
  before, and `--grep <term>` prints only the gotchas that contain the term
  (ignoring case), without the rest of the page. Both exit 2 with `-o json`,
  `-o yaml`, a `<resource>.<field>` path, or a command name, none of which
  prints a page. A script that read gotchas from the default output must add
  `--gotchas`.
- Empty positional arguments are rejected by the shared Args validator with exit 2 and the command's usage before authentication or requests. `get standard-fields --table-kind ""` also exits 2 instead of listing standard fields without the filter.
- Schema apply and `alert resolve --action apply_migration` (including `-f`) preview running ingestion jobs on stderr even under `--yes`, marking dry-run carriers. `describe table` independently lists those jobs as blockers of a confirmed draft, with `jobs.running_ingestion` in JSON/YAML; `rules.pending_validation` and the text Rules line count pending validation separately from `other`. Lookup failures warn without refusing a mutation or claiming a job blocker.
- `explain table-rule -o json|yaml` and `explain table-rule.spec` now include the additive `row_filter` field and its conditions, description, and predicate schema.

- `get table-kind <ref> -o yaml|json` now exports an applyable `kind: TableKind` manifest (`metadata.slug`, `spec.name`, `spec.description`, the complete `spec.concepts` slug list, and `status.id`) instead of the raw problem-domain response. Scripts reading its old top-level `id`, `name` or `concepts` must read `status.id`, `spec.name` or `spec.concepts`.
- Interactive confirmation questions are written to stderr, so `-o json`,
  `-o yaml`, and `-o name` keep stdout machine-readable while waiting for an
  answer. In particular, `qctl share table -o json|yaml` no longer requires
  `--yes` at an interactive terminal; scripts without an interactive stdin
  still need `--yes`.
- `qctl explain <resource>` (table output) no longer fails when the API server
  is unreachable or unconfigured: it prints the resource page, warns on stderr,
  and exits 0. A script that relied on `explain` failing while the server was
  down will now see success. `-o json`, `-o yaml`, and `explain <res>.<field>`
  are unchanged.
- `qctl api-resources` output changed shape: the `VERB` column became `VERBS`
  (all verbs, comma-separated), row order is now class-then-alphabetical, and
  the row count went from 18 to 26. `-o json` keeps `verb` with its old meaning
  and adds `verbs`, `schema`, and `summary`. The `explain` field is now
  populated on every row — every resource has a page, so `qctl explain <name>`
  always answers. A consumer that filtered on `explain == ""` to find the rows
  with no schema is now silently matching nothing: filter on `schema == ""`
  instead.
- With no server configured, `qctl explain <resource> -o json` now succeeds
  instead of failing at bootstrap for the manifest-backed resources whose
  schema ships in the binary — those schemas need no API call. They are the
  rows whose `schema` in `qctl api-resources -o json` begins `manifest:`;
  every `openapi:` row's `-o json`/`-o yaml` still needs a configured server,
  and machine output against a configured server is unchanged.
- `qctl <cmd> -o <value>` now rejects a format the command cannot render, with
  exit 2 and the valid values on stderr. Previously any value was accepted and
  silently rendered as something else (a table, YAML, Markdown or JSON depending
  on the command). The per-command vocabularies are pinned in
  `internal/commands/root/output_format_test.go` (`declaredOutputFormats`),
  which is the authority — every command not listed there accepts exactly
  `table|json|yaml|name`. (Six commands register a local `--output` of their
  own and are never format-validated: `download file`, `download data`,
  `download alert-report`, `download issues-report` and `download deliverable-run`, where it is a destination path, and
  `config set-context`, which validates the value it is about to store
  itself.) What narrowed:
  - `-o name` is now an error on all 16 `describe` commands, `get data`,
    `get table-kind`, `explain`, `api-resources`, and `inspect dry-run-job`.
    It was accepted everywhere before, but honored only where
    the command routes through a printer.
  - passing `-o table` explicitly is now an error on `inspect dry-run-job`,
    which has no table renderer and defaults to JSON. The root flag's own
    default is still `table`, but an unpassed flag is never validated, so the
    command keeps working with no `-o` at all.
  - `get rule` gained `-o code` as a *declared* value (it always worked) and
    still accepts all four standard formats, `name` included.
  - `-o json`, `-o yaml` and `-o name` are now errors on the 8 issue and
    review verbs that print prose alone:
    `comment-thread delete-comment`, `review delete-comment`, `review
    link-dry-run`, `queue-message retry` and `resurrect`, `rows set-cell` and
    `unignore-rule`, and `suggestion dismiss` — each a `table` line in
    `declaredOutputFormats`. They printed their prose at exit 0 under every
    format. `TestIssueAndReviewVerbsDeclareTheirFormats` fails for a leaf of
    the issue and review groups (and `privilege` and `share`) that declares no
    set, so a new verb cannot slip back to the default four.
  - `-o name` is now an error on the 22 of the next bullet's 44 verbs whose
    result names no one object a command takes: the four `data-review` cell
    verdicts, the comment verbs (`comment-thread reply` and `edit-comment`,
    `data-review comment`, `review comment` and `edit-comment`), the ten
    `rows` writes, `notification read` and `dismiss`, and `rule-upgrade
    notify`.
  - `-o name` is now an error on `review audit`, which printed nothing at
    exit 0 because an audit entry has no name.
  - `-o name` is now an error on `auth me`, `get privilege-audit`,
    `get rule-ignores`, `get data-review-policy` and `get rule-review-policy`
    (qctl-3z8v.9.9): they list nothing a command takes as a `kind/<id>`, and
    the two policies printed nothing at exit 0.
  - `-o json`, `-o yaml` and `-o name` are now errors on 19 local and
    session commands: `auth login`, `logout`, `sessions revoke` and
    `switch-org`, `cache aliases clear` and `resolve`, `config use-context`
    and `delete-context`, the five `completion` leaves, `docs`, `guide`,
    `skill install` and `uninstall`, `diff` and `help`. They printed their
    prose, script, page or diff under every format. `-o name` is also an
    error on `info`, `config current-context`, `version` and `cache aliases
    list`.
    `TestLocalCommandsDeclareTheirFormats` keeps every runnable command of
    those groups declared.
  - `-o json`, `-o yaml` and `-o name` are now errors on 20 mutating verbs
    that print prose: the `delete` leaves other than `rules`, `enable` and
    `disable` `cloud-source` and `rule`, `set-default rule`, both `kill`
    leaves, `run` `ingestion-job`, `profiling-job` and `sensor-job`, and
    `undelete cloud-source` and `file`. `-o name` is also an error on
    `submit rules`, whose result names no one revision, and `-o table` on
    `create dry-run-job`.
    `TestMutatingVerbsDeclareTheirFormats` keeps every runnable command of
    the 17 mutating groups declared, except the `download` leaves, whose own
    `--output` is a file path.
- Seven verbs that create something an agent addresses next print it under
  `-o json|yaml|name`, where they printed their prose result lines under every
  format: `alert submit`, `warning submit`, `comment-thread create`,
  `data-review request`, `review request`, `suggestion accept` and
  `suggestion edit` (`accept` has no name view). The prose moves to stderr
  under those formats through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, and the table view is
  unchanged; the notes on each group give the shapes. A context carrying
  `output: json` or `yaml` therefore switches these seven to a document,
  while the 8 narrowed verbs above keep printing prose for it, because
  `ApplyContextOutputFormat` applies a context's value only to a command that
  supports it.
- Seven mutating verbs print what they did under `-o json|yaml`
  (qctl-3z8v.9.16), where they printed their prose under every format: `delete
  rules`, `submit rules`, `submit table-kinds`, `run rule-run-replay`,
  `undelete table`, `upload file` and `create dry-run-job`. All but `submit
  rules` print a `kind/<id>` under `-o name`. The prose, including `upload
  file`'s confirmation block and progress lines, moves to stderr through
  `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, and several `-f` files print one List through
  `WriteRun`, as `apply -f` does; the notes on each group give the shapes.
  `create dry-run-job` takes `json`, `yaml` and `name`, and with no `-o` still
  prints its JSON. A context carrying `output: json` or `yaml` now applies to
  all seven.
- 44 issue and review verbs that report what they changed print a result
  document under `-o json|yaml` (qctl-3z8v.9.14), where they printed their
  prose under every format: the server's answer, or one qctl builds where the
  answer is not safe to print (`alert`/`warning resolve`, whose response can
  carry a stored file URL's cloud-provider auth fields) or says too little
  (`notification`, `table-rule move`). They are `alert` and `warning`
  `resolve` and `retract`; `comment-thread reply`, `edit-comment`, `resolve` and
  `unresolve`; nine `data-review` verbs (the four cell verdicts, `assign`,
  `unassign`, `close`, `re-request`, `comment`); eleven `review` verbs (the
  three decisions, `withdraw`, `close`, `reopen`, `assign`, `unassign`,
  `re-request`, `comment`, `edit-comment`); the ten `rows` writes other than
  `set-cell` and `unignore-rule`; `queue-message resurrect-dead` and
  `retire`; `notification read` and `dismiss`; `rule-upgrade notify`; and
  `table-rule move`. A result naming one object prints its `kind/<id>` under
  `-o name`. The prose, including pre-write output such as a preview or the
  `apply_migration` pre-flight, moves to stderr through
  `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, and the table view is unchanged; the notes on each
  group give the shapes. A context carrying `output: json` or `yaml` now
  applies to all 44.
- `-o name` prints `kind/<id>` (qctl-3z8v.9.9): the singular catalog
  resource name and the object's id, because names are not unique. The
  reflection printers take the kind from `output.NameKind(cmd)` and the id
  from the `id` JSON field (`Printer.NameAs` names another). Every reference
  positional strips a `kind/` prefix its slot accepts (`cmdutil.StripKindRefs`
  from root's init, slot kinds from `cmdutil.WithArgKinds`, defaulting to the
  command's resource), and so does every id flag whose kind `-o name` prints
  (`cmdutil.RefInt`, `RefIntVar`, `RefString`, `RefStringArray`,
  `RefIntSlice`; derived `get data`/`download data` flags through
  `paramflags.refFlagKinds`; `TestIDFlagsTakeTheirKind` pins the inventory and
  the exceptions such as `share table --to-id`); a prefix the slot does not
  take exits 2. Lists that printed a name now print
  the id (`get tables`, `cloud-sources`, `destinations`, `backup-settings`,
  `concepts`, `table-kinds`, `orgs` with the full UUID, `auth sessions`), and
  the bespoke emitters were re-keyed: `linked-table/<table id>`,
  `table/<new id>` from `copy table` (whose `--dry-run -o name` exits 2),
  `alert/<id>` from `get latest-alert`, `table-rule/<id>` from
  `get rule-upgrades`, `table-kind/<uuid>` from `get table-kind-suggestions`.
  `get table-schema`, `get value-recommendations` and `get standard-field-suggestions`
  keep their column names, values and `<column>/<slug>`. The cost: a name
  shaped exactly like `<kind>/<id>` no longer reaches its object through a
  positional; the name-only flags (`--table`, `--cloud-source`, ...) never
  strip.
- `apply -f` and its typed subcommands now accept `-o name` (qctl-1xrq).
  They print each returned resource reference on its own line, in file order,
  including unchanged and partial results. Most references use the resource
  ID; a schema names its table and a staged table rule names its suggestion.
  A linked-table validation or response without a table ID uses the manifest
  name. Result prose, warnings and multi-document summaries go to stderr.
- `apply -f` and the five `apply <resource>` subcommands print a document for
  every kind under `-o json`, and now take `-o yaml` too (qctl-3z8v.9.6). A
  one-document file prints its object; a file of several prints one
  `output.List` after the run, where it printed one bare object per document.
  `Rule`, `TableRule`, `TableSchema` and both review policies printed prose
  under `-o json`. A context carrying `output: yaml` therefore now switches
  `apply` to YAML.
- `info` and `config current-context` print the current context as a document
  under `-o json|yaml`, where they printed their text under every format. It
  carries a `scope` block with the server and organization after `--server`,
  `--org`, `QCTL_SERVER` and `QCTL_ORG`, which the text view does not show. A
  context carrying `output: json` or `yaml` therefore switches plain `qctl
  info` to the document.
- `version`, `config list-contexts` and `cache aliases list` print a document
  under `-o json|yaml` (qctl-3z8v.9.15), where they printed their text under
  every format: `{version, commit, current_context}`, one `{current, name,
  server, organization_id, organization_name, output}` per context, and one
  `{alias, resource, uuid}` per alias with `dataset_rule` named `table-rule`.
  The two lists print `[]` when empty instead of their prose line, and the
  table views are byte-identical. `list-contexts -o name` prints
  `context/<name>`: `context` is a name-keyed kind in `cmdutil.refKinds`, so
  `ParseKindRef` strips it only for a slot that takes a context (`set-context`,
  `use-context`, `delete-context`) and passes `context/...` through as a name
  everywhere else. A context carrying `output: json` or `yaml` therefore
  switches all three plain commands to the document.
- **Every context is protected unless allowlisted, so upgrading protects
  every existing context.** A context with no `protected` key is protected,
  and on one a destructive command needs `--context <name>` (a terminal is
  asked y/N; a script or agent gets exit 2). `config set-context <name>
  --protected=false` allowlists a context; it needs an interactive terminal.
- `info`, `config current-context` and `config list-contexts` carry a
  `protected` key under `-o json|yaml`, true for a context with no key, and
  the `list-contexts` table gains a PROTECTED column reading `yes` or `no`.
  The `info`/`current-context` text adds a `Protected:` line for every
  context, naming the allowlist command when it reads `yes`.
- `config delete-context` no longer treats a protected context specially.
- `--context <name>` is a global flag now, selecting a context for one
  invocation without switching to it. `get orgs --context` keeps working
  through it; an unknown name still exits 2, its message now prefixed
  `failed to load config:`.
- The `output` key in a context is now applied when `--output` is not passed —
  before, it was written by `config set-context --output` and never read.
  It behaves exactly as if the value had been typed on the command line,
  `Changed` flag included, so it reaches the commands that branch on whether
  `--output` was passed. For most `describe` commands that is a visible
  change on upgrade: a context carrying `output: table` flips
  `qctl describe destination 42` from its default YAML manifest to the
  printer's table view, because those commands print YAML only when
  `--output` was not given. Set the key to `yaml` to keep the manifests;
  removing it altogether means editing `$HOME/.qctl/config`, since
  `config set-context --output ""` is a no-op. `describe table` and
  `describe cloud-source` are not affected — neither has a manifest default
  to flip: each renders the same text summary regardless of whether
  `--output` was passed at all.
- `qctl explain <resource> -o json|yaml`, and the same for a dotted
  `<resource>.<field>`, now print one document in both formats, keyed as
  OpenAPI keys a schema: `name`, `type`, `format`, `description`, `required`,
  `readOnly`, `default`, `enum`, `nullable`, `ref`, `items`, `properties`,
  `anyOf` and `oneOf`, with an empty key left out. `-o json` used to print
  the Go field names (`Name`, `ReadOnly`, `Fields`, …) with every key
  present, and `-o yaml` the same names lowercased (`readonly`, `fields`), so
  a script reading either must switch. The schema's field list is now `properties`, like an object
  field's.
- `qctl explain <resource>.<field>` on a resource whose `schema` begins
  `openapi:` now exits 2 for a field the schema does not have, or for a path
  that descends through a field that is not an object, as the `manifest:`
  resources already did; it used to exit 1. A path through a nullable field
  that an OpenAPI 3.1 spec writes as `anyOf` with no `type` used to panic; it
  now descends into the field when it wraps an object, and exits 2 otherwise.
- `qctl explain` on an `openapi:` resource documents a field the server's
  OpenAPI 3.1 spec makes nullable — an `anyOf` of one schema and
  `{type: null}`, with no `type` of its own — as the schema it wraps, with
  `nullable: true`. Its `type`, `format`, `enum`, `ref`, `items` and
  `properties` used to be missing, on 249 of the 435 top-level fields the 16
  `openapi:` resources had when this changed. A field with two or more
  non-null alternatives lists them under `anyOf` (or `oneOf`), each without a
  `name`, and the text view joins them: `integer | string(uuid)`. The text
  view also names an array of arrays in full, `[][]string` where it used to
  read `[]array`.
- `qctl explain`'s text view names a field whose schema sets no type at all —
  JSON Schema's "any value", such as `alert.body.recommended_settings_value`
  — `any`, where it used to print an empty type (`<>` in the field list, a
  blank `TYPE:`), and an array of them `[]any` where it used to read
  `[]object`. `-o json` and `-o yaml` still leave its `type` out.
- Paged `get` lists print a List, `{kind: List, items, page, limit, total,
  next_page}`, under `-o json|yaml` instead of a bare array, so `jq '.[]'`
  becomes `jq '.items[]'`; the CHANGELOG names every list. Under the same
  formats `get rules`, `table-rules`, `rule-revisions`, `table-kinds`,
  `concepts`, `orgs` and `files` carry the API's objects instead of the table's
  display rows. `get rules` items are rule families, so a revision id is
  `.items[].primary_revision.id`. `describe alert`, `describe dry-run-job`,
  `describe table`, `describe table-rule` and `get table-rule` print RFC3339
  times where they printed "3 days ago".
- `qctl describe cloud-source <id>` prints a text summary where it used to
  print the `kind: CloudSource` manifest, and its `-o json|yaml` emit that
  summary (`cloud_sources.Summary`) instead of the manifest. The manifest moved
  to `qctl get cloud-source <id> -o yaml|json`, as the table's did to `get
  table`. A script that saved `describe cloud-source` output to re-apply must
  switch to `get`.
- Every watch and wait ends, and its exit code says how. A wait for one thing
  that runs out of time exits 124 (`qerrors.ExitTimeout`, through
  `qerrors.NewTimedOut`), where the `--wait` and download-file waits and
  `inspect dry-run-job --follow` exited 1 and
  `get latest-alert --watch --exit-on-change` exited 0. A stream watch that
  runs out of time exits 0 after a `timed out after …; resume with: …` stderr
  line. SIGINT and SIGTERM exit 130 and 143 (`*qerrors.Interrupted`, whose
  signal `qerrors.Exit` re-raises), where Ctrl-C exited 0 from the list,
  job-activity, dry-run-job and latest-alert watches and from
  `inspect dry-run-job --follow`, and SIGTERM exited 0 from
  `get latest-alert --watch`. A watch's `--timeout`
  defaults to the command's own limit, none or 10m for `inspect dry-run-job
  --follow`, whether or not stdin is a terminal;
  `QCTL_WATCH_TIMEOUT` (`config.WatchTimeoutFromEnv`) overrides that default
  and leaves `--wait-timeout` alone. The shared machinery is
  `internal/cmdutil/watch.go` (`AddWatchTimeoutFlag`, `WatchBudget`,
  `StartWait`, `Waiter.Ended`, `ResumeCommand`); the get, apply, copy/merge,
  create/inspect and download notes below say how each command uses it.

## get commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Printer | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `get tables` | `internal/commands/get/datasets.go` (+ `datasets_matches.go` for `--for-rule`) | `datasets.NewClient` (+ `dataset_kinds.Client.ResolveDatasetKindID` for `--table-kind`; `table_series.Client.ResolveID` for `--series`; `rule_versions.Client.GetRuleRevisionDatasetMatches` for `--for-rule`) | `output.NewPrinterFromCmd` + `tableui.Render` (series name, period lock marker, attention score and top-alert summary; `-v` adds health counts); `--for-rule` uses `tableui.PrintFromCmd` | `datasets_test.go`, `datasets_series_test.go`, `datasets_attention_test.go`, `datasets_matches_test.go` |
| `get tables-overview` | `internal/commands/get/tables_overview.go` (+ `tables_overview_display.go`) | `datasets.Client.GetTablesOverview` (`internal/datasets/overview.go`); `dataset_kinds.Client.ResolveDatasetKindID` for `--table-kind` | `tableui.PrintFromCmd` over explicit table/series rows; raw union List for JSON/YAML; mixed table/series references for name | `tables_overview_test.go` |

Notes:
- `get rules` lists rule **families** (grouped by name), not individual revisions.
- `get rule <name> -o code` outputs raw Python source.
- `get rule <name> -o yaml` outputs apply-compatible `kind: Rule` manifest.
- `get table-rules` accepts the table as a positional argument (ID or name) or via `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>`; `get table-rule` requires one of those two flags. Both register the pair through `cmdutil.AddTableFlags` and resolve it with `cmdutil.ResolveTableID` — `--table` is a NAME and is never parsed as an ID.
- `get table-schema` / `describe table-schema` also answer to the aliases `migration` and `table-migration` (the backend/API term; endpoint `datasets/{id}/migration-details`). The same aliases are declared in the `table-schema` resource page's frontmatter (`internal/resources/pages/table-schema.md`), so `qctl api-resources` and `qctl explain migration` surface them.
- `get table-schema -o name` prints column names via the server-cached fields endpoint, which unions the current (applied) and draft schemas into one unlabelled list; it never falls back to the details endpoint. All other formats use the details endpoint. `-o json|yaml` emits a `kind: TableSchema` apply manifest (one block per column: type, args, nullability, indexing, display flags/precision, plus a `status` block that apply ignores), not the raw response — the raw response, with per-field diffs and notable values, is `describe table-schema -v -o json|yaml`. Verbosity does not change `get`'s structured output.
- `get linked-table` takes the linked table positionally or by `--table`/`--table-id`, and `--revision`, exactly as `enable linked-table` does (`linked_tables.Client.ResolveRevision`). It only reads: the revision with its group (`GET /composite-spec-revisions/{id}`, `GetRevisionDetail`), the table (`GET /datasets/{id}`: name, table coordinates, `destination_name`, `backup_settings_id`), each source table by id, and the backup settings by id for their name. For YAML/JSON exports of a revision not yet enabled, it lists that revision's unfinished composite dry runs and reads their details to detect a pending auto-enable. `linked_tables.ExportManifest` builds the manifest: `metadata.name` is the composite spec group's name (the default table view retains the table's display name), `spec.spine.source` is the one source reading the group's spine table, `spec.release` and `spec.dry_run` are left out (the release is `status.release`), and `spec.desired_state` is `enabled` for an enabled revision or one with a pending auto-enable. `linked_tables.LoadManifest` decodes into `LinkedTableManifestWithStatus`, so `apply -f` and `diff -f` accept the `status:` block and do not use it. A 403 on the backup settings read, or backup settings with no name, leaves `spec.backup_settings` out with a `Warning:` on stderr; any other failed read exits with its own code. A join clause key the manifest cannot hold exits 5.
- `get rule-reviews --table <name>` / `--table-id <id>` scope the per-rule listing (`--rule` mode only) via `cmdutil.OptionalTableID`; unset means no dataset scope filter at all, which is why the resolved id is carried as a `*int`.
- `get rule-review-policy` / `get data-review-policy`: the table selector is optional (omitted = org policy, set = effective table policy) — `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>` via `cmdutil.AddTableFlags` + `cmdutil.OptionalTableID`, so `--table` is a NAME and is never parsed as an ID; `-o yaml` outputs an apply-compatible manifest.
- `get change-review-policy` follows the same selector rule, but reads the STORED organization policy with no selector (404 → exit 3) and the effective policy (`GET .../change-review-policy/effective`) with one, where `in_force: false` is exit 3 too. The table view is one row per key from `review_policies.ToChangeReviewEntryDisplays` (key parts joined with `/`), with a `LISTED` column and a `false` row per uncovered key on the effective view, and a summary line on stderr. `--template` (`GET .../change-review-policy/template`) prints YAML unless `-o` was set explicitly, and conflicts with `--table`/`--table-id` (exit 2). `spec.operations` is an untyped `map[string]any`, so both encoders print its keys sorted.
- `get data [id-or-name]`: fetches ONE backend page (default `--page 1 --limit 20`). The table can instead be supplied by the standard `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>` pair through `cmdutil.ResolveTableID`; `--table` is the exact-name route for a numeric-looking name that the positional would parse as an ID. Default output is an LLM Markdown payload via `internal/dataview`; it applies the response's per-field numeric display formats and marks a quarantined cell listed in `not_converted` as `[U]` without reformatting its source text. `-o json`/`-o yaml` emit the raw backend response; `-o table` (and the unset default) also renders Markdown, and `-o name` is rejected with exit 2 — this command has no name view. Every `GetDataParams` query parameter is auto-derived as a flag (see `data_flags.go`) except the denylist (`full_values`, `oauth_session`, `alert_item_id`) — including the scalar-UUID ones (`--comment-thread-id`, `--review-group-id`), which `internal/paramflags`' `KindForField` maps via `KindUUID`; `TestNoParamIsSilentlyUnsupported` fails if a future parameter is dropped instead. Denylisted from DERIVATION is not the same as absent from the CLI: `--alert-item-id` IS a flag, hand-registered by `paramflags.RegisterAlertItemIDFlag` on both `get data` and `download data`, because its `int | str` wire type is emitted as an inline anonymous struct that is both unconstructible outside `package api` and unserializable by the generated request builder — the value goes on the wire through `datasets.WithAlertItemID`, a `RequestEditorFn`. `--include-comment-threads` and `--include-issues` are the exceptions: both are hand-registered in `data.go`, not derived, because each is an extra request (the `comment_threads` by-rows overlay, chunked at 100 row ids; and one `alerts-msgs-list` / `warnings-msgs-list` batch per kind over the page's deduped ids) rather than a query parameter, and both are refused with `-o json|yaml` by the shared `validateOverlayFlags`. The `alert_item_ids_` / `warning_item_ids_` columns are consumed by `internal/dataview` into per-cell `[A<id>]`/`[W<id>]` markers and an Issues column, so neither is rendered raw in the Markdown view. `steward_overrides` is consumed the same way: it is not a column but a per-row marker list beside `results`, absent from a page with no pinned cell, and `internal/dataview/steward.go` turns it into an `[S]` after a pinned cell's other markers, a `Steward-Pinned Cells` count in the summary, and a `STEWARD OVERRIDES` section with the value each pin displaced, formatted like the cell. Nor is any other reserved column: `internal/dataview/reserved.go` mirrors atlas's `RESERVED_COLUMNS` and classifies each name shown / folded / hidden — `id` is shown, the five quarantine-lifecycle columns (`suspected_duplicate_` among them) fold into a `Row State` column, together with the per-row `approval_hold_reasons` and `waiting_for_approval` lists a quarantine page carries beside `results` (an undecided row prints `waiting-for-approval` when its entry is true, else `held-for-approval=<reason>`, and a list whose length differs from `results` is an error), `initial_signature_` folds into a `ROW SIGNATURES` dictionary keyed by row id, and the rest are dropped, with the summary naming every one. The classification is an ALLOWLIST, so a column qctl does not recognise is data and is always rendered; `--fields <reserved-column>` renders one raw instead and drops it from the fold. `--show-row-logs` folds `row_logs_` into a CELL HISTORY section with per-change checks, row approvals, submission membership and flag lifecycle events; `--fields row_logs_` keeps the raw column instead. `cell_flag_events` is aligned with `results`, marks displayed cells with `[F]`, and includes fields outside the grid in the section; a non-null sidecar with a different row count is an error. `renderDataMarkdown` hands `dataview.Normalize` the names from the built `GetDataParams.Fields`, which `paramflags.Build` has already split on commas and trimmed through `splitFieldNames`, so it matches the names the request sent. `reserved_test.go` pins the classification and `reserved_page_test.go` carries fixtures built from the real reserved sets (eleven columns on a clean page, nineteen on a quarantine page). The two deletion columns fold into `Row State` too, through `internal/dataview/deletes.go`: `deleted_kind_` as `deleted=<DeletionKind name>` and `row_delete_id_` as `row-delete=<id>`, with a `ROW DELETES` section built from `row_deletes`; a clean page shows `Row State` only when a row on it is deleted, and the summary splits `Matching Rows` into live and deleted whenever `--deleted-rows` is not `live` or a deleted row matched. Local validation of the derived query flags is deliberately narrow: non-positive `--page`/`--limit`, values outside generated enum sets, a malformed UUID, a `--fields` that names no column once split, and active quarantine-only options without `--is-bad-data` are refused with exit 2 before table-name resolution; the shared quarantine-only inventory (`badDataOnlyHelp`, `internal/paramflags/quarantinehold.go`) covers `--quarantine-hold`, `--held-for-approval`, `--waiting-for-approval`, `--approval-hold-reason`, `--passes-validation`, `--awaiting-processing`, `--dataset-rule-ids`, `--include-dataset-rule-ids`, `--review-status`, `--review-group-id`, `--review-change-history-id` and `--request-state`, while `download data` checks only the subset its generated route accepts. `validateDataRequestState` in `data_flags.go` also requires `--review-group-id` for `--request-state` and checks the state against `--request-view` (default `approver`) before any request; `--request-view` alone remains accepted. `--alert-item-id` has a separate shared validator: non-positive is exit 2, and so is the flag without `--is-bad-data`, because the backend applies the filter only under `is_bad_data` but does not list it in the bad-data-only 400 set, so a clean-rows request would otherwise answer 200 with the whole unfiltered page.
- `setDefaultColumns(cmd, "col1,col2")` is defined in `internal/commands/get/ingestion_jobs.go` and used across get commands.
- `watch.go` holds `lwWatchFlags`, `watchList` and `watchPoll`, the polling `--watch` of `get ingestion-jobs`, `profiling-jobs`, `queue-messages` and `dry-run-jobs`. `lwWatchFlags` reads `--watch` and `--timeout` (`cmdutil.AddWatchTimeoutFlag` with no limit by default, resolved by `cmdutil.WatchBudget`), refuses `--timeout` without `--watch` with exit 2, and renders the resume command before `setDefaultColumns` marks `--columns` as set. `watchList` runs the poll as a `cmdutil.WatchStream` wait under `cmdutil.StartWait`: one stderr banner (`watching ingestion jobs every 5s, for up to 10m0s; resume with: …` under `--timeout 10m`), SIGINT/SIGTERM exit 130/143, and running out of `--timeout` is exit 0 after a `timed out after …; resume with: …` stderr line (`Waiter.Ended`). Each poll returns the same `output.List` the one-shot listing prints, through the command's `listPage` helper, and `output.Stream` (`internal/output/stream.go`) writes it unless it is unchanged: a compact List per line in json, a `---` document in yaml, the changed items' names in name, and a table redrawn on a terminal or appended off one. Poll retry notices go through `Stream.NoticeWriter`; the poll context carries the same writer (`client.WithDiagnosticWriter`) for HTTP retry notices and verbose HTTP logs, so the next changed table starts below a notice and later updates redraw there. A first poll that fails ends the watch; after it, a failure `qerrors.IsPermanent` does not report is written to stderr as `poll failed (…); retrying in …` and retried after `backoffFor`'s wait (`latest_alert_watch.go`), and a permanent one ends the watch with its exit code.
- `get rule-suggestions` accepts the table as a positional argument (ID or name) or via `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>`; defaults to `--state pending` (repeatable `--state`, validated client-side against pending/applied/dismissed/failed) and also accepts `--source periodic|profiling|recreated_period|user`, with either enum rejecting a typo locally at exit 2 before auth or table-name resolution; `--page` and `--limit` are validated locally too. It declares its own --columns (default: id, rule, dataset_columns, action, state, source, created). `--show-params` adds a `params` column of compact JSON, reading full details for each displayed row; `--columns id,params` also fetches it. These reads stay on the selected page and are skipped for JSON/YAML/name. `--show-kind` adds a `type` column (candidate|edit) directly from `DatasetRuleSuggestionTinySchema.is_revalidation_candidate`, without detail reads of its own; it is inert under `-o json|yaml|name`, whose raw response carries the field independently of the flag.
- `--job-activity` is a flag on `get table <id>`, not a standalone `get job-activity` command. Add `-w`/`--watch` to stream updates over a websocket instead of one REST fetch (`--provider first-party|third-party`, `--changes-only` to suppress unchanged frames). A watch first reads the table over REST (`GetDataset`, or `GetThirdPartyDataset` for `--provider third-party`); a failed read returns its reason and exit code before any dial or watch banner. `-o name` with `--job-activity` exits 2 before any request: counts have no name. So do `--watch` without `--job-activity`, an explicit `--changes-only` or `--provider` without both `--job-activity` and `--watch` (even `--changes-only=false` or `--provider first-party`), and `--timeout` without `--watch` (`jawTableWatchFlags` in `dataset.go`). `runJobActivityWatch` (`job_activity.go`) runs the stream as a `cmdutil.WatchStream` wait: `--timeout` (`cmdutil.AddWatchTimeoutFlag`, no limit by default) running out is exit 0 after the `timed out after …; resume with: qctl get table <t> --job-activity --watch` line, SIGINT/SIGTERM exit 130/143, each redial prints `ws.ReconnectNotice`'s line on stderr; it and verbose WebSocket logs share `Stream.NoticeWriter` through the watch context, and a stream that ends with an error returns it, so close 1008 (atlas's `Authentication expired or revoked.`) exits 4.
- `get dry-run-job <id> --watch` reads the job over REST first, so a missing job exits 3 before any dial, and a job already in a terminal state is written once through `newDryRunJobFrameWriter` and judged without opening the stream. The terminal states are `dry_runs.IsTerminal` (`internal/dry_runs/verdict.go`), atlas's `_TERMINAL_STATES` (`capi/capi/views/dry_run_view_core.py`): finished, error, killed, stopped, version_id_mismatch, file_missing, blocked and partitioned. Otherwise `dry_runs.Client.Follow` (`internal/dry_runs/follow.go`) streams the job's progress as a `cmdutil.WaitUntilDone` wait bounded by `--timeout` (no limit by default; exit 2 without `--watch`). A terminal frame other than finished, and a stream the server closes before one, are settled by reading the job again, and `dry_runs.Client.Verdict` decides the exit: 0 for finished, 1 for any other terminal state, with the job's rule load errors, error message and a `Next:` line (for a blocked job, the alert that blocks it, read from its ingestion job when the dry run names none). Running out of `--timeout` exits 124 with the resume line and leaves the job running; a 401/403 handshake or close 1008 exits 4; each redial prints `ws.ReconnectNotice`'s line on stderr. `inspect dry-run-job --follow` shares all of it.
- `get files --deleted` lists deleted (ignored) files ONLY: the server's `is_trashed` is an equality filter, so active and deleted files never appear in one listing. Unset sends nothing and the server's active-files default applies.
- `get files --table <name>` / `--cloud-source <name>` are resolved client-side before the listing request: `/stored-items` has no exact name filter, only a fuzzy `search_query` OR'ed with a substring match on the file name, so `cmdutil.ResolveTable` / `cmdutil.ResolveCloudSource` turn names into IDs first and an unresolvable name exits non-zero instead of listing the whole org. `--table-id` alone stays a single request; `--table-id` combined with `--cloud-source <name>` costs one extra `GET /datasets/<id>` to fetch the table's name for the error messages.
- `get files --cloud-source <name>` requires `--table` or `--table-id` — cloud source names are unique only within a table and `/data-sources` has no name filter at all. `--cloud-source-id` is valid standalone.
- `get files` shows `routing` by default (`pending`, the assigned period key or blank), with `routing_pending`, `routing_period_key` and `contradicted_external_fixes_count` available through `--columns`. `describe file` includes all three fields under `status`, keeping unknown period keys and counts as null.
- `get alerts` / `get warnings` filter by table with `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>` (`cmdutil.AddTableFlags` + `cmdutil.OptionalTableID`); both send `dataset_id`. Their original `--dataset-id` survives as a **hidden** alias of `--table-id` (`MarkHidden`, not `MarkDeprecated` — no per-invocation stderr notice on a scripted filter). Passing `--dataset-id` alongside either selector exits 2 via `rejectDatasetIDWithTableSelector` (defined in `get/alerts.go`, shared by both).
- `get warnings --order-by impact_score` sorts highest impact first; `--reverse` reverses it. The default sort remains `id`. Its default table includes `impact_score` (`IMPACT-SCORE`); `whodunit` stays optional through `--columns`, as the full user object. Column availability is derived from `warnings.WarningTiny`, including both fields.
- `get latest-alert` accepts the table as a positional argument (ID or name) or via `--table <name>` / `--table-id <id>`, through `cmdutil.ResolveTableID` — there is no org-wide form, because `menu-stats` leaves `latest_alert_item_id` nil in its org-wide branch for every non-external role. `--watch` polls `menu-stats` every `--watch-interval` seconds (default 3) and compares each response to the previous response **from that endpoint**; it never compares against `get alerts`, whose server-side filters differ. `--exit-on-change` returns on the first change. `--timeout` (`cmdutil.AddWatchTimeoutFlag`: no limit by default; exit 2 without `--watch`) bounds the whole watch, which runs under `cmdutil.StartWait`. Without `--exit-on-change` it is a `cmdutil.WatchStream` wait: the baseline snapshot is printed at watch start, and expiry exits 0 after a `timed out after …; resume with: …` stderr line. With it the wait is `cmdutil.WaitUntilDone`: a quiet expiry exits 124 with empty stdout and the error `timed out after <limit>: no change to the latest alert on table <t>`, then the resume line (`runWatchLoop`'s error through `Waiter.Ended`). SIGINT and SIGTERM exit 130 and 143.
- `get alerts --file-id <id>` sends `stored_item_id` and always `is_row_level=true` (`applyAlertFileFilter` in `get/alerts.go`): atlas ANDs `is_row_level IS TRUE` into its file filter while defaulting `is_row_level` to false, so leaving it out would match nothing, and `--is-row-level=false` / `--is-muted=false` with it exits 2. Counts are then exact for the file; the table view prints one stderr line saying so (`printAlertFileScope`).
- `get alerts --is-row-level` filters by the API's `is_row_level`. The original `--is-muted` survives as a **hidden** alias (`MarkHidden`, matching `--dataset-id`); passing both exits 2 via `rejectIsMutedWithIsRowLevel`.
- `get alerts --issue-type <type>` filters server-side by the alert's issue type (the wire's `issue_type`, and the column `get alerts` prints). It is deliberately **not** validated client-side: `api.AlertType` had 140 members as of 2026-08-28 and a generated enum that lags a spec pull must never reject a type the backend has just started sending, so the value is forwarded as typed and a bad one comes back as the server's 422 (exit 2). Tab completion supplies the names from `api.IssueTypeAlertActions` (`completeAlertIssueType` in `get/alerts.go`), whose key set `TestIssueTypeKeysCoverEveryAlertType` pins to the enum. `get warnings --issue-type` follows the same policy, with completion from `api.IssueTypeWarningActions` (`completeWarningIssueType` in `get/warnings.go`).
- `get alerts --exclude-issue-type <type>` (repeatable, sends `exclude_issue_types`) follows the same unvalidated policy and completion as `--issue-type`; excluding the type `--issue-type` selects exits 2 (`parseExcludeIssueTypes`). `get alerts --rule <table-rule>` sends `dataset_rule_id`: a full UUID or `r-*` alias is used without a table, an instance name or short ID needs `--table`/`--table-id`/`--dataset-id` (exit 2 before Bootstrap otherwise) and resolves through `cmdutil.FindTableRule` on that table's rules (`parseAlertRuleFlag`, `resolveAlertRule` in `get/alerts_rule.go`). The server lifts its `is_row_level` and `resolve_after_migration` defaults under `dataset_rule_id`; qctl does not re-impose them. `get warnings` has neither flag — the warning list takes neither parameter.
- Every `get` list command's empty-result message names the filters that produced it, threaded from the response's row count (`total *int` — `resp.TotalRows`, or `nil` where the caller passes none, e.g. `get rule-reviews`, `get value-recommendations`). Same call at all 19 sites, but Task 5 changed every site's signature to pass its total: `printEmptyResult` / `printEmptyResultWithHint` / `activeFilters` / `pastLastPage` in `internal/commands/get/context_banner.go`.
  - With nothing set it is unchanged (`No rules found in org "X" (context: "y").`); with filters set it becomes `No rules matched --exclude-builtin, --search "foo" in org "X" (context: "y").` `activeFilters` walks `cmd.Flags().Visit()` minus the `nonFilterFlags` denylist (root globals, pagination, sorting, the `--watch`/`--timeout` streaming flags, and the `--context`/`--all` scope flags), so a **new filter flag needs no change; a new non-filter flag needs a denylist entry**.
  - Positional scope renders first, as a bare `%q` value, gated on `cmd.Args != nil` — only `get table-rules` and `get rule-suggestions` declare one (`qctl get table-rules orders --search "foo"` → `No table rules matched "orders", --search "foo" in org "X" (context: "y").`); every other list command lets cobra's `legacyArgs` discard a positional silently, and echoing one there would name a token the server never saw.
  - When the server's `total_rows` is positive but the page came back empty, pagination — not the filters — emptied it: the first line adds `on page N` (`No rules on page 99 in org "X" (context: "y").`, or with filters, `No rules matched --search "foo" on page 99 in org "X" (context: "y").`), and the second line names the last page (`The last page is 2 (60 rules, 50 per page).`, or with filters, `--search "foo" matches 3 rules — the last page is 1.`).
- `get rules` goes further when `--search` empties the list: it re-runs the same listing minus `--search` (every other filter kept) to print the catalog count and a `Did you mean:` line ranked by `internal/pkg/fuzzy` — token rotation first, so `field_required` finds `required_field`. The second request runs in every output format, its hint always on stderr, but is skipped behind an invalid `--columns`, skipped when the page branch above fires (a page past the end was not caused by the search, so re-fetching without it would answer a question nobody asked), and non-fatal: a failure just omits the hint.
- Every `get` command with a `--search` flag (13 of them: `alerts`, `cloud-sources`, `destinations`, `error-incidents`, `history`, `orgs`, `profiling-jobs`, `rules`, `rule-revisions`, `tables`, `table-kinds`, `table-rules`, `warnings`) rejects a query shorter than 3 characters before the request, exit 2 — `parseSearchFlag` in `internal/commands/get/search.go`, the single place the flag is read. The backend drops a shorter `search_query` and answers with the **unfiltered** set (atlas `capi/capi/views/pagination.py` `get_search_filters`), recording `applied=False` in a field no response schema carries, so qctl cannot detect the drop afterwards and enforces the floor client-side. The count is in runes, matching the server's Python `len()`; the value is not trimmed; an absent flag and `--search ""` are unchanged (neither sends a filter, so neither can misreport one). Commands that resolve a table validate the search **first**, so a rejected search costs no name lookup — which is why `get table-rules` now parses its params before `cmdutil.ResolveTableID`. `TestEverySearchCommandRejectsShortSearch` walks the real tree of `get` subcommands, so a new `get` subcommand with `--search` is covered the day it is added.
- `get table <id-or-name> -o yaml|json` emits the apply-compatible `kind: Table` manifest (`datasets.APIResponseToManifest`), not the raw backend response; `-o table`/`-o name` still go through `output.NewPrinterFromCmd`. This mirrors `get rule-review-policy`: both have exactly one switch arm covering both `yaml` and `json` (`runGetDataset` in `internal/commands/get/dataset.go`, `runGetRuleReviewPolicy` in `internal/commands/get/rule_review_policy.go` — the two case values are listed in opposite order between the files, the shape is what matches).
- `get cloud-source <id> -o yaml|json` emits the apply-compatible `kind: CloudSource` manifest (`cloud_sources.APIResponseToManifest`), secrets left out; `-o table`/`-o name` print the full response through `output.NewPrinterFromCmd` with the list's default columns. One read, `GET /data-sources/{id}`, for every format, so a deleted source is a 404 whatever `-o` says. The argument is an ID only, like every other single-source verb: a name is unique only within a table.
- `get table-config <table>` is a second projection of the same `GET /datasets/{id}` response: the settings model, backup settings, migration policy, anomaly thresholds and retry limits. `--show-not-anomalies` adds the potentially large, read-only `not_anomalies_per_column` ledger to text, JSON or YAML output; it is omitted by default and remains absent from Table manifests. There is no `apply table-config`; the settings model round-trips through `spec.settings_model` on the Table manifest via the same converter, `settingsSchemaToSettingsModel` (`internal/datasets/manifest.go`), that `TableConfigFromAPIResponse` (`internal/datasets/config.go`) calls too.
- `get rule-ignores` requires `--rule` or `--row-id` (the route answers 400 with neither) and lists ONE cloud source's ACTIVE ignores. The source comes from `--cloud-source`/`--cloud-source-id`, or is auto-detected when the table has exactly one, which is acceptable for a read. The ignore-rule writes refuse to guess. Rule and row together are ANDed client-side, because atlas checks `row_id` first and silently drops `dataset_rule_id`. Rows are sorted by row id then rule position, because the route's select has no ORDER BY.
- `get deleted-rows` lists one page of `GET /datasets/{id}/deleted-rows`, newest delete first, one record per row per delete. Every filter is optional and none is auto-detected: no cloud-source flag means every source. `--cloud-source-id`, `--file-id` and `--delete-id` go to the route as given; only a `--cloud-source` NAME costs a lookup, through `cmdutil.ResolveCloudSource`. `--page`/`--limit` go through `paginationFlags`, and `--limit` above 100 (the route's ceiling) is refused at exit 2 before any request. `rows delete-quarantined` prints a `qctl get deleted-rows` line in its follow-up block after a delete that removed rows, under `-o table` only and never for a `--shared` table (the listing has no third-party route), narrowed with `--cloud-source-id` at cloud-source scope and not at all at file scope, because the file route deletes matching quarantine rows of every source (atlas-9po84.5) and a `--file-id` listing would hide some of them; the delete response carries no delete id, so the line cannot name `--delete-id`.
- `get dropped-lines <file-id>` lists one page of `GET /stored-items/{id}/dropped-lines`, in line order. The response carries no `next`, so the following-page hint and the List's `next_page` come from `total_rows`. `--limit` above 100 (the route's ceiling) is refused at exit 2 before any request. `describe file` and `describe ingestion-job` print a `Next: qctl get dropped-lines <file-id>` line on stderr when `deleted_lines_count` is positive (the job's own count, naming its `stored_item_id`).
- `get data` renders a true `sent_or_changed_by_me` entry as `cannot-approve=self` in Row State; `get data-reviews` maps the same positional sidecar before sorting into its `cannot_approve` table column (`self` or `-`). Both reject a misaligned list. This marks self-exclusion only; approval permission and reviewer designation remain separate checks. Raw data and review-overlay structured outputs keep their existing contracts.

- `get table-schema`, `describe table-schema`, `get table-config`, `get dry-run-jobs`, `get latest-alert`, `get table-rules`, `get rule-suggestions`, `get standard-field-suggestions`, `get table-kind-suggestions`, `get value-recommendations`, `get rule-ignores`, `get deleted-rows`, `get data` and `describe table` — these fourteen table-scoped read commands — take the table as a positional argument (ID first, then name) or via `--table`/`--table-id`, through `cmdutil.ResolveTableID`. Which commands are allowed which shape is pinned by `tableScopedCommands` in `internal/catalog/table_flags_test.go`; commands whose positional slot holds a rule name or a suggestion UUID stay flags-only via `cmdutil.ResolveTableIDFromFlags`. The pair is **not** universal: `get table`, `download data`, `undelete table` and `share table` are recorded as `selectorPositionalOnly` — the table is the resource itself, addressed by an `<id-or-name>` positional (through `datasets.Client.ResolveID`, except `undelete table`, which resolves a name through the `states=deleted` list and refuses when two rows match), so a table literally named `42` is unreachable on those four. An ambiguous deleted name reports the observed IDs and requires rerunning `undelete table` with one of them. `ResolveID` deliberately requests one exact-name row rather than paginating: the endpoint's default active/disabled states are the same rows covered by Atlas's org-scoped unique display-name index. That is the remaining numeric-name hole named in `internal/resources/pages/table-schema.md`.
- `get history` is the organization's change ledger — the read-only half of the undo/redo gap. It scopes to a table with `--table`/`--table-id` (`cmdutil.AddTableFlags` + `cmdutil.OptionalTableID`) and filters by `--model`, `--model-id`, `--field`, `--value`, `--operation` (repeatable), `--undo-status`, `--author`/`--mine`, `--since`/`--until` and `--search`. `--operation`, `--undo-status` and `--order-by` are closed vocabularies validated client-side (exit 2) from `internal/history`'s `OperationNames`/`UndoStatusNames`/`OrderByNames`, each pinned to the generated enum by a test in that package; `--model` is deliberately **not** validated, on the `get alerts --issue-type` precedent, and gets tab completion instead. `--author` takes an email or a UUID — an email costs a `GetUsersList` lookup whose 403 is re-reported as exit 2 naming `org:users:list`, because the `editor` and `external` roles can read history without being able to list users. `--mine` resolves through `auth.GetMe` for exactly that reason and never through the users listing. `--reverse` means "oldest first": the endpoint's own `reverse` defaults to true. `-o json`/`-o yaml` re-encode the decoded `api.HistoryListSchema` rather than pass the response bytes through, so a field the generated type lacks is dropped and a null `omitempty` field (the unit tags) is left out; `-o table`/`-o name` go through `history.ToRows`, whose `field`, `change` and `status` columns are synthesized.
- `get notifications` is the per-user, per-org inbox. Read and dismissed are two independent axes: `--unread` narrows the read axis (`unread_only`), `--include-dismissed` widens the dismissed axis (`include_dismissed`), and neither is ever derived from the other. `--type` is repeatable and its vocabulary comes from `notifications.TypeNames()`, built from the `api.NotificationType` constants; `internal/notifications`' `TestTypesCoversTheGeneratedEnum` fails when the spec adds a value nobody added there. Default columns are `id,type,msg,actor,table,read,age`, plus `dismissed` when `--include-dismissed` is set; `count`, `job_id`, `job_type`, `quarantined_rows_count`, `row_id`, `field_name`, `change_proposal_id`, `alert_item_id`, `warning_item_id`, `stored_item_id`, `trigger`, `period_key`, `cloud_source_id`, `series_id` (the last four from `series_escalation`), `thread_id`, `comment_id`, `support_step` and `broker_report_refusal` (a `file_download_failed` row's refusal reason) are reachable through `--columns`; absent context fields are blank, while a zero quarantine count prints `0`. `-o name` prints the ids — `tableui.PrintMessagesFromCmd` hands `name` to `output.NewPrinterFromCmd`, whose renderer looks for a `name` then an `email` field before falling back to `id`, and `notificationRow` deliberately has neither. `-o json`/`-o yaml` are intercepted before the table printer and emit the raw wire object, the only views carrying `review_id`, `review_group_id`, `dataset_rule_id`, `read_at` and `dismissed_at`.
- `get notifications` prints no next-step line for `file_download_failed`: the row names its table and report but carries no `stored_item_id`, so no command for the file can be built from it; its reason is the `broker_report_refusal` column.
- `get notifications` ends its table output with one stderr line per distinct next-step command on the page (`notificationHints`, deduplicated by `printNotificationHints`): `qctl describe change-proposal <change_proposal_id>` for any row naming a proposal, and, by notification type, `qctl get submission-statuses --series <series_id> --period <period_key>` (`--cloud-source-id <data_source_model_id>` when `series_id` is null; `seriesEscalationHint`) plus `qctl describe table <dataset_id>` when the period has a table for `series_escalation`, `qctl describe ingestion-job <job_id>` for `problem_on_our_side`, `qctl describe comment-thread <thread_id> --table-id <dataset_id>` for `comment_reply` (the thread route is dataset-scoped, so a row without a table gets no hint), `qctl describe alert <alert_item_id>` plus `qctl describe file <stored_item_id>` for `support_request` and `file_needs_decision`, `qctl describe duplicate-group <duplicate_group_id> --table-id <dataset_id>` for `duplicate_group_rows_changed` and `duplicate_group_response`, and the comment-thread hint for `duplicate_thread_shared` too. `duplicate_group_decision_changed` names no group and goes to the data source's external senders, whom the reviewer routes refuse, so it gets `qctl get duplicate-groups --sender --table-id <dataset_id>`. JSON/YAML keep the raw response, and name output keeps notification references.
- `get notifications --count` switches to the unread-count endpoint and prints `needs_you` / `total_unread`. That endpoint has no filter parameters — `GetUnreadNotificationCountParams` holds only the cross-cutting `OauthSession` — so `--count` alongside `--type`, `--unread`, `--include-dismissed`, `--page` or `--limit` exits 2 via `rejectCountFilters` — the same "reject loudly rather than answer a different question" rule `get rule-reviews` applies to its mode-specific flags. `--count -o name` also exits 2: there is no id to print, and an empty line at exit 0 would read as "nothing unread". `needs_you` is the backend's actionable subset (`NEEDS_YOU_NOTIFICATION_TYPES` in atlas `common/common/types.py`, mirrored by `notifications.IsNeedsYou`); see `qctl explain notification --gotchas` for the supported types and older-server differences. `change_proposal_submitted` belongs to that subset; `change_proposal_decided` and `change_proposal_execution_failed` land in `total_unread` only. The command prints the server's two numbers as sent; nothing in qctl counts by `IsNeedsYou`. Both numbers are counted over rows that are unread AND undismissed, so dismissing lowers them exactly as reading does.
- `get notifications` has no `--watch`. `get latest-alert --watch` is the local precedent and a watch on the unread count is a reasonable follow-on, deliberately scoped out of the plan that shipped this.
- `get soft-deleted-tables` reads the ops-only, platform-wide `GET /orgs/{org}/ops/soft-deleted-tables` report (resource `soft-deleted-tables`). `--min-age-days` goes through `cmdutil.NonNegativeCountFlag` and is sent only when set, so an explicit 0 reaches the server; `--columns` is validated before `Bootstrap`. The route answers a non-ops caller 404, so a 404 is wrapped with a sentence naming the ops gate (still exit 3). The table flattens the groups in server order; `printSoftDeletedSummary` writes the cutoff, count and total, then one `Size unknown for …` line per group with a `size_error`. An empty report prints one stderr sentence naming the cutoff and no table.
- `get privilege-requests` and `get privilege-audit` are the ops-only read half of the break-glass privilege workflow (resource `privilege-request`; the audit ledger is a verb of that resource, not a second one). Both routes are platform-wide whichever org is in the path, so both hand `printEmptyResultWithHint` a hint saying so after the org-naming empty sentence. `--status` goes through `privilege_requests.ParseStatus` and `--page`/`--limit` through `paginationFlags` before `Bootstrap`; a blank `--target-user` is exit 2 there too (`privilege_requests.CheckTargetUser`). `privilege_requests.Client.ResolveTargetUser` passes a UUID `--target-user` through with no directory request and otherwise resolves it through `FetchUsers` → `Directory.Resolve` (no match or ambiguous = exit 2, before the listing request). The listing's person cells come from the embedded emails, falling back to the UUID; `decided` stays blank while Pending. Both print `More <requests|audit entries> — fetch the next page with --page N+1.` on stderr when `total > page*limit`, from the page and limit the server echoed. `get privilege-requests -o name` prints `privilege-request/<id>`; `get privilege-audit` refuses `-o name`, since an audit entry's id is no request's.
- `get change-proposals` validates everything but the table name before `Bootstrap` (exit 2, zero requests): `--state` through `change_proposals.ParseStates` (case-insensitive, repeatable), `--operation` through `ParseOperation` (only the proposable `alert_resolve`/`warning_resolve`/`dataset_rule_patch`), `--page`/`--limit` through `paginationFlags` with `--limit` capped at `change_proposals.MaxLimit` (200), and `--reviewable` with a `--state` set that leaves out `pending_review`. Without `--state` it sends the live states (`change_proposals.LiveStates`), except under `--reviewable`, which sends no state because the server pins `pending_review`. `--mine` sends `authored_by_me`, `--reviewable` `reviewable_by_me`. The listing carries no total, so a full page prints `Showing N change proposals, a full page; more may exist: use --page N+1.` on stderr, and the empty-result hint names the live-state default and a past-the-end page. Only the table format sweeps the users directory (`cmdutil.FetchDisplayDirectory`, which warns and falls back to UUIDs); `-o json|yaml` is the raw `{items}` and `-o name` prints `change-proposal/<uuid>`. `depends_on` (the `depends_on_id`, `-` for none) is a `--columns` column, not a default one. TARGET is `alert/<id>`, `warning/<id>` or, for a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal, `rule/<uuid>` (`targetLabel`).
- `get privilege-audit` names people through ONE users-directory sweep per invocation (`cmdutil.FetchDisplayDirectory`, shared with the two data-review listings and `describe data-review`): a sweep a name-typed `--target-user` already made is reused, `-o json|yaml` skip it, and a failed sweep warns once on stderr and renders UUIDs at exit 0. `-o json` is the wire `PrivilegeAuditListSchema` verbatim and the stable compliance contract; the table columns are presentation.
- `get email-senders` is the read half of the `email-sender` resource. `--cloud-source-id` is required and declared with `cmdutil.RefInt` (kind `cloud-source`), so `cloud-source/<id>` works; `cmdutil.PositiveIDFlag` checks it before `Bootstrap`. One `GET .../data-sources/{id}/email-intake/senders`, printed in server order. The default columns are email, first_name, last_name, delivery and since (`timeutil.FormatRelativePtr` of `email_delivery_status.since`, empty when it is null); user_id and reason are available through `--columns`, and `emailSenderRow` declares them (`cmdutil.DeclareColumns`).

## cache commands

| Command | File | Store | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cache aliases list` | `internal/commands/cache/list.go` | `aliasstore` | `list_test.go` |
| `cache aliases resolve <alias>` | `internal/commands/cache/resolve.go` | `aliasstore` | `resolve_test.go` |
| `cache aliases clear [--all-servers]` | `internal/commands/cache/clear.go` | `aliasstore` | `clear_test.go` |
| `cache releases list` | `internal/commands/cache/releases.go` | `releases` | `releases_test.go` |
| `cache releases gc [--dry-run]` | `internal/commands/cache/releases_gc.go` | `releases` (`Layout.GC`) | `releases_gc_unix_test.go`; retention matrix in `internal/releases/gc_unix_test.go` |
| `cache releases pin <version>` | `internal/commands/cache/releases_pin.go` | `releases` | `releases_test.go` |
| `cache releases unpin <version>` | `internal/commands/cache/releases_pin.go` | `releases` | `releases_test.go` |

Notes:
- Persistent UUID aliases (`r-*` for table rules) live in `~/.qctl/cache/aliases.db` (bbolt), scoped per normalized server URL.
- `r-*` aliases are accepted transparently in `get table-rule` / `describe table-rule` and bypass `--table`/`--table-id` (see `cmdutil.ResolveTableRuleAlias`).
- Integer IDs (e.g. file `stored_item_id`) are never aliased.
- `cache releases` manages the qctl releases the launcher (`internal/launcher`) builds under `~/.qctl/versions` (`releases.DefaultLayout`); every verb is local and contacts no server. "Required by a context" is `Index.ContextsRequiring`: the saved contexts' servers, keyed by `config.NormalizeEndpointKey`, looked up in the index's last observations.
- `cache releases gc` (`Layout.GC`) decides about every `<version>/` directory and index entry whose name `releases.ParseVersion` accepts, and every `.staging/<version>-*` directory. A release is kept while a context requires it, it is pinned, its build lock (`<tag>.lock`) or in-use lock (`<tag>.inuse`) is held, or its later of last use and install (directory mtime when the index has neither) is within `releases.RecentUse`; staging is kept while younger than `releases.StaleStaging` or while its tag's build lock is held. A release is removed holding both locks exclusively (`filelock.TryLock`), inside one `UpdateIndex` that re-decides on the fresh index, deletes the directory and drops the `Installed` entry. `filelock.ErrUnsupported` keeps the entry. `--dry-run` takes and releases the same locks and removes nothing.

| `describe privilege-request <id>` | `internal/commands/describe/privilege_request.go` | `privilege_requests.NewClient` | **agent text payload** (`privilege_requests.RenderDetail`) | `privilege_request_test.go` |
| `describe change-proposal <id>` | `internal/commands/describe/change_proposal.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (+ `alerts`/`warnings` for the target issue, `dataset_rules` (`GetDatasetRuleDetailContext`) for a target rule, `comments.FetchUsers` for names) | **agent text payload** (`renderProposal`) | `change_proposal_test.go` |
| `describe rule <name>` | `internal/commands/describe/rule.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | **plain text** | `rule_test.go` |

| `describe rule-suggestion <id>` | `internal/commands/describe/rule_suggestion.go` | `rule_suggestions.NewClient` (+ `dataset_rules` for the staged diff, `datasets` for a held candidate's period lock) | **agent text payload** | `rule_suggestion_test.go` |
| `describe row <row-id>` | `internal/commands/describe/row.go` | `datasets.NewClient` (+ `datasets.Client.GetRowRuleEvidence`) | **agent text payload** (`datasets.RenderRowEvidence`) | `row_test.go` |
| `describe table-rule <name>` | `internal/commands/describe/table_rule.go` | `dataset_rules.NewClient` | yaml | `table_rule_test.go` |
