| `describe comment-thread <id>` | `internal/commands/describe/comment_thread.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` | **agent text payload** (`comment_threads.RenderThread`) | `comment_thread_test.go` |
| `describe duplicate-group <group-id>` | `internal/commands/describe/duplicate_group.go` | `duplicate_groups.Client.Get`, or `GetSource` for `--sender` (+ `cmdutil.ResolveTableIDFromFlags`) | **agent text payload** (`duplicate_groups.RenderDetail`: UNAPPLIED PERIODS with table, outcome and reason; DECISION, PENDING DECISION and CARRIED DECISION sections, YOU with one line per `caller.can_*` ability and its refusal (`refusalText`), NEXT ACTIONS from `NextActions`; `--sender`: `RenderSourceDetail`, NEXT ACTIONS from `SourceNextActions`); raw detail (JSON/YAML) | `duplicate_group_test.go`, `internal/duplicate_groups/render_test.go`, `internal/duplicate_groups/source_render_test.go` |
| `describe table-series <name-or-id> [--period <key>]` | `internal/commands/describe/table_series.go` | `table_series.NewClient` | **plain text** header + member grid + Deadlines (`table_series.RenderDeadlines`); JSON/YAML: API record plus `deadline_schedule`; bare reference (name) | `../root/table_series_test.go`, `../root/table_series_describe_test.go` |
| `describe deliverable <ref>` | `internal/commands/describe/deliverable.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` | **plain text** (`deliverables.FormatDetail`); `{deliverable, revisions, latest_run}` (JSON/YAML) | `../root/deliverables_test.go` |
| `describe deliverable-revision <ref>` | `internal/commands/describe/deliverable_revision.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` | **plain text** sorted binding tree and template inventory (`deliverables.FormatRevision`), or template grid under `--sheet`; original API record (JSON/YAML) | `../root/deliverable_revisions_test.go` |
| `describe deliverable-run <id>` | `internal/commands/describe/deliverable_run.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `DescribeRun`) | **plain text** run fields, totals, sources named from the revision tree and readiness summary (`deliverables.FormatRun`); `{run, readiness}` (JSON/YAML) | `../root/deliverable_runs_test.go` |

Notes:
- All describe commands default to yaml except `describe row` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response), `describe rule` (**plain text**), `describe rule-review` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response), `describe rule-suggestion` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response), `describe table` (**plain text** summary; `-o json|yaml` emits the structured summary, not a manifest — the manifest moved to `get table -o yaml|json`), `describe cloud-source` (**plain text** summary; `-o json|yaml` emits the structured summary — the manifest moved to `get cloud-source -o yaml|json`), `describe table-schema` (**plain text** header+grid), `describe table-kind` and `describe standard-field` (**plain text**), `describe table-series` (**plain text** header + member grid + Deadlines; `-o json|yaml` emits the API record plus `deadline_schedule`, null for a failed schedule read, which prints the view and then returns the error, and `-o name` prints its bare reference without reading the schedule), `describe deliverable` (**plain text**; `-o json|yaml` emits `{deliverable, revisions, latest_run}`), `describe deliverable-revision` (**plain text** binding tree and template inventory, or template grid under `--sheet`; `-o json|yaml` emits the original API record), `describe deliverable-run` (**plain text**; `-o json|yaml` emits `{run, readiness}`, readiness null for a cancelled run or a failed readiness read, which prints the view and then returns the error), `describe comment-thread` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response), `describe data-review` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw review response only, `-o name` exits 2), `describe privilege-request` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response), and `describe change-proposal` (**agent text payload**; `-o json|yaml` emits the raw API response, `-o name` exits 2).
- `describe row <row-id>` requires `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>` — the route is dataset-scoped. It reads `GET .../datasets/{id}/rows/{row_id}/rule-evidence`, which takes no query parameters and resolves the id against the clean rows first and the quarantined rows second, so one id reaches either side of the table and a deleted row is a 404. The payload is one block per rule run (newest first) with the manifest it executed and one line per configured rule; default lists only the entries that did something to the row and counts the rest, `-v` lists every entry. Rules are named by the same `r-*` aliases `get data --include-dataset-rule-ids` allocates — the same `aliasstore` bucket, so an alias means one rule across both commands — and issue tokens by the same `[A<id>]`/`[W<id>]` markers, through `dataview.ParseIssueToken`/`RenderIssueRefs` rather than a second reader. Revisions print as their 8-hex short ID and are seeded into the short-ID cache **through the alias-store handle the render already holds**: `rule_versions.RememberRevisionShortIDs` opens the store itself, and a second read-write open blocks on the exclusive flock for the full 5s `aliasstore.openTimeout` and then fails.
- `describe table-rule` requires `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>` (unless the argument is an `r-*` alias).
- `describe privilege-request <id>` parses the id as a full UUID before `Bootstrap` (exit 2, zero requests) and makes one request; an unknown id is the route's 404 → exit 3. The payload labels people from the detail's embedded users (UUID when one is null), including each audit row's `performed_by`, so it needs no directory request. A rejected request's approver prints as `Rejected by:`. NEXT ACTIONS appears for a Pending request only, and it never computes or shows an expiry.
- `describe change-proposal <id>` parses the id as a UUID before `Bootstrap` (exit 2, zero requests). `-o json|yaml` makes one request and emits the raw response: the proposal with `review`, `impact_at_submit` and `impact_diff` (`change_proposals.Detail`; the page's schema is `ChangeProposalDetailSchema`). The text payload adds a read of the target alert, warning or rule (a 404 prints it `gone`; any other failure warns once and prints `unknown`; a rule prints as `rule/<uuid> (<instance name>)` with its state) and a users-directory sweep for names, and prints a `Note:` line when a live, non-executing proposal's target issue is resolved or gone, or its target rule is `deleted` or gone, since the server obsoletes it only on the next write step. `Depends on:` is `change_proposals.DependsOnLine`. PAYLOAD is the stored payload strictly decoded into the `AlertResolution`/`WarningResolution` document it maps onto, or the raw body when it does not; a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal's is its `params` (`change_proposals.RuleParams`; the raw body when the payload carries any other key). REVIEW is `change_proposals.WriteReview`: the GET's `review` (validity, reasons, blockers, the bound impact's keys, linked files and same-issue sweep, `impact_digest`) and `impact_diff` against `impact_at_submit`, or a line saying the server reviews only draft, pending_review and changes_requested proposals; it adds `Resource keys:` (`review.resource_keys`) and, under a `dependency_failed` reason or a `dependency_not_executed` blocker, the step that clears it. A `dataset_rule_patch` proposal's impact goes through `writeRuleImpact` (`rule_render.go`) instead: the params change from the rule's live params, the base params, the series rule approval with `approve_reapproves_series_rules`' prediction, and the validation dry run with its counts, rates and rule errors. OVERLAPS is `change_proposals.WriteOverlaps`: the GET's `overlaps` (null without a review, `[]` with none), one line per proposal with its target and shared keys, and a `--depends-on` edit hint when `edit` is allowed. NEXT ACTIONS prints one `qctl change-proposal <verb>` line per server-sent `allowed_actions` entry, spelled by `change_proposals.NextCommand` (`edit` on a rule-param proposal spells `--set-param`, and `rerun_dry_run` is `change-proposal rerun-dry-run`). EXECUTION renders `response`, `error`, `partial`, `settled_after_timeout` and `target_state` as lines and any other key as YAML, except that a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal's outcome keys (`change_proposals.RuleOutcomeKeys`) print as `change_proposals.RuleOutcome` lines and its `target_state` through `RuleTargetState`.
- `describe rule` flags: `--release`, `--show-code`.
- `describe table-rule` verbosity: default=the round-trip manifest, `-vv`=raw dump. `-v` adds nothing; it once added `status.dataset_field_names`, which the API dropped.
- `describe table-schema` verbosity: default=header+grid, `-v`=adds per-field diffs/definitions/technical IDs (and notable values in `-o json|yaml`), `-vv`=raw API dump.
- `describe table-schema` recognizes the server's HTTP 404 with `title: Processing` and `severity: info` as a status, exits 0, and prints `Status: Processing` in text or `{dataset_id, state: processing, message}` under JSON/YAML (also under `-vv`). Other schema lookup failures keep their exit codes.
- `describe rule-suggestion` requires `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`; default output is an agent text payload with the staged values diffed against the current rule and a status-aware next-actions footer; `row_filter` appears in that diff only when the staged filter differs from the live rule's, live and staged as text blocks, and one-sided when there is no live rule; only a pending, non-candidate create or update is labelled with what accepting does, and a delete suggestion with any staged value shown carries a note that accepting it applies none of them (`stagedRowFilterBlock`, `acceptWritesFilter`, `internal/rule_suggestions/render.go`); `-o json|yaml` emits the raw object.
- `describe table` is a summary, not a manifest: identity/state, destination, loading process, row counts (with the quarantine's validation split, hold counts and not-loaded duplicate copies from `count-by-status` when it has rows), every column with the rules bound to it (a rule-produced column marks its producer `(produces)`, from the schema's `generated_by` stamp), schema draft state, pending suggestions, the newest ingestion and profiling job, open alert count, and a BLOCKING list. At least eight requests across four backend permissions, assembled by `fetchTableSummary` (`internal/commands/describe/table_summary.go`); pending suggestions are paginated and update/delete details are read when a draft exists, with the blocking count derived from `alerts.BuildSuggestionGate`; a section whose fetch fails renders `(unavailable: ...)` and warns on stderr rather than failing the command. `-o json|yaml` emit `datasets.TableSummary`. The manifest moved to `get table -o yaml|json`, and the settings model to `get table-config`.
- `describe cloud-source` is a summary, not a manifest: type, table, for a series source its table series id and routing (the mode, plus the pattern for `file_name` or the column for `column`), state, schedule, last run, clean and quarantined row counts, unresolved alerts, the applied delta cursor when there is one, per-period binding cursors for a series source, and the names — never the values — of the overrides that are set. Two required requests, both fatal: `GET /data-sources/{id}` for identity, configuration and overrides, first so a deleted source keeps its 404, then `GET /data-sources/{id}/tiny` for `last_sync`, `clean_rows_count` and `unresolved_alerts`, which the detail schema lacks. For an ordinary source with a positive quarantined-row count, a third request reads `GET /datasets/{table_id}/quarantine/count-by-status?data_source_model_id={id}`: the Rows line gains the validation split, Quarantine shows the hold split, a `Not loaded:` line shows a `not_loaded` above zero, and JSON/YAML add `quarantine_by_validation`, `quarantine_by_hold` and `quarantine_not_loaded`. This read warns and omits all three on failure. Series sources and zero/unknown quarantined counts skip it. The hold projection and formatter are shared with `describe table`. The override names come from `cloud_sources.SetOverrides`, which reads the raw response body and keeps every non-null key ending in `_override`, suffix removed, so a regen that adds one needs no edit. A portal (`qluster_ui`) source in a table series also gets an `Email intake:` line (`EmailIntake.state` and `issuesReport` in `internal/cloud_sources/summary.go`: `on — <address>`, `off (keeps <address>)` or `off`, then whether issues reports are sent automatically), a trailing `qctl get email-senders --cloud-source-id <id>` hint, and `email_intake {enabled, address, auto_send_issues_report}` in the JSON/YAML summary; `NewSummary` reads the three fields from the same `GET /data-sources/{id}`, so the intake costs no request of its own.
- `describe dry-run-job` verbosity: default=state, rules, timestamps, plus `status.error_message` and `status.rule_load_errors` when present (`dry_runs.setFailureDetail`); `-v`=adds sampling, metrics, `status.structured_summary` and each load error's `stack_trace`; `-vvv`=the raw API response under `raw_response`. `structured_summary` is the wire object decoded and re-encoded (`dry_runs.APIResponseToManifest`), never re-typed, so a key the server omitted stays omitted — a current server omits `rate_valid` and `not_estimable_reason` on every valid stat, and a 2.118.0 server omits `mode` on both summary shapes, which newer servers set. Nothing in qctl reads `rate`, `delta_rate`, `is_significant` or the validity keys, and no view formats them: `-o table` embeds `status`, summary and `error_message` included, in its JSON cell, then prints the full Summary and Error Message sections. Pinned by `TestDescribeDryRunJob_StructuredSummaryIsTheWireObject`; the rules for reading those keys are GOTCHAS in `internal/resources/pages/dry-run-job.md`.

## review commands (rule review workflow)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `review request <rule>` | `internal/commands/review/request.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (+ `rule_versions`, `datasets`, `dry_runs` for `--dry-run-job`) | `request_test.go` |
| `review close <id>` | `internal/commands/review/close.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `lifecycle_test.go` |
| `review reopen <id>` | `internal/commands/review/reopen.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `lifecycle_test.go` |
| `review assign <id>` | `internal/commands/review/assign.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `review unassign <id>` | `internal/commands/review/unassign.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `review re-request <id>` | `internal/commands/review/re_request.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `review audit <id>` | `internal/commands/review/audit.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `audit_test.go` |
| `review link-dry-run <id> <job>` | `internal/commands/review/link_dry_run.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (+ `dry_runs` pre-validation) | `link_dry_run_test.go` |
| `review comment <id>` | `internal/commands/review/comment.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (+ `dry_runs` for `--dry-run-job`) | `comment_test.go` |
| `review edit-comment <id> <comment>` | `internal/commands/review/edit_comment.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `comment_test.go` |
| `review delete-comment <id> <comment>` | `internal/commands/review/delete_comment.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `comment_test.go` |
| `review approve <id>` | `internal/commands/review/approve.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (shared runner `decision.go`) | `decision_test.go` |
| `review reject <id>` | `internal/commands/review/reject.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (shared runner `decision.go`) | `decision_test.go` |
| `review request-changes <id>` | `internal/commands/review/request_changes.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` (shared runner `decision.go`) | `decision_test.go` |
| `review withdraw <id>` | `internal/commands/review/withdraw.go` | `rule_reviews.NewClient` | `withdraw_test.go` |

Notes:
- Review/comment ids are always full UUIDs (no short-prefix resolution).
- `review comment` and `review edit-comment` print the review id from `args[0]`, not from the response: the backend's unified `Comment` model carries `thread_id`, not `review_id` (`api.CommentResponseSchema`, aliased as `Comment` in `internal/rule_reviews/client.go`). Output is byte-identical because review ids are always full UUIDs with no short-prefix resolution — the line above is the premise that makes the substitution safe.
- Mutations lead with one stable line, `rule-review/<uuid> <action>`, most of them ending in `(status: <status-from-backend>)`; some add lines after it, such as `Next: qctl describe rule-review <uuid>`, `re-request`'s `Nudged:` line, and a completing `approve`'s manual `qctl enable rule` line.
- Result documents go through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves the result lines to stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`. `review request` prints the created review, or the review `LinkDryRun` answered with when `--dry-run-job` linked one; when the link fails after the create, the created review is still written before the error. `approve`, `reject` and `request-changes` print the server's `{decision, review_completed, review_status}`, `withdraw`, `close`, `reopen`, `assign` and `unassign` the review, and `re-request` the server's `{review_id, rerequested_reviewer_ids, status}`; each of these and `request` prints `rule-review/<uuid>` under `-o name`. `comment` and `edit-comment` print the comment and have no name view. `review audit` declares `table,json,yaml` (an audit entry has no name, so `-o name` printed nothing at exit 0); `delete-comment` and `link-dry-run` declare `table` alone.
- Reads live under `get rule-reviews` (inbox without `--rule`, REVIEW_EDIT; per-rule listing with `--rule`, REVIEW_LIST) and `describe rule-review`.
- `review link-dry-run` pre-validates the job via `dry_runs.GetDryRunJob` (a missing job → clean not-found, exit 3) and warns when the enable gate wants a passing run but the job is not finished; `--comment <uuid>` links to a comment (display-only) instead of the review headline. `review request --dry-run-job <int>` pre-validates then links after create.
- Comment bodies: `--body` inline or `--body-file <path>` (`-` = stdin); mutually exclusive. `--mention <user>` (repeatable) resolves via the users directory and appends a `cc [@Name](mention:<uuid>)` line — bare `@name` in a body does not notify.
- `review comment --dry-run-job <int>` pre-validates the job (like `link-dry-run`) then embeds it in the comment (display-only).
- Decisions (`approve`/`reject`/`request-changes`) share one runner; the backend upserts one decision per reviewer (resubmit = change your decision). `--comment` (≤4000 chars) becomes a thread comment. Requires `REVIEW_APPROVE`, which excludes the `agent` role — a human renders the verdict. The output's `approvals: n/m` segment comes from a best-effort follow-up detail read.
- `review close` is terminal (atlas `close_review_async`; `reopen` takes only a rejected review), so it is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands` (protected-context gate). It has no `--yes` and no prompt of its own.
- `review withdraw` undoes the caller's own standing decision; the resulting status is printed verbatim from the response (may reopen, stay approved, or close as superseded). `REVIEW_REOPEN_BLOCKED` → disable the rule first.
- `review delete-comment` prompts unless `--yes`; leaf comments are removed outright, comments with replies are tombstoned (`[comment deleted]`).
- `describe rule-review` now renders `## DECISIONS (n)` and `## COMMENTS (n top-level, m total)` between POLICY and NEXT ACTIONS, with full-UUID `[c: <uuid>]` tags for reply/edit/delete.

| `get data-reviews` | `internal/commands/get/data_reviews.go` | `datasets.NewClient` + `datasets.Client.GetData`, `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.ByRows` | `data_reviews_test.go` |
| `get data-review-groups` | `internal/commands/get/data_review_groups.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` | `data_reviews_test.go` |
| `get data-review-changes` | `internal/commands/get/data_review_changes.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` | `data_reviews_test.go` |
| `data-review progress` | `internal/commands/data_review/progress.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` | `progress_test.go` |
| `data-review approve [<review-id>...]` | `internal/commands/data_review/approve.go` (runner: `decision.go`) | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Approve` | `decision_test.go` |
| `data-review withdraw [<review-id>...]` | `internal/commands/data_review/withdraw.go` (runner: `decision.go`) | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Withdraw` | `decision_test.go` |
| `data-review accept-cell <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/accept_cell.go` (runner: `cell.go`) | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Get`/`RecordAction` | `cell_test.go` |
| `data-review clear-cell <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/clear_cell.go` (runner: `cell.go`) | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Get`/`RecordAction` | `cell_test.go` |
| `data-review withdraw-cell <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/withdraw_cell.go` (runner: `cell.go`) | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Get`/`RecordAction` | `cell_test.go` |
| `data-review request` | `internal/commands/data_review/request.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.FetchUsers`/`Submit` | `request_test.go` |
| `data-review assign <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/assign.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.FetchUsers`/`Get`/`Assign` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `data-review unassign <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/unassign.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.FetchUsers`/`Unassign` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `data-review re-request <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/re_request.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.FetchUsers`/`Rerequest` | `reviewers_test.go` |
| `data-review close <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/close.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Close` | `close_test.go` |
| `data-review comment <review-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/comment.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.Get`/`FetchUsers`/`AddComment` | `comment_test.go` |
| `data-review-group assign <group-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/group.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.FetchUsers`/`AssignGroup` | `group_test.go` |
| `data-review-group re-request <group-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/group.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.RerequestGroup`/`FetchUsers` | `group_test.go` |
| `data-review-group cancel <group-id>` | `internal/commands/data_review/group.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.CancelGroup` | `group_test.go` |
| `describe data-review <review-id>` | `internal/commands/describe/data_review.go` | `data_reviews.NewClient` + `data_reviews.Client.ListDecisions`/`ByRows`/`FetchUsers`, `auth.Client.GetMe` for the caller, `comment_threads.NewClient` for the thread | `data_review_test.go` |

Notes:
- The four listing reads and `data-review request` require exactly one table selector: `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`. `describe data-review` takes none — the review names its table.
- `get data-reviews` is the reviewer's queue and makes exactly two requests after the usual `--table` name lookup: one `GET /data` page (`is_bad_data=true`, `review_status=<--state>`, `review_group_id` only with `--group`, `order_by=id`, `--page`/`--limit` defaulting to 1/20 like `get data`), then the `by-rows` overlay over that page's row ids — skipped when the page is empty. Rows are joined on `row_id` and rendered in ascending row id; a row the overlay omits (no `open`/`changes_requested` review) shows `-` in every review column and is left out of `-o name`. `requester` (default) renders the overlay's embedded requester (name, then email, then UUID — no directory request), and the opt-in `requests` column comma-joins its `review_group_ids`, the ids `data-review-group` takes. `approvals` renders the overlay's `current_approvals`/`required_approvals` as `1/2`, like `get rule-reviews`, and `-` when either is absent. `undecided` counts every change that is neither `approve` nor blocking (`reject`/`request_changes`), so `comment_only` is undecided, matching the release gate. `-o json|yaml` emit a List whose items are the by-rows overlays, whose `page` and `limit` are the values sent, and whose `total` is the data page's `total_rows`, so it counts the bucket's rows, not reviews. A failure names the endpoint (`data endpoint` / `by-rows endpoint`), and a 401/403 also names the access that endpoint needs; `--state`, `--group`, `--page` and `--limit` are all refused with exit 2 before any request.
- `get data-review-groups` lists submission batches, defaults to open groups, paginates with `--limit`/`--offset`, and offers raw `json|yaml` plus `name` output (`data-review-group/<uuid>`, which `--group` takes). Default table columns include `description` and `reviewers`; reviewers use the embedded submission snapshot's user names, falling back to email then UUID, independently of the requestor's directory lookup.
- `get data-review-changes` ranks one table's submitted cell changes and deliberately defaults to `--state unresolved`, matching the web inbox rather than the backend route's `undecided` default. `--group` scopes both the list and count to a request's snapshot; `--offset` skips ranked live changes after refinement, with `--limit` 1-20. Its count is a stage-1 upper bound, so the stderr footer says "up to" and a full page with potential successors prints a qualified next-offset hint; short and empty pages print no hint. The default `flagged` column prints `yes` for a change whose `has_open_flag` is true and is blank otherwise. JSON/YAML keep the List's `page`, `total` and `next_page` null and skip the count.
- `data-review progress` joins the dataset-data count-by-state read with table-wide sign-off progress. `--group <uuid>` scopes the row counts and adds `RequestStateCounts` from `internal/data_reviews/request_states.go`, rendered as approver (you) and sender counts; signoff-progress accepts no group selector. JSON/YAML keep the existing `count_by_state` and `signoff_progress` keys and add `request_state_counts` only with `--group`.
- `data-review approve`/`withdraw` share one runner (`decision.go`) and one request: a POST whose body carries exactly one selector — positional `<review-id>...` (validated as UUIDs, deduped in order, 1..500) or `--group <uuid>` (every open or changes_requested review in the submission). Both, neither, a malformed id or more than 500 distinct ids exit 2 before any request. An id list never prompts; `--group` goes through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes` skips; non-interactive without `--yes` exits 2; a decline exits 1). `approve` always sends a `comment` key (`null` without `--comment`); `withdraw` registers no `--comment` and never sends the key. Output: one stdout line per processed review (`status`, `approvals: c/r`, `released: n row(s)`), then one per skipped review (`skipped: <reason> [<error_code>]`); a processed `approve` still `changes_requested` adds one stderr warning; a single processed id ends with `Next: qctl describe data-review <id>`. `-o json|yaml` is the raw `{processed, skipped}` (declared `table,json,yaml`; `-o name` exits 2) and skips the stderr lines. Exit rule (`bulkOutcome`): no skips → 0; nothing processed and every skip shares one code → `NOT_FOUND` 3 (with the org scope note), `VIEW_ONLY` and `APPROVER_NOT_DESIGNATED` 4, `REVIEW_NOT_OPEN` 2, anything else 1, with the distinct reasons (at most three) as the message; any other outcome with a skip → 1, `N of M review(s) skipped; the other K processed`. A route-level 403 exits 4 and names `DATA_REVIEW_APPROVE`.
- `data-review accept-cell`/`clear-cell`/`withdraw-cell` share one runner (`cell.go`) over `POST /data-reviews/{id}/actions/{history_id}`, sending `approve`/`comment_only`/`withdraw`. Each takes `<review-id>` plus exactly one of `--field <name>` or `--history-id <int>`; neither, both, an empty `--field`, a non-positive id or a malformed review UUID exits 2 before any request. `--field` costs one `GET /data-reviews/{id}?include=changes` and an exact, case-sensitive match on `field_name` (a field appears at most once per review); no match exits 2 listing the changed fields, and an empty change set exits 2 with a `--history-id` hint, because the backend answers 200 with `changes: []` to a caller without the table's data access. `--history-id` skips the GET. The body always carries `comment` and `suggested_value` as strings (`comment` is `""` without `--comment`, `suggested_value` always `""`), so `accept-cell` and `clear-cell` replace the cell's previous comment and erase any stored suggestion. `withdraw-cell` deletes only the caller's current ledger decision and registers no `--comment`: the backend ignores both strings on withdrawal. It preserves the backend's ownership refusal (403, exit 4) and missing-decision refusal (422, exit 2), and explains the edit permission, table access and approval permission needed over a stored veto. The result line is `data-review/<id> cell <field> [h: <hid>] <label> (status: <own status>, affected: N review(s) / M row(s), released: K row(s))`, the field omitted under `--history-id`, the status omitted with a stderr note when the review is missing from `affected_review_statuses` (closed, or its row has left quarantine), a stderr line when other reviews share the change, then `Next: qctl describe data-review <id>`; under `-o json|yaml` those lines go to stderr and stdout carries the server's `{ledger_id, decision, affected_data_review_ids, affected_review_statuses, affected_row_ids, released_row_ids}`, and `-o name` exits 2. The route's 403s carry no `error_code`, so `mapCellVerdictError` explains them by verb: `accept-cell` names `DATA_REVIEW_APPROVE`, `clear-cell` names `DATA_REVIEW_APPROVE` for a stored `reject` or `request_changes` verdict and `DATA_REVIEW_EDIT` for every clear, and `withdraw-cell` names ownership plus the edit and approval permissions; all three name the table's data access (exit 4). A 403 on the `--field` lookup names `DATA_REVIEW_LIST`. `REVIEW_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH` (422) exits 2 naming the history id; 404 exits 3.
- `data-review request` requires a table selector, `--row-id` (IntSlice: repeatable and comma-separated, 1..500 positive ids; a duplicate is refused, since the backend would fail the whole batch on its unique index) and `--title` (trimmed; blank exits 2); every one of those is checked before any request. `--reviewer` values resolve through one directory sweep, so the budget is at most three requests (table name, users, `POST /data-reviews/batch`), and an unresolvable one exits 2 before the POST. `description` and `assigned_reviewer_ids` go out as `null` when omitted. The response is a bare array in `row_ids` order and names the new group only inside each review's `review_groups`, so the group is the newest entry (by `created_at`) of the first review; a review older than that group is one the backend reused (any non-closed review, `approved` included) and is marked `(re-submitted)` — both timestamps are Python-side defaults and the group is flushed first, so a new review is never older. Output: `data-review-group/<uuid> created "<title>": N review(s)`, one `data-review/<uuid> row <id> (status: <s>, approvals required: <n>)` line per review, then `Next: qctl get data-reviews <the selector as given> --group <uuid>`. `-o json|yaml` print `{group, reviews}` instead (`submission`, its `group` null when the response names none) and `-o name` one `data-review/<uuid>` per review, both through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves those lines to stderr. A response without a group still exits 0, with a stderr pointer to `get data-review-groups`. A 403 exits 4 naming the batch endpoint's permission from generated metadata and access to the table or assigned sources.
- `data-review assign`/`unassign`/`re-request`/`close` take a full review UUID (malformed exits 2 before any request) and name `DATA_REVIEW_EDIT` and table access on a 403 (the backend checks both and the 403 does not say which). `assign` resolves `--reviewer` (required), then reads the review (`GET /data-reviews/{id}`) and drops anyone already in `assigned_reviewers`, printing `already assigned: <names>` on stderr, because the route re-notifies every id it is sent (the web UI filters the same way); when nobody is left it sends nothing and prints `reviewers unchanged`, except that an `approved` or `closed` review exits 2 there, the guard the skipped POST would have applied. `unassign` POSTs straight after the directory; its stderr `retained:` line counts only the `unassigned_vets` entries whose `decided_by_id` is a reviewer this call named, because that list also carries verdicts by anyone else who is neither assigned nor the requester (on every change the row was submitted with, superseded ones included). Both map `REVIEW_NOT_OPEN` (422) to `review is <status>; reviewers can only be changed on an active review` (exit 2); the backend sends no `context` on that code, so the status comes from `context.status` if present, else the status `assign` just read, else "no longer active". `re-request` sends `{}` without `--reviewer`, fetches the directory after the POST only to name who was notified (a failed sweep warns and prints UUIDs), and prints a "nobody" line whose reason depends on whether `--reviewer` was given; `review_not_active` (409, with `context.status`) exits 2 naming the status. `close` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands` (protected-context gate) and confirms through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes` skips; non-interactive without it exits 2; a decline exits 1) with the web UI's four consequences, two reworded (see `closeConsequences`), and adds "(only open or changes_requested reviews can be closed)" to the route's code-less 422, whose own message says "already closed" for an approved review too. All four print `data-review/<uuid> <action> (status: <s>)` then `Next: qctl describe data-review <uuid>`, which move to stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`. `-o json|yaml` print the review for `assign` (the one it read when nobody was left to assign), `unassign` and `close`, and the server's `{data_review_id, rerequested_reviewer_ids, status}` for `re-request`; `-o name` prints `data-review/<uuid>`.
- `data-review comment` wraps the one review-scoped comment route (`POST /data-reviews/{id}/comments`, COMMENT_WRITE). The review UUID and `--reply-to` are validated, `--body`/`--body-file` resolved through `cmdutil.ReadBodyFlags` (`-` = stdin, sent verbatim) and checked by `cmdutil.ValidateCommentBody` (blank or over 10000 characters exits 2), all before any request. The budget is at most three requests: `GET /data-reviews/{id}` only to learn `dataset_id` for the printed recipes, the users directory when `--mention` is given, then the POST. A 404 on the read exits 3 before anything is posted; a 403 on it (the caller lacks DATA_REVIEW_LIST, which commenting does not need) only warns on stderr and the recipes print `--table-id <table-id>`. `--mention` values resolve through `comments.Directory.ResolveAll` and are appended by `Directory.CCLine`, the helper `review comment` and `comment-thread reply` share; an unresolvable one exits 2 before the POST, and the cap is re-checked on the body with the cc line. There is no `--dry-run-job`: the route ignores `linked_dry_run_job_id`. The result lines are `data-review/<uuid> comment added [c: <comment-uuid>] (thread: <thread-uuid>)`, then `Next: qctl comment-thread reply <thread> --table-id <id> --body "..."` and separate `edit-comment ... --body "..."` and `delete-comment` recipes; the delete recipe includes `--yes` without a terminal — listing, editing, deleting and resolving review comments are the dataset-scoped comment-thread routes' job. Under `-o json|yaml` they go to stderr and stdout carries the created comment, whose `thread_id` is what `comment-thread reply` takes; `-o name` exits 2. `COMMENT_DEPTH_EXCEEDED` (422) exits 2 with the depth-3 hint, a 422 identifying the `--reply-to` parent as missing exits 2 saying the parent must be an undeleted comment of this review's thread; mention refusals and other validation errors retain their own diagnostic, a 403 exits 4 naming COMMENT_WRITE and table access, and a 404 exits 3.
- `data-review close` and `data-review-group cancel` map `REVIEW_HAS_STANDING_REJECT` (409) to exit 2, naming `context.data_review_ids` with a `qctl describe data-review <id>` hint per valid review ID and asking each rejecting reviewer to approve the row (`qctl data-review approve <review-id>`) or withdraw their reject (`qctl data-review withdraw <review-id>`) before retrying; a blocked cancellation changes nothing. Only the reviewer who recorded a whole-row reject can clear it.
- `data-review-group assign`/`re-request`/`cancel` act on a whole request (`POST /data-reviews/groups/{id}/assign|re-request|cancel`, all `DATA_REVIEW_EDIT` plus table access, which a 403 names). The positional `<group-id>` must be a full UUID (`data-review-group/<uuid>` is accepted; malformed exits 2 before any request). `assign` resolves `--reviewer` (required) and POSTs with no pre-read, because the route notifies only reviewers new to the request; the response's `assigned_reviewer_ids` is the whole assignee list afterwards, printed as `data-review-group/<uuid> reviewers assigned (now: <names>)`, and a requested reviewer that list lacks is named on stderr (the backend silently drops non-members) at exit 0. `re-request` sends no body and names who was notified, or prints a "nobody" line; both map `REVIEW_NOT_OPEN` (422, the request has no open or changes_requested member) to exit 2. `cancel` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands` (protected-context gate) and confirms through `cmdutil.Confirm` with consequences adapted from the web UI (`cancelConsequences`); it has no status guard, so it is sent even when nothing is open. It prints `cancelled: N review(s) removed, M closed`, then one `data-review/<uuid> closed` or `not closed (in another request, or no longer open)` line per removed id; a repeat answers two empty lists and prints `it had no reviews left, so nothing changed` at exit 0. All three move their result lines to stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`; `-o json|yaml` print the server's response and `-o name` prints `data-review-group/<uuid>`.
- `data-review assign` and `request --reviewer` report requested reviewers missing from each returned review on stderr, naming the review and preserving exit 0. They reuse the resolving directory for names and otherwise print UUIDs without another lookup; JSON/YAML/name stdout is unchanged.
- Table renderers resolve user UUIDs through `data_reviews.FetchUsers`; a directory failure warns once and falls back to raw UUIDs. Raw structured output skips every presentation-only request — the directory on both reads, and the count on `get data-review-changes`, which feeds only the stderr footer.

## comment-thread commands (discussions on table data)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comment-thread create` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/create.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` | `create_test.go` |
| `comment-thread reply <thread>` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/reply.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` (+ `comments.Directory` for `--mention`) | `comment_test.go` |
| `comment-thread edit-comment <thread> <comment>` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/edit_comment.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` | `comment_test.go` |
| `comment-thread delete-comment <thread> <comment>` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/delete_comment.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` | `comment_test.go` |
| `comment-thread resolve <thread>` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/resolve.go` | `comment_threads.NewClient` | `resolve_test.go` |
| `comment-thread unresolve <thread>` | `internal/commands/comment_thread/resolve.go` (both verbs share one file and one runner) | `comment_threads.NewClient` | `resolve_test.go` |

Notes:
- Thread and comment ids are always full UUIDs (no short-prefix resolution); a malformed id is exit 2 before any request.
- A table selector (`--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`) is **required on every verb**, including the get/describe pair — `dataset_id` is in the route path, so there is no org-wide lookup. Registered with `cmdutil.AddTableFlags`, read with `cmdutil.ResolveTableIDFromFlags`.
- With no ids, `get comment-threads` lists one page of readable unresolved threads through `comment_threads.Client.List` (`ListOpenCommentThreads`), most recently updated first, using `--page` (default 1) and `--limit` (default 20, range 1-100). It returns targets and counts without transcripts; external callers see only readable threads and targets on their assigned sources. With ids, it fetches each thread in argument order, including resolved ones and full transcripts; explicit `--page`/`--limit` then exit 2 before requests. JSON/YAML use the standard List envelope, with paging metadata for the listing and null paging for by-id fetches.
- Rule-review threads are unreachable here by design — the backend answers 404 for a thread carrying a `rule_revision_review_id` — and keep the `qctl review comment|edit-comment|delete-comment` verbs. `cmdutil.CommentThreadNotFoundHint` is the shared prose `cmdutil.HintOnNotFound` appends to a 404, naming a missing thread, source visibility and rule-review discussions.
- `create` targets are the cross product of `--row` and `--field`; with no `--field` the rows are targeted whole. The four backend caps (500 rows, 2000 cells, body 1-10000, title ≤500) are mirrored client-side in `create.go` and refused with exit 2.
- `create` is a partial success: `dropped_targets` from the 201 body is printed on stderr at exit 0 by `reportDroppedTargets`.
- Result documents go through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves the result line to stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`. `create` prints the 201 body, `{thread, dropped_targets}`, and `resolve` and `unresolve` the thread, each with `comment-thread/<uuid>` under `-o name`. `reply` prints the created comment, whose `id` is what `--reply-to` and `edit-comment` take, and `edit-comment` the edited one; neither has a name view. `delete-comment` declares `table` alone, so `-o json|yaml|name` exits 2.
- `resolve`/`unresolve` answer with the thread's state and an empty `comments` list, so they print a count line rather than re-rendering the transcript.
- `delete-comment` prompts unless `--yes`, matching `review delete-comment`.
- `internal/comments/` is the shared home of the unified `Comment` model — `errors.go` (`APIError`, `WrapAPIError`, `HandleError`), `render.go` (transcript rendering), `users.go` (the `--mention` directory). Both `internal/comment_threads` and `internal/rule_reviews` re-export from it via type aliases, so `rule_reviews.APIError` and friends still resolve for the eight files under `internal/commands/review/`.

## suggestion commands (staged rule changes)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `suggestion accept <id>...` | `internal/commands/suggestion/accept.go` | `rule_suggestions.NewClient` | `suggestion_test.go` |
| `suggestion dismiss <id>...` | `internal/commands/suggestion/dismiss.go` | `rule_suggestions.NewClient` | `suggestion_test.go` |
| `suggestion edit <id>` | `internal/commands/suggestion/edit.go` | `rule_suggestions.NewClient` | `edit_test.go` |

Notes:
- Suggestions are staged pending changes to a table's rules (backend `DatasetRuleSuggestion`), created when a TableRule patch touches `params`/`state` while the table has a draft migration under review, when a TableRule *instantiate* would touch the schema under the same condition (the whole instantiation is staged, `action: create`), when a TableRule patch changes the rule's configuration — its revision, column mapping or produced fields, plus `params` when the rule is in `error` — and the backend validates it as a **revalidation candidate** (`is_revalidation_candidate: true`, no migration needed), or by profiling/periodic analysis. A candidate is resolved by its own dry run: `suggestion accept` on one is refused with 409, only `dismiss` applies, and `state: failed` is reachable by nothing else.
- All three verbs require `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`, and every id is a full UUID: `parseIDs` (`internal/commands/suggestion/selector.go`) rejects anything `uuid.Parse` will not take, with exit 2 and no request, on the single-id route as well as the bulk one, and `newEditCommand` in `edit.go` calls it directly so all three refuse the same typo identically. Note that is looser than "the canonical hyphenated form" — `uuid.Parse` also accepts the braced, `urn:uuid:` and unhyphenated spellings — the comment on `parseIDs`'s `seen` map names those three, which is why it de-duplicates on the canonical form — and uppercase hex besides, which that comment does not mention. Harmless on the wire: every route re-parses through `parseSuggestionID`, so what qctl sends is canonical whatever the user typed.
- Naming ids prompts for nothing. The `--source`/`--action` route does: it prints what matched and asks, and `--yes` skips the prompt (`confirmFilterWrite`, `filter.go`). `cmdutil.Confirm` (`internal/cmdutil/prompt.go`) owns the two exits there — 2 when stdin cannot answer, 1 when the user declines.
- The filter route runs **no** pre-flight — the set is the server's, so there is no caller id list to read back — but it still predicts `accept`'s one refusal, because `is_revalidation_candidate` rides on the compact list row it already fetched (`splitRevalidationCandidates`, `filter.go`). Candidates stay in the listing, marked, and stay in the write (qctl cannot subtract from a set the server re-selects without switching to an id list); what changes is that the prompt says how many will actually land instead of promising all of them. When *nothing* would land, `approveFilterWrite` (`filter.go`) refuses with exit 2 rather than ask about a write of zero. `dismiss` refuses nothing, so its split is empty by construction.
- `accept.go` holds `runResolve`, the shared flow both resolve verbs run; the pieces live beside it — `selector.go` (route choice, id parsing, the `--source`/`--action` vocabularies, `maxBulkIDs`), `preflight.go` (the per-id read-back), `filter.go` (the preview walk and the confirmation), `result.go` (bulk rendering and the exit code). Their tests are `selector_test.go`, `preflight_test.go`, `filter_test.go` and `result_test.go`.
- `dismiss --family` maps to the backend's `dismiss_family` (never re-suggest any revision of this rule family for this table). It is one flag for the whole invocation: `newDismissCommand` in `internal/commands/suggestion/dismiss.go` passes it to `BulkDismiss` too, so on a multi-id or `--source`/`--action` dismiss it applies to every suggestion in the batch.
- `accept` declares `table,json,yaml`: one id prints the accepted suggestion, whose `created_dataset_rule_id` names the rule a create made; several ids or a selection print `{processed, skipped, errors, missing}` (`bulkOutcome`, `result.go`), printed even when a refusal or a missing id makes the batch exit 1, and an empty selection prints that object zeroed. `edit` prints the edited suggestion, and `rule-suggestion/<uuid>` under `-o name`. Both go through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves the result lines, the filter preview and `Next:` to stderr. `dismiss` runs `accept`'s `runResolve` but declares `table` alone.
- Reads live under `get rule-suggestions` and `describe rule-suggestion`. `suggestion accept`/`dismiss` take one id (the single-item route, unchanged), several ids (a per-id pre-flight, then `bulk-accept`/`bulk-dismiss`), or a `--source`/`--action` selection (a paged preview plus a confirmation, then the same bulk route); ids and filters cannot be combined, because the server ANDs them. The pre-flight exists because `_resolve_pending_suggestions` filters `state == pending` in SQL (`../atlas capi/capi/views/suggestion_view.py`): an unknown or already-resolved id never enters the working set, so it is absent from `processed`, `skipped` and `errors` alike, and only reading each id back first keeps `accept a b` reporting the same typo as `accept a`. A partial result exits 1 naming what landed, as `share table` does (`runShareTable`'s `if len(result.Failed) > 0` branch, `internal/commands/share/table.go`). `suggestion edit` reads the suggestion once before every edit, including `--severity` alone, and refuses a revalidation candidate with exit 2 and `qctl apply -f` advice before sending `PatchSuggestion`; `--set-param`/`--map-column` merge into the suggestion's current values and `--unset-param`/`--unset-column` delete from those merged values because the route assigns each field outright (`patch_suggestion`, `suggestion_view.py`). `--row-filter-file` replaces the whole row filter with a document `row_filters.ReadFile` reads with its description required, and `--remove-row-filter` sends `row_filter: null` (`parseRowFilterFlags`, `edit.go`; `patchBody`, `internal/rule_suggestions/client.go`); naming both exits 2. Its hand-written params decode (`decodeSuggestion`, `client.go`, which the get, accept, edit and dismiss responses all go through) and flag parser both use `json.Number`, so a merge never routes JSON numbers through `float64` and the `accept`/`edit` `-o json` output echoes them exactly; `-o yaml` keeps each value but not always its spelling (`1.20` prints as `1.2`).

## alert commands (act on an alert)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `alert resolve <id>` | `internal/commands/alert/resolve.go` (flag path) + `internal/commands/alert/resolve_file.go` (flag matrix, `-f` runner, `confirmResolution`) + `internal/commands/alert/migration_rules.go` (draft-deletion warning) + `internal/commands/alert/key_flags.go` (`--key-columns`, `--no-key-columns`, the `--dry-run` key-step read) + `internal/commands/resolveexplain/resolveexplain.go` (`Alert`: the failure explainers, shared with `change-proposal approve`) + `internal/commands/resolveexplain/staged.go` (`Staged`: a keyword change staged as a rule suggestion, shared with `warning resolve`) | `alerts.NewClient` (+ `rule_suggestions`, `datasets`, `dataset_rules` for the pre-flight; `rule_suggestions` again to read a staged suggestion) | `alert_test.go`, `resolve_file_test.go`, `result_test.go`, `field_names_resolution_test.go`, `migration_rules_test.go`, `migration_decisions_test.go`, `key_step_test.go` |
| `alert propose <id>` | `internal/commands/alert/propose.go` (flag matrix shared with `resolve` through `resolveInputs`, key flags through `key_flags.go`; the rest in `internal/commands/change_proposal/propose.go`) | `change_proposals.NewClient` (+ `alerts` for the target check) | `internal/commands/change_proposal/change_proposal_test.go`, `internal/commands/change_proposal/propose_file_id_test.go`, `internal/commands/change_proposal/migration_resolution_test.go` |
| `alert submit` | `internal/commands/alert/submit.go` | `alerts.NewClient` | `user_submitted_test.go` |
| `alert retract <alert-id>` | `internal/commands/alert/retract.go` | `alerts.NewClient` | `user_submitted_test.go` |

Notes:
- `alert resolve` validates `--action` against the fetched alert's `spec.actions`. The flag path sends an action name alone; `-f` sends an `AlertResolution` manifest with request fields. The manifest's envelope and `spec` are checked before sending (exit 2 on bad input), and multi-document files run in order with `--fail-fast` support. `apply_migration` runs a pending-suggestion pre-flight and asks for confirmation; `--yes` skips only its prompt. `confirmResolution` in `resolve_file.go` handles this on both paths and maps a declined multi-document entry to a skip. Cross-source actions, including value-mapping, datetime-format and `accept_gtin_prefix`, run without a prompt; `RenderNextActions` in `internal/alerts/nextactions.go` describes their reach. `accept_gtin_prefix` requires a prefix from the alert's `prefix_candidates`, which `describe alert` and the `--dry-run` skeleton show. An offered action needing input appears in NEXT ACTIONS as a `--dry-run` generator command. The alert `--action` path does not reject such actions client-side; the backend may reject a missing field. `resolveexplain.Alert` appends the backend's action note to a failure on either path.
- `alert resolve <id> --action <action> --file-id <id>` sends `stored_item_id` with the action. `resolveInputs` (`resolve_file.go`) refuses it, exit 2 before any request, with `-f` (the manifest's `spec.stored_item_id` instead), with `--dry-run`, and with an action whose `api.AlertActionFields` entry does not accept `stored_item_id` — today only `trash_file` does. An alert still open on the re-read is reported as staying open while it links another kept file, on both paths. `describe alert` lists the files under `status.affected_files` (`affectedFiles` in `internal/alerts/types.go`, each id once). `alert propose` accepts the same `--file-id` and stores it in the proposal payload through `change_proposals.AlertDocument` and `ProposeOptions.ProposeDocument`.
- `alert resolve|propose <id> --action apply_migration --key-columns a,b` sends `key_columns`, and `--no-key-columns` sends `no_key_columns: true`. `resolveInputs` (`resolve_file.go`) refuses, exit 2 before any request, both together (`cmdutil.ValidateConflictingFlags`), an empty `--key-columns`, either with `-f` (the manifest's `spec.key_columns` / `spec.no_key_columns`), with `--dry-run`, or with an action whose `api.AlertActionFields` entry does not accept `key_columns`. Every `apply_migration` — both paths of `resolve` and `propose`, and `change-proposal edit` through `CheckTarget` — then reads the table's migration details once (`alerts.KeyChoicePreflight`, `internal/alerts/key_step.go`) and `AlertResolutionSpec.CheckKeyStep` refuses at exit 2 neither field at a `required` key step (listing `datasets.FormatKeyColumnCandidates`), either field at a null step, and `no_key_columns` at the `optional` step; a failed read warns on stderr and sends. On `resolve` the same read feeds `migrationRuleWarning`, and `confirmMigration` prints `KeyOutcome`. `--dry-run` reads it too, and `alerts.Skeleton` at the `required` step writes a bare `key_columns:`, a commented `no_key_columns: true` and the candidates. `resolveexplain.Alert` explains a 422 at loc `body.key_columns`/`body.no_key_columns` and the key-step `409 ERR_MIGRATION_CONFLICT` (`explainKeyChoice`). `alerts.ApplyMigrationKeyArgs` makes the NEXT ACTIONS, `apply -f` TableSchema and table-rule `--wait` apply_migration lines key-aware; `describe change-proposal` prints `bound.key_columns` (`keyColumnsLine`, `internal/change_proposals/render.go`).
- `alert resolve`'s `resolveexplain.Alert` takes the action that was sent: a 409 `PERIOD_LOCKED` carries apierror's unlock `Hint:`, then an `Instead:` line naming `spec.new_value` for `assign_reporting_period`, or a `Note:` naming the alert's held period otherwise. A 403 `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_REQUIRED` or `CHANGE_REVIEW_POLICY_DENIED` whose `context.operation` is `alert_resolve` gets an `Instead:` line naming `qctl alert propose <id>` or a `Next:` line naming `qctl get change-review-policy --table-id <dataset_id>` (`explainChangeReview`) under apierror's generic `Hint:`; one about another operation (an implied `dataset_rule_patch` key) gets the `Hint:` alone. A 409 with no error code on a compound `validation_add_a_new_keyword__*` action (`refusedKeywordStaging`) gets the two steps: the plain keyword action with the same `spec.new_value`, then the compound action again (`explainKeywordStagingRefusal`); `Warning` does the same. A 200 whose `dataset_rule_suggestion_id` is set is reported by `resolveexplain.Staged` and `StagedKeyword.Report`: it reads the suggestion to choose `qctl suggestion accept` (draft-migration suggestion) or `qctl describe rule-suggestion` (revalidation candidate), falls back to the describe line when that read fails, and the result document carries `dataset_rule_suggestion_id`. `describe alert` shows a `series_file_routing` alert's `spec.body.reason` and `spec.body.period_key` at every verbosity (`defaultTierBody` in `internal/alerts/types.go`); `get alerts` cannot, because `AlertTinySchema` carries no body.

- `alert resolve -f` and `alert propose -f` reject a non-empty `spec.key_columns` or `spec.no_key_columns: true` on a column mapping locally at exit 2: `validateFieldNames` lists only `field_names_resolution`, so `actionfields.Validate` refuses any other field. A code-less `apply_migration` 409 carrying the not-pending-suggestion message gets manifest-editing guidance through `resolveexplain.Alert`, including on `change-proposal approve`; unrelated conflicts keep their own explanation. Membership stays server-validated because the suggestion preview omits periodic rows. `alert propose -f` carries accept/dismiss IDs in the payload for approval without independent suggestion writes.
- `alert resolve <id> --dry-run` without `--action` generates a column-mapping template for an open `initial_fields` or `unexpected_field_name` alert awaiting a decision. `alert resolve -f` accepts its `spec.field_names_resolution` without `spec.action`; `internal/alerts/field_names_resolution.go` validates the nested request shape and the fetched alert, then builds the template. The normal dependency check and outcome re-read still run on submission. Structured results have `action: ""` for this path.
- `alert resolve` can resolve a user-submitted alert, as of the `-f` path: all five actions a `user_submitted` alert offers carry `Required: []string{"new_value"}` in `api.AlertActionFields`, which `--action` cannot supply and a manifest can. `alert retract` is no longer the only CLI path to clear one — the split is now about intent, not capability: retract means "I flagged the wrong cell", resolve means "this is fixed".
- `alert submit` is the group's one verb with a result document: `-o json|yaml` print the server's `{issue_item_ids, results}`, the only view carrying each row's own record id, and `-o name` prints `alert/<id>` for each aggregate a flagged row joined (`user_submitted.IssueNames`, the set the headline names), through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves the result lines to stderr. A submit in which every row failed is still an error with nothing on stdout. `retract` prints the server's `{issue_resolved, results}` and `alert/<id>`. `resolve` prints `{id, action, resolved, dataset_id}` per alert sent an action, `resolved` being the re-read alert's state or null when the re-read failed, and one `List` of them for a file of several documents; qctl builds it rather than printing the route's answer, whose `stored_item_url.fields` can hold a cloud provider's keys and auth info. Its prose, the `apply_migration` pre-flight included, moves to stderr, and `--dry-run` refuses `-o json|name` because it writes a YAML manifest. `warning submit`, `resolve` and `retract` mirror the alert verbs with `warning/<id>`.

## warning commands (act on a warning)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `warning resolve <warning-id>` | `internal/commands/warning/resolve.go` (flag path, `rowActionNote`) + `internal/commands/warning/resolve_file.go` (flag matrix incl. `--row-id`/`--set`/`--reason` in `checkValueFlags`, `-f` runner, `confirmResolution`) + `internal/warnings/flag_fields.go` (`FlagsCanSupply`, `FlagForm`, `CheckRuleOutputs`) + `internal/commands/resolveexplain/resolveexplain.go` (`Warning`: the compound keyword 409 and change review lines `alert resolve` gets, then the action note on a failure) + `internal/commands/resolveexplain/staged.go` (`Staged`) | `warnings.NewClient` (+ `rule_suggestions` to read a staged suggestion) | `resolve_test.go`, `resolve_file_test.go`, `result_test.go`, `rule_flags_test.go` (helpers in `helpers_test.go`) |
| `warning propose <warning-id>` | `internal/commands/warning/propose.go` (flag matrix shared with `resolve` through `resolveInputs`; the rest in `internal/commands/change_proposal/propose.go`) | `change_proposals.NewClient` (+ `warnings` for the target check) | `internal/commands/change_proposal/change_proposal_test.go` |
| `warning submit` | `internal/commands/warning/submit.go` | `warnings.NewClient` | `warning_test.go` (helpers in `helpers_test.go`) |
| `warning retract <warning-id>` | `internal/commands/warning/retract.go` | `warnings.NewClient` | `warning_test.go` (helpers in `helpers_test.go`) |

Notes:
- The group is `internal/commands/warning/warning.go`. `submit`/`retract` are user-submitted-only — `retract` refuses a rule-raised warning with exit 2 after pre-fetching it — while `resolve` is the mirror of `alert resolve` and reaches any warning that offers actions, user-submitted ones included (through `-f`; see below).
- `warning resolve` validates `--action` against the fetched warning's `warning_actions_list`. The flag path rejects offered actions needing fields (exit 2, with a `--dry-run` hint) unless `warnings.FlagsCanSupply` says `--row-id`, `--set` and `--reason` cover every required field — today `ignore_rule_for_row` and `overwrite_rule_outputs`, derived from `api.WarningActionFields`, not listed; the matrix (`checkValueFlags`) refuses a flag the action does not read and a missing required one before `Bootstrap`, and `CheckRuleOutputs` refuses a `--set`/`spec.field_name_to_value` key outside the fetched warning's `rule_output_columns` on both paths. Neither row action reaches `resolve_one_warning`, so their report replaces the warning-wide republish note with `rowActionNote`. `-f` accepts a `WarningResolution` manifest and checks its fields against `api.WarningActionFields` before sending. Multi-document files and declines use the same runner as alert resolve. `ignore_column_for_future_anomalies` prompts on either path and `--yes` skips that prompt. The four value-mapping forms and `accept_gtin_prefix` can resolve across the table's data sources without prompting; `RenderNextActions` in `internal/warnings/nextactions.go` describes their reach. `accept_vin_manufacturer_code` is also marked in the footer. User-submitted warnings can be resolved with a manifest; `warning retract` expresses a different intent.
- `describe alert` and `describe warning` print, under each NEXT ACTIONS line, the action's entry from `api.AlertActionCatalog` / `api.WarningActionCatalog` for the issue type (`alerts.CatalogNote`), and follow the server's `action_modes`: `propose` swaps the command to `qctl alert|warning propose` with an approver note (and the 409 `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE` holder when a live proposal holds the issue), `none` prints `<action>: not open to you` instead of a command, and a missing map prints `resolve` lines as before. `explain alert.alert_actions_list` / `warning.warning_actions_list` print an ACTIONS section of every catalog entry, and `explain alert|warning.issue_type` adds each value's business label and fix owner (`internal/commands/explain/issuetype.go`); text output only.
- Same shapes as the alert pair (see above), with two differences: `warning submit` accepts `--yes` as a no-op and never prompts, because a warning holds no row; and a successful `warning retract` can leave the note on display, because the token removal reaches quarantined rows only — the backend logs that and has no status for it.
- `qctl get data <table> --is-bad-data --warning-item-id <id>` filters to the rows carrying one warning, and `--alert-item-id <id>` is its alert-side twin. The alert one is hand-registered rather than derived — `alert_item_id` stays on `dataFlagDenylist` because its `int | str` wire type cannot be constructed or serialized through the generated params struct — and qctl refuses it without `--is-bad-data` (exit 2), which the backend does not. `describe alert` prints the whole line in its NEXT ACTIONS footer for an unresolved row-level alert, on every view but `-o json` — YAML output, including the default `-vv` raw dump, carries the footer as comments; `-o table` carries it as plain lines at every verbosity; and `-o json` alone carries none, having no comment syntax to hide it in. The rendered page remains the other route: every flagged cell carries `[A<id>]` and the row's Issues column lists the tokens, with the raw `alert_item_ids_` column under `-o json`.

## notification commands (clear your inbox)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `notification read [<id>]` | `internal/commands/notification/read.go` | `notifications.NewClient` | `read_test.go` |
| `notification dismiss [<id>]` | `internal/commands/notification/dismiss.go` | `notifications.NewClient` | `dismiss_test.go` |
| `notification set-preference <type>...` | `internal/commands/notification/set_preference.go` | `notification_preferences.NewClient` | `set_preference_test.go` |

Notes:
- Both verbs take one notification id, or `--all` for the bulk form; `--type` is repeatable and narrows `--all`. The shared `scopeFlags` in `notification.go` validates that shape and parses `--type` **before** `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, so a wrong command line costs no config read and no request. Its `--all` guard covers ANY argument count, not just one, so it stays safe if a verb's `cobra.Args` is ever widened.
- Reads live under `qctl get notifications`. There is no `describe notification`: the payload is small enough that `-o json` on the listing is the detail view, and the ids worth following (`review_id`, `thread_id`, `dataset_rule_id`) belong to other resources.
- `read` is idempotent — the backend sets `read_at = coalesce(read_at, now)` with no dismissed filter, so a second read is a no-op and a *dismissed* notification can still be marked read. `read --all` is the narrower operation, not a bulk version of the same thing: `mark_all_read` filters `read_at IS NULL AND dismissed_at IS NULL`, so it never touches a dismissed row. (It is still idempotent — a second `read --all` just reports 0.)
- `dismiss` is **not** idempotent: the backend's update filters `dismissed_at IS NULL` and the view turns a zero rowcount into `APIError(404, "Notification not found or already dismissed")`, so a repeated dismiss exits 3. `dismiss --all` is not affected — an empty inbox is a 200 with a count of 0.
- `read --all --type a --type b` is ONE request (`mark-all-read` takes the repeatable filter as a query parameter). `dismiss --all --type a --type b` is N: dismiss-by-type takes the type in the URL **path**. `dismissByTypes` attempts every type even after one fails, prints the counts that landed, and returns the first failure wrapped with `%w` so the exit code survives.
- Only `dismiss --all` is gated behind `cmdutil.Confirm` + `--yes`; it is irreversible and names nothing it acts on. A single `dismiss` is unprompted, matching `comment-thread resolve`.
- `set-preference` is read-modify-write over the email preferences routes, mirroring cosmosview's `useNotificationEmailPreferences.ts`: it GETs `{preferences, defaults}`, refuses (exit 2, before the PUT) any `<type>` that is not a GET key, sets each named type in the effective map, and PUTs only the entries that then differ from `defaults` (`notification_preferences.WithEmail`), because the PUT replaces every stored override. It skips the PUT when no named type changes. `--email` is a string flag taking exactly `true` or `false`, required and checked before `Bootstrap`. It prints one `<type>: email on|off (was on|off|unchanged, default on|off)` line per type; `-o json|yaml` moves that to stderr and prints the resulting `{preferences, defaults}`. It is not in `destructiveCommands`, like `read` and `dismiss`.
- Mutations print one stable line: `notification/<uuid> marked read`, `notification/<uuid> dismissed`, `marked N notification(s) read[ (types)]`, `dismissed N notification(s)[ (type: n, ...)]`. Under `-o json|yaml` that line goes to stderr and stdout carries `{id, read: true}` or `{id, dismissed: true}` for one id, whose route answers only `{message, success}`, and `{read_count}` or `{dismissed_count}` for `--all`, the dismiss count summed over the types that landed. Neither verb has a name view.

## slack commands (an organization's Slack settings)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slack set-alert-type <alert-type>...` | `internal/commands/slack/set_alert_type.go` | `slack_config.NewClient` (`Get`, `Put`) | `set_alert_type_test.go` |

Notes:
- The verb half of the `slack-config` resource; the read is `get slack-config`. Per alert type Slack settings are deliberately CLI-only: cosmosview's Slack settings page edits the use case switches, the tier delays and the reminder interval and sends `alert_types` back unchanged (cosmosview-rb9r).
- `set-alert-type` is read-modify-write: the PUT replaces the whole config, so it GETs it, applies one `slack_config.Change` to each named `alert_types` entry (`slack_config.WithAlertTypes`) and PUTs all of it, skipping the PUT when no entry changes. An entry left with neither `enabled` nor `delay_hours` is removed, because the server refuses an empty entry. Because the use case settings go back as read, the PUT's ops channel 403 (a change to which use cases post to `ops` without `ops:slack:manage`) cannot fire.
- `--enabled` (`true|false|default`) and `--delay-hours` (`N>=0|default`) are string flags, and `--reset` drops the entry and combines with neither; `changeFromFlags` checks all of it before `Bootstrap`, exit 2. Alert type names are forwarded as typed, like `get alerts --issue-type` (the server 422s an unknown one, exit 2); completion offers `api.IssueTypeAlertActions`' keys.
- It prints one `<type>: enabled on|off|default, delay <N>h|default (was ...|unchanged)` line per named type; `-o json|yaml` moves those lines to stderr and prints the resulting config. No `-o name`. It is not in `destructiveCommands`, like `notification set-preference`.

## rule-upgrade commands (rollout of a new default revision)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rule-upgrade notify <rule>` | `internal/commands/rule_upgrade/notify.go` | `rule_upgrades.NewClient` (+ `rule_versions` resolver) | `notify_test.go` |

Notes:
- `get rule-upgrades` and `rule-upgrade notify` share one addressing step, `rule_upgrades.ResolveFamily`: `ResolveRuleIDAny` for the revision, then `GetRuleRevisionAllReleases` for the family id and every release. The read needs a FAMILY id and the notify needs the family's DEFAULT revision id, and the positional names a revision, never a family: `ResolveRuleIDAny` does not take the `family_id` that `get rules -o json` carries.
- `rule-upgrade notify` rejects a non-default `--release` client-side, exit 2 (`selectRevision`). The backend refuses it as well but raises it as a `ValueError` the view maps to **404**, which would read as "no such revision" for a revision that plainly exists.
- The 403 explanation is attached only to a 403 from `Blast`: the server says "Only the revision creator can blast notifications" without naming which revision, and the creator test runs *before* the default test, so a non-creator sees 403 even on a non-default revision.
- `--message` is read through `Changed()`, not emptiness: omitting it sends JSON `null`, which leaves each notice's existing text alone. `--message ""` blanks them. `TestNotifyWithoutMessageSendsNull` guards the distinction.
- `-o json|yaml` print the server's `{message, reactivated_count}` and move the prose to stderr; there is no name view.
- Reads live under `qctl get rule-upgrades <rule>`. There is no `describe rule-upgrade`: the whole object is one response, and `-o json` on the listing is the detail view — it is the only rule-upgrade view carrying `family_id`, `new_default_revision_id` and each item's `current_revision_id`.

## table-rule commands (execution order)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `table-rule recheck-clean-rows <name-or-id>` | `internal/commands/table_rule/recheck.go` | `dataset_rules.NewClient` (`CleanRowRecheckCount`, `RecheckCleanRows`) | `recheck_test.go` |
| `table-rule move <name-or-id>` | `internal/commands/table_rule/move.go` | `dataset_rules.NewClient` (`ListAllDatasetRules`, `ReorderDatasetRule`) | `move_test.go` |
| `table-rule test-filter [<name-or-id>]` | `internal/commands/table_rule/test_filter.go` | `dataset_rules.NewClient` (`ListAllDatasetRules`, `RowFilterCount`), `row_filters.NewClient` (`CountKept`) | `test_filter_test.go` |
| `table-rule propose <name-or-id>` | `internal/commands/table_rule/propose.go` (flags and create through `internal/commands/change_proposal/propose.go`; `--set-param`/`--unset-param` through `dataset_rules.ParseParamEdit`) | `dataset_rules.NewClient` (`ListAllDatasetRules`, `GetDatasetRuleDetailContext`), `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Create`) | `propose_test.go` |

Notes:
- `table-rule move` exists because position is not a field of the PATCH route (`PatchDatasetRuleRequestSchema`), so `apply -f` cannot carry it. A declarative `spec.position` was rejected because the server clamps the value and never closes gaps, so a manifest would show drift that no apply can fix. Reads stay under `get table-rules` (whose default order is `position`) and `describe table-rule`.
- Addressing is `describe table-rule`'s: an instance name, a SHORT-ID or a UUID with `--table`/`--table-id`, or an `r-*` alias alone. An alias alone is resolved to its table through `GetDatasetRuleDetail`'s `dataset_id`. A table flag always decides the table, alias or not (`moveTableID`), so an alias beside a flag must name a rule on that table or exits 3 — unlike `describe table-rule`, a read, which lets the alias win. `--before`/`--after` take the same forms and are resolved on the same table.
- Resolution is `dataset_rules.FindRuleInList` over ONE full ordered read (`ListAllDatasetRules`). It is stricter than `ResolveDatasetRule`: there is no case-insensitive substring fallback, and a full UUID must be in the list, so a write never picks a rule from a fragment or from another table. No match exits 3 with the org-scope note; an ambiguous one exits 2.
- `dataset_rules.PlanMove` computes the landing position, the clamp and the shifted rules with the server's own arithmetic (`RuleController.reorder_rule`). `move_test.go` in `internal/dataset_rules` checks every plan against a transcription of that function over gapped tables. `--before`/`--after` translate to one stored position that depends on the direction of travel: before X is X's position moving up and X-1 moving down; after X is X's position moving down and X+1 moving up.
- A plan that changes nothing sends no request and prints `unchanged`. A rule already adjacent to its target counts as unchanged even across a gap.
- `table-rule propose` names the rule as `table-rule move` does (`validateRuleSelector`, `resolveRuleOnTable`), reads `--set-param`/`--unset-param` through `dataset_rules.ParseParamEdit` (neither given exits 2) and `change_proposal.ReadProposeOptions` before `Bootstrap`, GETs the rule's live params (`GetDatasetRuleDetailContext`, display units), merges with `ParamEdit.Apply` (an unknown `--unset-param` key exits 2 with no create) and creates through `ProposeOptions.CreateRuleParams` (`change_proposals.RuleParamsRequest`: `{operation: dataset_rule_patch, dataset_rule_id, payload: {params}}`). It prints `change_proposals.WriteParamsDiff` of live vs proposed (or that they are equal; the no-op is still sent, since atlas accepts it) to the human stream, then `change_proposal.WriteResult`. It has no `--dry-run`, and is catalogued under `change-proposal`.
- `--position <= 0` is refused with exit 2, where the server would clamp it to first.
- There is no confirmation prompt: the move is reversible, and atlas mints no HistoryItem and runs no reprocess for it. The result prints the rules that shifted and a `--position <old>` line that moves the rule back, naming the rule by UUID because a free-form instance name can look like an `r-*` alias, which `FindTableRule` resolves before any name.
- `-o json|yaml` print `{id, instance_name, table_id, from, to, requested, clamped, unchanged, shifted}`, built from the plan because the reorder route's answer carries none of it: `requested` is null unless `--position` was given, and `shifted` lists the `{id, instance_name}` of every rule that moved one place. `-o name` prints `table-rule/<uuid>`. The prose moves to stderr under all three.
- cosmosview is not a reference implementation for positions. `DatasetRulesListTable.tsx` sends `targetIndex + 1` from a single page (qctl-022).
- `table-rule test-filter` counts the rows a row filter keeps from exactly one input: a rule (resolved as `table-rule move` resolves it) reads its saved filter's count (GET `.../dataset-rules/{rule}/row-filter-test`, "Checks"); `-f` reads `spec.dataset_id` and `spec.row_filter` from one `kind: TableRule` document, and `--row-filter-file` reads a bare filter with `--table`/`--table-id`, both POSTing the draft without its description (`.../datasets/{id}/row-filter-test`, "Keeps"). Every usage refusal, including a table flag beside `-f`, exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`. The prose is `row_filters.WriteCount`'s, except that a rule with no row filter on a non-empty table gets one line of its own (`writeSavedFilterCount`); `-o json|yaml` print the server's `RowFilterCountSchema` and move the prose to stderr.

## table-series commands (period locks, period delete and recreate, rule-set approval)

| Command | File | Client | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `table-series approve-rules <name-or-id> [--fingerprint <sha256>]` | `internal/commands/table_series/approve_rules.go` | `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `GetRuleApproval`, `ApproveRules`; `internal/table_series/rule_approval.go`) | `../root/table_series_rule_approval_test.go` |
| `table-series lock <name-or-id> --period <key>` | `internal/commands/table_series/table_series.go` | `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `Get`, `SetLocked`) | `../root/table_series_lock_test.go` |
| `table-series unlock <name-or-id> --period <key>` | `internal/commands/table_series/table_series.go` | `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `Get`, `SetLocked`) | `../root/table_series_lock_test.go` |
| `table-series delete-period <name-or-id> --period <key>` | `internal/commands/table_series/period_ops.go` | `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `Get`, `DeletePeriod`; `internal/table_series/period_ops.go`) | `../root/table_series_period_ops_test.go` |
| `table-series recreate-period <name-or-id> --period <key>` | `internal/commands/table_series/period_ops.go` | `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `Get`, `ListReloadableFiles` for `--reload-all-files`, `RecreatePeriod`; `internal/table_series/period_ops.go`) | `../root/table_series_period_ops_test.go` |

Notes:
- `table-series approve-rules` follows `deliverable-run sign-off`: one GET of `/table-series/{id}/rule-set-approval`, `table_series.FormatRuleApprovalPreview` on stderr even with `--yes`, `cmdutil.Confirm`, then one POST of the previewed fingerprint. `--fingerprint` goes through `table_series.NormalizeFingerprint` (64 hex digits, lowercased; exit 2 before authentication) and must equal the current fingerprint, else exit 2 with no write. An empty `rule_set` exits 2 and an already-approved rule set prints `already approved` without a POST. It is in `cmdutil`'s `destructiveCommands`, so the protected-context gate runs first.
