A 409 gets the `get table-series <uuid> --rule-approval` re-review line. Both responses are re-decoded with `jsonnum.Unmarshal`, and JSON/YAML print the response body itself (`table_series.WriteRuleApproval`).
- `table-series delete-period` and `recreate-period` (`internal/commands/table_series/period_ops.go`) share `resolvePeriod`: `ValidateReference` and `table_series.ValidatePeriodKey` exit 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, then `ResolveID` and `Get` find the member with the exact key, an unknown key exiting 3 with no write. `recreate-period` checks every flag first in `parseRecreateFlags` (exit 2 before auth): `--schema` copy|copy-without-columns|learn-from-file (hyphens or underscores), `--leave-out-column` only and always with copy-without-columns, `--key-column` xor `--no-key-columns` and neither with learn-from-file, `--loading-process` without composite, `--include` against `api.DatasetCopyOption` minus settings, `--reload-file` (positive id or `file/<id>`) xor `--reload-all-files`. `--reload-all-files` GETs reloadable-files before the prompt and sends every id. Both are in `cmdutil`'s `destructiveCommands` and confirm with `cmdutil.Confirm`; the DELETE and the recreate POST run under `client.ResolveTimeout(periodOpTimeout)` (5 minutes) and are never retried. JSON/YAML print the response body; `-o name` prints `table/<id>` (the deleted table, or the new one), with prose on stderr. A failed file reload is a stderr `Warning:` at exit 0. `SERIES_PERIOD_CHANGE_BLOCKED` gets apierror's context hint (`seriesPeriodChangeBlockedHint`): one command per blocker.

## submission-status commands (period waivers)

| Command | File | Client | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `submission-status waive --cloud-source-id <id> --period <key> --reason <text>` | `internal/commands/submission_status/submission_status.go` | `submissions.NewClient` (`Waive`) | `../root/submission_status_waiver_test.go` |
| `submission-status unwaive --cloud-source-id <id> --period <key>` | `internal/commands/submission_status/submission_status.go` | `submissions.NewClient` (`WithdrawWaiver`) | `../root/submission_status_waiver_test.go` |

Notes:
- `submission-status waive` and `unwaive` share one runner (`runWaiver`). Both require `--cloud-source-id` (a positive id or `cloud-source/<id>`, through `cmdutil.PositiveIDFlag`) and `--period` (`table_series.ValidatePeriodKey`); a missing, blank, padded or multi-line value exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`. `waive` also requires `--reason`, trimmed, whose missing or blank value exits 2 there too; it is the POST's `WaiveSubmissionSchema` body. The key is sent as typed, as one escaped path segment of `/data-sources/{id}/periods/{period_key}/waive`: `waive` POSTs it (`WaiveSubmissionWithResponse`), `unwaive` DELETEs it (`WithdrawWaiverWithResponse`), one request each with no read first and no prompt. Neither is classified as destructive. The table view is one `submissions.StatusLine`; `-o json|yaml` print the response body through `output.WriteRaw` and move that line to stderr; `-o name` exits 2. An `unwaive` answered 204 prints a `no longer expected for period` line instead (stderr under `-o json|yaml`, stdout empty) and exits 0. Errors go through `internal/comments`, so 404 exits 3, 403 exits 4, and the 409s (`SUBMISSION_PERIOD_NOT_EXPECTED`, `SUBMISSION_ALREADY_DECLARED`, `PERIOD_LOCKED`) and 422 `SERIES_PERIOD_KEY_INVALID` exit 2, with apierror's hints for the three 409 codes. qctl has no commands for nil returns.

## deliverable commands (draft authoring, row filter checks and completeness)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deliverable accept-column-suggestions <ref> --sheet <sheet>` | `internal/commands/deliverable/accept_column_suggestions.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveDraftTarget`, `GetRevision`, `AcceptColumnSuggestions`) | `../root/deliverable_column_suggestions_test.go` |
| `deliverable confirm-columns <ref> --sheet <sheet> --column <column>` | `internal/commands/deliverable/confirm_columns.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveDraftTarget`, `GetRevision`, `ConfirmColumns` → `PatchColumnBinding`) | `../root/deliverable_confirm_columns_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/confirm_columns_test.go` |
| `deliverable confirm-cells <ref> --sheet <sheet> --cell <A1>` | `internal/commands/deliverable/confirm_cells.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveDraftTarget`, `GetRevision`, `ConfirmCells` → `PatchCellBinding`) | `../root/deliverable_confirm_cells_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/confirm_cells_test.go` |
| `deliverable completeness <ref>` | `internal/commands/deliverable/completeness.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `Completeness`) | `../root/deliverable_completeness_test.go` |
| `deliverable test-filter [<ref>] --sheet <sheet>` | `internal/commands/deliverable/test_filter.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveID`, `ResolveRevision`, `GetRevision`, `SheetRowFilterReport`), `datasets.NewClient` (`ResolveID`), `row_filters.NewClient` (`CountKept`) | `../root/deliverable_test_filter_test.go` |

Notes:
- `deliverable confirm-cells` selects the draft through `draftRevision`, shared with `confirm-columns`. `--sheet` is an exact cell-binding sheet name and need not have a sheet binding. Required `--cell` accepts repeated or comma-separated A1 references, normalized with `NormalizeCellRef`; malformed or duplicate selectors exit 2 before authentication. Every selected cell must be bound before any write (exit 2 otherwise). Flagged cells are PATCHed in reference order with their stored source, row filter, format and label-cell reference; unflagged cells are reported `already confirmed` without a write. The server re-reads the label or chooses a nearby default. JSON/YAML emit the selected binding array on stdout and the report on stderr; name output exits 2. A failed PATCH preserves the API exit code and names earlier successful confirmations. Apply/diff report remaining flagged cells, excluding planned cell PATCH/DELETE requests.
- `deliverable accept-column-suggestions`: `--release` (exact label or full UUID) or `--revision` (full UUID or `deliverable-revision/<uuid>`) selects a revision of the named deliverable; otherwise `ResolveDraftTarget` chooses its only draft. The command reads its binding tree, checks its current draft state and parent, and resolves `--sheet` as an exact bound sheet name before the one bodyless `POST /deliverable-sheet-bindings/{id}/column-bindings/apply-suggestions`. A missing sheet exits 3 and local flag errors exit 2 before authentication. The server's draft gate still handles a concurrent enable with 409 (exit 2).
- The text report prints the server's created and replaced counts, all current mappings with origins, match reasons and warnings, and returned empty-column hints. Replaced means deleted suggested bindings, including deletions without a new mapping. The API does not identify which individual mappings were created or replaced. JSON/YAML preserve the original response and move the report to stderr; name output exits 2. Suggestions are explicit; no read or apply calls this operation.
- `deliverable confirm-columns` selects its draft and sheet exactly as `accept-column-suggestions` does (both go through `draftSheet` in `deliverable.go`). `--column` takes letters or 1-based indexes, repeated or comma-separated, each through `deliverables.ParseColumnKey`; a missing, malformed or duplicate column exits 2 before authentication. `deliverables.Client.ConfirmColumns` refuses, with exit 2 and before any write, a named column the sheet does not bind, listing the bound ones as letters. It then sends, one at a time in column order, a `PATCH /deliverable-sheet-bindings/{sheet}/column-bindings/{id}` for each named binding with `needs_confirmation` set, whose body is the binding's stored `target_column_index`, `source_type`, `source_column`, `run_param` and `format_override`, nulls included, plus `literal_value` for a literal source; an unflagged binding is reported `already confirmed` and not sent. A failed PATCH's error names its column and, wrapped with `%w`, the columns confirmed before it, keeping the failure's exit code. The text report prints one `Column <n> (<letter>) <- <source>: confirmed|already confirmed` line per named column, plus any PATCH warnings; JSON/YAML print the named bindings (the PATCH response, else the tree's) on stdout and the report on stderr; name output exits 2.
- `deliverable test-filter` takes exactly one of a deliverable reference and `-f`; every usage refusal, including a flag the chosen input does not take, exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`. With a reference it selects the revision as `inspect deliverable` does (`ResolveRevision`: `--release`/`--revision`, else the enabled revision, else the only one, in any state), checks the revision's parent, and resolves `--sheet` as an exact bound sheet name through `boundSheet` in `deliverable.go`, which `draftSheet` shares (a missing sheet exits 3). A series sheet without `--period`, or `--period` on a fixed-table sheet, exits 2 before the one `GET /deliverable-revisions/{rev}/sheet-bindings/{sheet}/row-filter-report` (`period_key` is sent only for a series sheet); `--table`/`--table-id` exit 2 because the report reads the binding's own table. It prints `deliverable/<id> release "<release>" sheet "<sheet>" on table N` (a series sheet: `on period "<period>" of series <uuid>`), then `row_filters.WriteReport`; `-o json|yaml` print the report response through `output.WriteRaw`. With `-f` it reads one `kind: Deliverable` document (`deliverables.ParseManifest`; several documents exit 2), requires `spec.sheets.<sheet>` and a non-null `row_filter` on it, and POSTs the filter without its description (`row_filters.Client.CountKept`) on `--table`/`--table-id` when given, else on `spec.sheets.<sheet>.table` through `datasets.Client.ResolveID`. The count prints through `deliverables.WriteRowFilterCount` under the key `deliverables.SheetFilterKey` builds, so it reads as apply's and diff's do, naming the series' reference table only when no table flag was given and the sheet's `series` is set; a 422 is wrapped `spec.sheets.<sheet>.row_filter does not fit table N: %w` (exit 2), and `-o json|yaml` print the server's `RowFilterCountSchema`. The prose moves to stderr under `-o json|yaml`, and `-o name` exits 2.

- `deliverable completeness` resolves including the trash and reads `GET /deliverables/{id}/completeness`, with `--limit` (1–10000) sent only when given and validated before bootstrap. Its text grid names the sheet, table id, row id, problem, what happens next and re-issue eligibility; waiver, carrier and pending re-issue dispositions take that order of precedence, as in the UI. It uses state-neutral carrier wording and integer run ids without extra metadata reads. JSON/YAML print the original response, unknown fields included, and name output exits 2. A 400 keeps exit 2 and adds conditional filter repair and readiness/waive command templates; it invents no identities from the server's prose. Waiving a row absent from readiness's rows requires a verified `waivable_row` target on a `carried_rows` error.

## deliverable-run commands (one run's readiness, render, sign-off, waivers, drift and source overrides)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deliverable-run readiness <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/readiness.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `Readiness`) | `../root/deliverable_run_readiness_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run drift <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/drift.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `Drift`) | `../root/deliverable_run_drift_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run render <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/render.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `RequestRender`, `WaitForRender`) | `../root/deliverable_run_render_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run sign-off <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/sign_off.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `GetRun`, `Readiness`, `SignOff`) | `../root/deliverable_run_sign_off_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run mark-delivered <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/lifecycle.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `MarkDelivered`) | `../root/deliverable_run_lifecycle_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run cancel <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/lifecycle.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `CancelRun`) | `../root/deliverable_run_lifecycle_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run waive <id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/waivers.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `CheckWaivableRow`, `CreateWaiver`) | `../root/deliverable_run_waivers_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run unwaive <id> <waiver-id>` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/waivers.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `DeleteWaiver`) | `../root/deliverable_run_waivers_test.go` |
| `deliverable-run override-source <id> [<source-id>]` | `internal/commands/deliverable_run/override_source.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ParseRunID`, `GetRun`, `GetRevision`, `FindRunSource`, `OverrideSource`) | `../root/deliverable_run_override_source_test.go` |

Notes:
- `deliverable-run readiness` takes a run id or `deliverable-run/<id>` (`deliverables.ParseRunID`). An unknown `--bucket` (checked against `deliverables.ReadinessBuckets`), `--page` below 1 and `--limit` outside 1–500 (the server's own bounds) exit 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`; `--bucket`, `--page` and `--limit` are sent only when given, so the server's defaults (all buckets, page 1, 50 rows) apply otherwise. It makes one `GET /deliverable-runs/{id}/readiness`, which `deliverables.Client.Readiness` gives a 45s deadline of its own so the server's 30s timeout answers first. The table view is `deliverables.WriteReadiness`; `-o json|yaml` print the whole response body through `output.WriteRaw`, preserving unknown fields; the table rows include `WAIVER REASON` and nullable `FILTER UNDECIDED` (`yes`, `no`, or `-`); `-o name` exits 2. A cancelled run's 409 exits 2.
- `deliverable-run drift` takes a run id or `deliverable-run/<id>` (`deliverables.ParseRunID`). `--limit` outside 1–10000 (`deliverables.MaxDriftLimit`, the server's own bound) exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, and is sent only when given, so the server's default of 1000 rows applies otherwise. It makes one `GET /deliverable-runs/{id}/drift`, which `deliverables.Client.Drift` gives a 45s deadline of its own so the server's 30s timeout answers first. The table view is `deliverables.WriteDrift`: remedy, reissue and superseding runs, rows shown against the limit, a truncation line, the server's note, then a row grid or `No changes found in the delivered rows.`; `-o json|yaml` print the whole response body through `output.WriteRaw`; `-o name` exits 2. A run that is not delivered answers 409, which exits 2.
- `deliverable-run render` takes a run id or `deliverable-run/<id>` (`deliverables.ParseRunID`) and makes one `POST /deliverable-runs/{id}/render`; a run that is not open, rendered or stale answers 409, which exits 2. The answer is the run as it was before any worker claimed the render. Without `--wait` the table view is `deliverables.WriteRenderRequested` (`deliverable-run/<id> render requested`, state, Next: line); `-o json|yaml` print the response body through `output.WriteRaw`, `-o name` prints `deliverable-run/<id>`. `--wait` (`cmdutil.AddWaitFlags`, default 900 s) takes the answer's `attempt_id` and `rendered_at` as the baseline (`deliverables.BaselineOf`) and polls `GET /deliverable-runs/{id}` through `pollwait.Run` (`deliverables.WaitForRender`) until `deliverables.RenderSettled`: the run is signed_off, delivered or cancelled, or its `attempt_id` is above the baseline's and its `rendered_at` moved or its `render_error` is set. Progress (`WriteRenderProgress`) goes to stderr on each change of state or attempt. `deliverables.RenderVerdict` passes only state `rendered` with no `render_error`, printing `WriteRendered` (stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`); anything else exits 1 with the render error or the run's message, and running out exits 124 saying whether a worker ever claimed the render. The command's `Render` function is shared with `create deliverable-run --render`.
- The `deliverable-run` parent itself is a pure dispatch group; the run reads are `get deliverable-runs`, `get deliverable-run` and `describe deliverable-run`.
- `deliverable-run sign-off` takes a positive run id or `deliverable-run/<id>`, reads the run, refuses a state other than `rendered` with exit 2, then reads readiness with `limit=1`. `deliverables.FormatSignOffPreview` prints the reviewed attempt, typed parameters, last-render totals, the waived count (sheets plus unbound carried sheets plus `waived_without_sheet`) and `FormatReadinessSummary` on stderr even with `--yes`. `cmdutil.Confirm` prompts on a TTY and otherwise requires `--yes`; the command is classified as destructive, so the protected-context gate runs first and `--yes` does not satisfy it. One `POST /deliverable-runs/{id}/sign-off` sends that previewed `expected_attempt_id`, with no read after confirmation and no automatic POST retry. A `SIGNOFF_BLOCKED` refusal prints the server's best-effort aggregate context and a readiness hint; `STALE_ATTEMPT` asks for a fresh describe and render review; a 403 names the required permission through generated metadata and explicitly says editor and agent roles cannot sign off. HTTP exit codes and `APIError` remain reachable through the unwrap chain, including object-shaped 422s. Table output acknowledges the locked report; JSON/YAML preserve the full response body and name output prints `deliverable-run/<id>`.
- `deliverable-run waive` validates its table selector (`ValidateTableSelectorFromFlags`), positive `--row-id` and non-blank `--reason` before bootstrap. `ResolveTableIDFromFlags` resolves names exactly, including numeric names. `CheckWaivableRow` pages live readiness at 500 rows per request until that table/row has a `blocked_awaiting_review`, `blocked_failing_rules`, `blocked_indeterminate` or `carried_unbound` entry, or matches a positive `errors[].waivable_row` target on a `carried_rows` error. The response must name the requested run in `open`, `rendered` or `stale`; frozen readiness and rows without evidence are refused with exit 2 before the POST. Readiness text prints the exact waiver command for a verified carried target; prose-only errors and warnings authorize none. The server rechecks eligibility at the POST and returns 409 `WAIVER_NOT_ELIGIBLE` (exit 2) if the row is no longer eligible. The reason is trimmed. Table output prominently prints the created waiver id and the `unwaive` command; `-o name` is the bare numeric waiver id, and JSON/YAML preserve the original response body. No waiver id is cached.
- `deliverable-run unwaive` requires positive run and waiver ids and makes one `DELETE /deliverable-runs/{id}/waivers/{waiver-id}`, expecting 204. It supports table output only and is classified as destructive (protected-context gate), because the deleted waiver and its reason have no history entry. Both waiver clients preserve HTTP exit codes through the shared deliverables error wrapper, including object-shaped 422s.
- `deliverable-run override-source` names the source with exactly one of `--sheet`, `--cell Sheet!A1` (`deliverables.ParseSourceCell`: split at the last `!`, A1 normalized) or a positional source id, and needs a table selector (`ValidateTableSelectorFromFlags`) and a non-blank `--reason` (trimmed); each failure exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`. It reads `GET /deliverable-runs/{id}` and refuses with exit 2 a run that is not open, rendered or stale (`CanOverrideSources`), then reads the run's revision and resolves `--sheet`/`--cell` through the binding tree to a binding id and from there to the source (`FindRunSource`), so it never relies on a source id cached across an enable. An unknown sheet or cell, a cell that is not an aggregate, and a total that mirrors a sheet's rows (pointing at `--sheet <mirrored>`) exit 2; an unknown source id exits 3 with the org-scope note. A target equal to the source's current `dataset_id` exits 2 with no PATCH. It then makes one `PATCH /deliverable-runs/{id}/sources/{source-id}` with `{dataset_id, reason}`. Table output is `deliverables.FormatSourceOverride` (the binding, the new table, the previous one, the resolved one, the reason), then `SourceOverrideNotices` (the source's warning, and a line when the answer is stale: went stale from rendered, or already stale) and `Next: qctl deliverable-run readiness <id>`; `-o json|yaml` print the response body through `output.WriteRaw` and `-o name` prints `deliverable-run/<id>`, with the notices on stderr. It is not classified as destructive.

## queue-message commands (repair the internal job queue)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `queue-message retry <id>...` | `internal/commands/queue_message/retry.go` | `queue.NewClient` (`Reschedule`) | `retry_test.go` |
| `queue-message resurrect <id>` | `internal/commands/queue_message/resurrect.go` | `queue.NewClient` (`GetMessage`, `Resurrect`) | `resurrect_test.go` |
| `queue-message resurrect-dead` | `internal/commands/queue_message/resurrect_dead.go` | `queue.NewClient` (`ListDeadIDs`, `ResurrectBatch`) | `resurrect_dead_test.go` |
| `queue-message retire` | `internal/commands/queue_message/retire.go` | `queue.NewClient` (`Retire`) | `retire_test.go` |

Notes:
- Every route in this group, and the two reads (`get queue-messages`, `describe queue-message`), is gated on the ops-only `OPS_QUEUE_READ` / `OPS_QUEUE_MANAGE`. The group lives in the main command tree anyway, on the `get orgs` precedent: that command calls the ops-only `ORG_LIST` route and is not segregated either.
- Every route scopes through atlas's `QueueOrganizationScope.message_filter` (`capi/capi/controllers/queue_controller.py`): a message belongs to the organization by its cloud source, or, when its `data_source_model_id` is negative on `CUEUE_DATASET_LEARN`, `CUEUE_MIGRATION`, `CUEUE_TRAINING_JOB_COMBINER`, `CUEUE_COMPOSITE` or `CUEUE_DELIVERABLE`, by the settings model, training job, table, composite spec group or deliverable run it names. `normalize_queue_message_identifiers` returns every one of those with `data_source_model_id` null. A composite or deliverable message handed to a warm worker (`WorkerController.deploy_composite_run_job_or_publish_for_worker` / `deploy_deliverable_render_job_or_publish_for_worker`) moves to `CUEUE_WORKER` with its negative id, where no arm matches it, before or after the worker acknowledges it there. A message enqueued with no `data_source_model_id` at all — the combiner's automatic hand-off to `CUEUE_MIGRATION` is one — matches no arm, so no route here can read or repair it. The `dataset_id` filter behind `--table` (`get_queue_dataset_filter`) has no `CUEUE_DELIVERABLE` arm, and its composite arms need a `changed_dataset_id` in the payload or no `composite_run_job_id`, so no `--table` finds a deliverable render or a group-scoped composite run that a warm-worker hand-off or a stale-worker recovery has stamped with its run job, and `--queue` selects those. `--cloud-source-id` still refuses a negative value, which names no cloud source.
- `retry` (the backend's reschedule) and `resurrect` are kept as two verbs because the backend keeps two eligibility rules: reschedule resets ANY unacknowledged message to deliverable-now, while resurrect only touches one whose `receive_count` has reached the ceiling and can delay redelivery. The names are the product's own — cosmosview's buttons are "Retry Now" and "Resurrect Dead Messages".
- `retry` takes ids only, with no `--filter` mode: the route answers `{"result": "ok"}` and discards the UPDATE's rowcount, so a filter mode could not honestly report what it did. The result line names the ids sent and says the server does not report how many were applied.
- `resurrect` reads the message first and refuses locally (exit 2) when it is acknowledged or not dead — the fetch-then-guard shape of `delete cloud-source` / `cloud_sources.RequireNotDeleted` — because `_resurrect_one_by_id`'s UPDATE matches nothing there and the view answers **500**. The two refusal arms differ: a live message is retry's job, while an acknowledged one is nothing's, since retry carries the same `acknowledged = false` guard and no route un-acknowledges.
- `resurrect-dead --dry-list` calls `GET /queue-messages/dead`, whose `_list_dead_ids` shares `_resurrect_maxed_out`'s predicate and its `ORDER BY dequeued_at NULLS FIRST, id`, so it is the backend's own candidate list. `--older-than` is sent to it as `idle_for` (honoured since atlas #965, which fixed atlas-ph2x), and both routes fall back to `settings.CUEUE_INVISIBLE_TIME` when it is unset — the dead route through `queue['any']`, whose per-queue lookup finds no `any_INVISIBLE_TIME` — so no queue's own `<QUEUE>_INVISIBLE_TIME` applies to either. The route's `q_name` is never sent: the batch route takes no queue, so a queue-scoped preview would not describe the run. `--limit` defaults to the batch route's own 1000 and is forwarded to both, because the dead route's default is 100.
- The confirmation prompts follow the count rule of `internal/commands/rows/confirm.go`. `resurrect-dead` carries the candidate route's count for the same `--older-than` and `--limit`; a count request the server refuses as bad usage (a malformed interval, which both routes check with `validate_postgres_interval`) fails the command before the prompt. `retire` never carries one: no read route applies its `enqueued_at` predicate.
- `retire --older-than` is REQUIRED, refusing to inherit the server's silent 30-day default. Its confirmation states that no route un-acknowledges a message — so a retired one can never be retried or resurrected again — and that cueue's own deletion of old acknowledged rows, which runs elsewhere in the platform and not from this call, counts its retention window from the row's `dequeued_at` and never deletes a row whose `dequeued_at` is NULL. A same-key re-publish with `make_duplicate_msg_available_now` can leave a NULL there, on a dead message the queue does not reset (its `<QUEUE>_RESET_DEAD_MSG` is off) and on an already-acknowledged row (atlas-8ua0). It quotes no retention window: cueue's config sets `CUEUE_DELETE_ACKNOWLEDGED_AFTER` to 7 days on `BaseConfig`, 5 minutes on `DevConfig` and 5 hours on `SandboxConfig`.
- `--older-than` is measured from a different column on the two bulk verbs: `_resurrect_maxed_out` compares `dequeued_at` (a message never dequeued always qualifies), `_acknowledge_very_old_dead` compares `enqueued_at` and has no idle clause at all.
- The two bulk verbs print the server's answer under `-o json|yaml` and move their prose to stderr: `resurrect-dead` its `{count, resurrected_ids}`, or under `--dry-list` the dead route's `{dead_ids, total_count}`, and `retire` its `{count, retired_ids}`. `-o name` prints `queue-message/<id>` for each of those ids. `retry` and `resurrect` declare `table` alone, so `-o json|yaml|name` exits 2.

## privilege commands (break-glass ops privilege requests)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `privilege request` | `internal/commands/privilege/request.go` | `privilege_requests.NewClient` (`ResolveTargetUser`, `Create`) | `request_test.go` |
| `privilege approve <id>` | `internal/commands/privilege/approve.go` (+ `decision.go`) | `privilege_requests.NewClient` (`Get`, `Approve`) | `decision_test.go` |
| `privilege reject <id>` | `internal/commands/privilege/reject.go` (+ `decision.go`) | `privilege_requests.NewClient` (`Get`, `Reject`) | `decision_test.go` |

Notes:
- The verb half of the `privilege-request` resource; the reads are `get privilege-requests`, `get privilege-audit` and `describe privilege-request`. Every route is ops-only (`OPS_PRIVILEGE_*`, held only by `Role.ops`), so the group is visible to everyone and a non-ops caller gets the backend's 403 (exit 4), the `queue-message` precedent.
- `privilege request` checks everything local before `Bootstrap`: `--action` through `privilege_requests.ParseAction` (case-insensitive, sent canonical), a blank `--target-user` through `CheckTargetUser`, and the `--justification`/`--justification-file` pair through `ValidateConflictingFlags` + `RequireOneOfFlags`, reading the file through `cmdutil.ReadTextFile` (`-` = stdin; an empty path is exit 2, an unreadable file exit 1). `--target-user` and `--action` are `MarkFlagRequired`, which root's `PersistentPreRunE` turns into exit 2. The justification's 10..4000 bound is left to the server's 422 (exit 2), which names it.
- Budget: one POST, plus the users-directory pages when `--target-user` is not a UUID; an unresolvable or ambiguous target exits 2 before the POST. A 400 (self-target, target not found, already ops / not ops, not an HQ admin, duplicate Pending) passes through with its text; the duplicate message's request id is shown, never parsed.
- The result line reads the 201's status: `created` for Pending, `created and applied` plus an "Applied immediately" line for the sole-eligible-ops exemption, where the server approves inline. `-o json|yaml` emit the wire request (YAML via a JSON round-trip); `-o name` prints only the id, for `id=$(...)` hand-off.
- `approve` and `reject` share `fetchPending` (`decision.go`): the id goes through `cmdutil.ParseUUIDArg` before `Bootstrap` (exit 2, zero requests), then `Get` runs BEFORE any POST. The decision routes answer an unknown id with 400, so the GET's 404 is what makes it exit 3, and a status other than Pending is refused locally with `privilege-request/<id> is already <status>; nothing to <verb>` (exit 2, the one message the tests pin by text). Budget: two requests on success, one on every refusal before the POST.
- `approve` then asks through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`; exit 2 without a terminal, `ErrDeclined` exit 1 on a decline). `approvePrompt` puts the justification's lines above `Approve privilege-request/<id>: <action> <target> (requested by <requestor> <age>)?`, falling back to UUIDs for a null embedded user; `ConfirmYesNo` sanitizes the whole prompt. The POST's 403 (two-person rule) and 400 (expired — which is also what marks it Expired — stale is_ops, not an HQ admin, decided in a race) pass through untouched.
- `reject` has no prompt, no `--yes` and no `--reason` (the controller discards `rejection_reason`; `Client.Reject` sends it null). Its result names the target from the pre-fetched detail, because `RejectionResponseSchema` carries no user.
- All three verbs print through `printResult` (`privilege.go`): the result lines then `Next: qctl describe privilege-request <id>`; `-o json|yaml` the wire response (`output.WriteRaw`); `-o name` the request id alone. `approve`'s line reads `target: <email> is_ops now <bool>` from `ApprovalResult.TargetUser`.

## pii-access-request commands (requester and admin workflow)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pii-access-request request` (alias `ask`) | `internal/commands/pii_access_request/pii_access_request.go` | `pii_access_requests.NewClient` (`Create`) | `pii_access_request_test.go` |
| `pii-access-request withdraw <request-id>` | `internal/commands/pii_access_request/pii_access_request.go` | `pii_access_requests.NewClient` (`Withdraw`) | `pii_access_request_test.go` |
| `pii-access-request approve <request-id>` | `internal/commands/pii_access_request/decision.go` | `pii_access_requests.NewClient` (`Approve`) | `decision_test.go` |
| `pii-access-request deny <request-id>` | `internal/commands/pii_access_request/decision.go` | `pii_access_requests.NewClient` (`Deny`) | `decision_test.go` |

- Requesters select a table with `--table`/`--table-id`; `--shared` selects the third-party provider through `cmdutil.ResolveDataTableID`. Both mutations confirm through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes` for noninteractive use); withdrawal honors protected contexts.
- `request` validates a nonblank reason of at most 4000 characters and a duration from `pii_grants.ParseDuration` before bootstrap. A denial cooldown's 429 retains `apierror.ThrottleError` and prints Retry-After without retrying the POST.
- `get pii-access-requests [table]` polls the caller's own requests; JSON/YAML are a paged List with the API records, including `deny_reason` and `grant_expires_at`. Mutation JSON/YAML are one API request record; `-o name` is `pii-access-request/<id>`, accepted by withdrawal.
- `get pii-access-requests --all` lists every requester's requests through the admin route (`pii-grant-requests`, `pii:grant`) with `requester`, `decided_by` and `grant` columns; it conflicts with `--shared` (exit 2), because the admin routes exist only for the owning organization.
- `approve` and `deny` select the table with `--table`/`--table-id` only (no `--shared`) and check the id (`parseRequestID`), `--duration` through `pii_grants.ParseDuration` and `--reason` through `parseReason` before `Bootstrap`. `approve` sends an unset `--duration`/`--reason` as absent/null so the server keeps the request's own; `deny` requires `--reason`. Both confirm through `cmdutil.Confirm`; `deny` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands`, `approve` is not, like `pii-grant create`. The server's 404/409/400 pass through untouched. Results print through `printDecision`: approve names the created `pii-grant/<id>` in `pii-grant create`'s wording then `Next: qctl get pii-grants --table-id <id>`; `-o json|yaml` the admin request record; `-o name` `pii-access-request/<id>`.

## duplicate-group commands (decide, mark or retract a mirror or append table's duplicate groups)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `duplicate-group allow <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/decide.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Decide`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group keep-one <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/decide.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Decide`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group not-duplicates <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/decide.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Decide`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group approve <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/vote.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Approve`) | `vote_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group disagree <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/vote.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Disagree`) | `vote_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group dismiss <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/vote.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Dismiss`) | `vote_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group withdraw <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/withdraw.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Withdraw`) | `vote_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group stop-series <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/stop_series.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `StopSeries`) | `vote_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group allow-all` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/table_verbs.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`AllowAll`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group reapply` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/table_verbs.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Reapply`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group comment <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/discussion.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`AddComment`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group share <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/discussion.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Share`) | `duplicate_group_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group mark` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/mark.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Mark`) | `mark_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group retract <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/mark.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Get`, `Retract`) | `mark_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group respond <group-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/respond.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`Respond`) | `respond_test.go` |
| `duplicate-group withdraw-response <group-id> <response-id>` | `internal/commands/duplicate_group/respond.go` | `duplicate_groups.NewClient` (`WithdrawResponse`) | `respond_test.go` |

- Every verb takes the table through `--table`/`--table-id` (`cmdutil.ResolveTableIDFromFlags`); the group is a full UUID or `duplicate-group/<uuid>` (`refKinds`). The UUID, the table selector, `--comment` (`cmdutil.ValidateCommentBody` when given), `--count`, `--member`, `--reason` (`disagree` and `dismiss` require it non-blank) and `--decision` are checked before any request (exit 2).
- `allow`, `keep-one`, `not-duplicates`, `approve`, `disagree`, `dismiss`, `withdraw`, `stop-series` and `share` read the group first (`GET .../duplicate-groups/{id}`). `checkOrigin` applies atlas's per-origin rules (`_VALID_KINDS` and `_MIN_ALLOWED_COUNT` in cettings `duplicate_group_controller.py`) to that read: repeated_key `--count` at least 2 or omitted, identical_rows `--count` required, person_marked no `--count`; keep-one `--member` must be an active member, which `checkMember` reads as a member with an `outcome` and no `deleted_at`, and identical_rows keep-one takes no `--member`; not-duplicates only on person_marked. A breach exits 2 after the one read. The decide prompt (`pendingNote`) reads `pending_decision`: the same choice (compared after the server's normalization) approves it; a different one replaces the caller's own (`caller.can_withdraw`) or, when `caller.withdraw_reason` is `not_proposer`, is noted as refused with `DUPLICATE_DECISION_PENDING` but still sent; with nothing pending, a `decision` or `carried_decision` makes it a change proposal. The result verb is `approved` when the result is the pending decision read first, else `decided` (effective) or `proposed` (pending); a result with `replaces_decision_id` adds a line naming the replaced decision (`replacesLine`).
- The decision body always carries all five keys; `kept_member_id` and `reason` are null except on keep-one and not-duplicates, because the server refuses them on any other kind.
- `approve`, `disagree`, `dismiss` and `withdraw` (`vote.go` `readPending`, `pendingTarget`) act on the group's `pending_decision`: approve, disagree and dismiss post to `.../decision/{decision_id}/approve|disagree|dismiss` with `{comment}`, `{reason}` and `{reason}`, withdraw to `.../decision/withdraw` with `{decision_id, comment}`. No pending decision exits 2 naming the group's effective decision and, when `caller.stop_for_series_decision_id` is set, the `stop-series` command; `--decision <uuid>` naming another decision exits 2 naming the pending one. `approve` on a decision the caller already approved (`caller.caller_approved`) without `--comment` prints that and exits 0 with no POST (`-o name` still prints the ref). The withdraw and dismiss prompts say what governs the group afterwards (`settleNote`). Whether the caller may vote (designated, excluded, proposer, admin/ops) is left to the server.
- `stop-series` (`stop_series.go`) posts `{decision_id: caller.stop_for_series_decision_id, comment}` to `.../decision/stop-series`; it exits 2 after the read when that id is null or the group has a `pending_decision`. Its result is the stop proposal (kind `stop_series`), pending or enacted.
- `allow-all --file-id <id>` (accepts `file/<id>`) posts `{stored_item_id, comment}`; `reapply` posts no body. `comment` posts `{body, parent_id}` from `--body`/`--body-file`/`--reply-to` (201). `share` refuses `--comment` on an already shared discussion (exit 2), because the server posts it only when it newly shares; it is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands`.
- Every verb but `comment` confirms through `cmdutil.Confirm`. Output: `duplicate-group/<uuid> <decided|approved|disagreed with|dismissed|withdrew|proposed> <label> [d: <uuid>] (decision: <state>, approvals: c/r, group: <state>)`, a series-scope line for a pending or effective series decision that is not a stop, one `table/<id> <outcome>: moved/released/reset/enqueued` line per period (periods can name other tables), a `Waiting for N more approval(s)` recipe and a `Held by K disagreement(s)` line while pending (decide, approve, stop-series; `writeProgress`), a "no longer applies in any period" line for an enacted stop, a take-it-back line after `disagree`, and a stderr warning per period not applied. `-o json|yaml` print the server response and move those lines to stderr; `-o name` prints `duplicate-group/<uuid>` (allow-all: each group allowed or approved; `reapply` and `comment` declare `table,json,yaml`).
- `mapError` adds a hint per code: `APPROVER_NOT_DESIGNATED` names `get data-review-policy`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_PENDING` names `approve` and `disagree`, `DUPLICATE_THREAD_SPANS_SOURCES` lists `context.data_source_model_ids`, and a code-less 403 names `DATA_REVIEW_APPROVE`. The vote verbs go through `mapVoteError` first (`DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_LIVE`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_YOURS` naming disagree and dismiss, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_PROPOSER` naming `withdraw`, and on dismiss a code-less 403 naming the admin/ops role), `stop-series` through `mapStopSeriesError` (`DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_SERIES_ALLOW`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_PENDING`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_LIVE`), and `allow-all` through `mapAllowAllError` (`DUPLICATE_DECISION_PENDING`: run it again, which skips the group as `pending_differs`). `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_REQUIRED` with operation `data_review_approve` gets `changeProposalRequiredHint`'s no-proposal-path hint, never `alert propose`.
- Reads live under `get duplicate-groups` and `describe duplicate-group` (see their rows above). `get duplicate-groups` validates `--origin`, `--state` and `--sender-response` against the route's values (hyphens read as underscores), `--file-id`, `--page` and `--limit` (at most 100) before any request (exit 2); `--state ended` also sends `include_ended`. `--row-id` cannot combine with `--page`/`--limit`: `duplicate_groups.Client.FindByRow` reads every page at limit 100 until the server sends no `next`, keeping the groups with a listed member on the row, and prints them as one page (List `total` = matches).
- `--sender` on `get duplicate-groups` and `describe duplicate-group` reads the source routes (`GET .../source-duplicate-groups[/{id}]`, `duplicate_groups.Client.ListSource`/`GetSource`) instead. The list refuses `--origin`, `--state`, `--sender-response` and `--row-id` with `--sender`, and `--cloud-source-id` (accepts `cloud-source/<id>`, sent as `data_source_model_id`) without it, both at exit 2 before any request; `--file-id`, `--include-ended`, `--page` and `--limit` are shared. `SourceNextActions` emits `qctl duplicate-group respond <uuid> --kind separate-rows|corrected-file-follows --comment <text> [--cloud-source-id <id>] --table-id <id>` (the source flag only when the caller's copies span several sources) and one `qctl duplicate-group withdraw-response <uuid> <response-id> --table-id <id>` per live response, or the `caller.reason` refusal when `caller.can_respond` is false. `Respond` and `WithdrawResponse` are in the domain client for those verbs.
- `allow`, `keep-one`, `not-duplicates`, `approve`, `disagree`, `dismiss`, `withdraw` and `stop-series` end their human output with `Next: qctl describe duplicate-group <uuid> --table-id <id>`; `mapVoteError`'s `DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_LIVE` hint, `mapStopSeriesError`'s hints and `mapError`'s hints for `DUPLICATE_GROUP_ENDED`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_PENDING` and `DUPLICATE_DECISION_NOT_LIVE` name the same command.
- `respond` and `withdraw-response` are the sender verbs, on the source routes (`POST .../source-duplicate-groups/{id}/responses` and `.../responses/{response_id}/withdraw`). `respond` checks `--kind` (`separate-rows`|`corrected-file-follows`, underscores accepted), `--comment` (required; `cmdutil.RequireCommentBody`, then sent trimmed — the backend caps no length, so not `ValidateCommentBody`) and `--cloud-source-id` (accepts `cloud-source/<id>`, sent as `data_source_model_id`); `withdraw-response` parses both UUIDs (`ParseResponseID`). All of that, and the table selector, exits 2 before any request. Neither reads the group first and neither confirms: a response changes no rows and is replaced by the next one or withdrawn. Output: `duplicate-group/<uuid> <responded|withdrew> <kind> [r: <uuid>] (cloud-source/<id>)`, a request-not-a-decision line after a `separate_rows` respond, then `Next: qctl describe duplicate-group <uuid> --sender --table-id <id>`; `-o json|yaml` print the server's response record and move those lines to stderr, `-o name` prints `duplicate-group/<uuid>`. `mapSourceError` hints `DUPLICATE_RESPONSE_NOT_LIVE`, `DUPLICATE_GROUP_ENDED` and `PERIOD_LOCKED` at that describe command, the 422 whose `loc` ends in `data_source_model_id` at `--cloud-source-id`, a 404 with `--cloud-source-id` at the source, and a code-less 403 at `DATASET_INGEST_UPLOAD`; it never adds a reviewer hint, and the source routes have no change review gate.
- `mark` and `retract` are the marker's verbs (`mark.go`). `mark` takes no argument; `--row-id` (IntSlice: 2..500 distinct positive ids, the server schema's bounds), `--mode` (`hold`|`flag`, case-insensitive, required, no default) and `--comment` (required; `cmdutil.ValidateCommentBody`, sent as given) are checked with the table selector before any request (exit 2). It sends one `POST .../duplicate-groups` (201) and does not read anything first. `retract <group-id>` reads the group and exits 2 after that one read when it is not person_marked, is `ended`, or has an effective decision of its own (`decision`; the hint says a decided mark cannot be retracted and another reviewer may propose a different decision) — the three refusals atlas's `_retract_state_refusal` applies to every caller; whether this caller may retract is left to the server. The retract prompt names a `pending_decision`, which the server withdraws as the group ends. Both confirm through `cmdutil.Confirm` and are not protected-context destructive. `mark` prints `duplicate-group/<uuid> marked N row(s) (mode: <m>, thread: <uuid>)`, one `member <id>: row <id> (cloud-source/<id>)` line per member, the hold's apply lines (and `warnUnapplied`), a line saying the marker cannot decide it and naming `retract`, then `Next: qctl describe duplicate-group ...`; `retract` prints `duplicate-group/<uuid> retracted (ended <time>, reason: <r>)`, the comment id, the rows queued for a reprocess (at most ten named), then the same `Next:`. `-o json|yaml` print the server response with those lines on stderr; `-o name` prints `duplicate-group/<uuid>`. `mapMarkError` hints `DUPLICATE_ROW_ALREADY_MARKED` at `retract` and `DUPLICATE_ROW_IN_UNDECIDED_GROUP` at `describe duplicate-group` (the concrete group when `context.group_ids` names one, `<group-id>` otherwise), names `context.row_ids` of the 422 `ERR_VALIDATION`, and a code-less 403 at SUBMIT_ALERT/SOURCE_DATA_WRITE; `mapRetractError` hints `DUPLICATE_GROUP_NOT_PERSON_MARKED`, `DUPLICATE_GROUP_NOT_MARKER`, `DUPLICATE_DECISION_EXISTS` (the decided hint, naming `describe duplicate-group`) and a code-less 403 (SUBMIT_ALERT, marker only), then defers to `mapError`. Both routes are change-review direct-only (`user_alert_submit`, `rows_quarantine_move` for a hold, `user_alert_retract`), so a policy refusal is `CHANGE_REVIEW_POLICY_DENIED` and passes through. `describe duplicate-group`'s NEXT ACTIONS adds `qctl duplicate-group retract <uuid> --table-id <id>` on a person_marked group when `caller.can_retract`. The rest of its NEXT ACTIONS follows the other `caller` flags (`NextActions`): with a `pending_decision`, `approve` (`can_approve` and not `caller_approved`), a waiting line with any counted disagreements (`caller_approved`) or the approve refusal, `disagree --reason <text>` (`can_disagree`), `withdraw` plus the decide lines as replacements (`can_withdraw`) and `dismiss --reason <text>` (`can_dismiss`); with nothing pending, the decide lines when `can_decide` on an ungoverned group or when `can_change` on one a `decision` or `carried_decision` governs (as a change proposal), else the refusal; and `stop-series` when `can_stop_for_series`.
- `describe alert`'s NEXT ACTIONS footer (`internal/alerts/nextactions.go` `duplicateGroupLines`) adds, for `repeated_key`, `identical_rows` and `person_marked_duplicate` alerts, `qctl describe duplicate-group <body.group_id> --table-id <id>` and a note that resolving the alert does not release its rows, plus, for `person_marked_duplicate`, `qctl duplicate-group retract <body.group_id> --table-id <id>`. A body without `group_id` (atlas stamps a load's `repeated_key` and `identical_rows` alerts only after the load commits, and a server older than atlas-70zyu never sends it) gets `qctl get duplicate-groups --table-id <id> --row-id <a held row's id>` and `retract <group-id>` instead (`FindDuplicateGroupCommand`). `alert resolve` on any of the four hold types, which offer no actions since atlas-ro6ns, refuses it (exit 2, `alerts.ValidateAction`) with the same group command, and `warning resolve` on a `repeated_key`, `identical_rows` or `person_marked_duplicate` warning does likewise (`warnings.ValidateOffered`). `alert retract` refuses a `person_marked_duplicate` alert (exit 2, before the DELETE) with the retract command for `body.group_id`, or with those two commands when the body names no group. A `duplicate_not_loaded` alert (the hold alert of a group whose reviewer kept another copy) gets the same group command (`--row-id <a not-loaded row's id>` in the fallback), a note that resolving it does not load the rows, and `qctl rows delete-quarantined --table-id <id> --signature <a not-loaded row's signature> --reason <why>` instead of the retract.

## pii-grant commands (temporary unmasked access to a table's personal data)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pii-grant create` | `internal/commands/pii_grant/create.go` | `pii_grants.NewClient` (`Create`; `datasets.Client.ListMembers` for `--to`) | `pii_grant_test.go` |
| `pii-grant revoke <grant-id>` | `internal/commands/pii_grant/revoke.go` | `pii_grants.NewClient` (`Revoke`) | `pii_grant_test.go` |

Notes:
- The verb half of the `pii-grant` resource; the read is `get pii-grants`. Every route needs `pii:grant` (admin and ops), so a non-admin caller gets the backend's 403 (exit 4).
- Both verbs select the table with `--table`/`--table-id` only, checked before `Bootstrap`. `create` also checks `--to`/`--to-id` (exactly one; a malformed UUID exits 2), a blank `--to` or `--reason`, and `--duration` through `pii_grants.ParseDuration`; `revoke` checks the grant id (positive integer, `pii-grant/<id>` stripped by the `pii-grant` entry in `cmdutil.refKinds`), `--reason` through `pii_grants.ParseRevokeReason`, and `--reason other` without a non-blank `--note`. Every one exits 2 with no request.
- `--to` resolves through the table's member listing (`pii_grants.ResolveGrantee`, case-insensitive); no match or more than one exits 2 before the POST. The grant's eligibility is the server's to decide: its 400s pass through untouched.
- Both prompt through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`; exit 2 without a terminal). `pii-grant revoke` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands`, like `table-member remove`; `create` is not, like `share table`.
- Both print through `printGrant` (`pii_grant.go`): the result line then `Next: qctl get pii-grants --table-id <id>`; `-o json|yaml` the grant (`output.WriteRaw`); `-o name` `pii-grant/<id>`.

## reveal commands (one masked personal-data value, unmasked and recorded)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reveal cell [table]` | `internal/commands/reveal/cell.go` | `pii_reveals.NewClient` (`Reveal`) | `reveal_test.go` |
| `reveal alert <alert-id>` | `internal/commands/reveal/issue.go` | `pii_reveals.NewClient` (`Reveal`; `alerts.Client.GetAlert` without a table selector) | `reveal_test.go` |
| `reveal warning <warning-id>` | `internal/commands/reveal/issue.go` | `pii_reveals.NewClient` (`Reveal`; `warnings.Client.GetWarning` without a table selector) | `reveal_test.go` |

Notes:
- Resource `pii-reveal`. All three POST one target to `datasets/{id}/pii-reveal` through `pii_reveals.Client.Reveal`, which encodes the body itself (`CellTarget`/`AlertTarget`/`WarningTarget`) because the generated union body has no setters. The route needs `pii:reveal` (admin, ops, read_only), so anyone else gets the backend's 403 (exit 4). There is no prompt and no `--yes`.
- `cell` takes the table as positional, `--table` or `--table-id` (`cmdutil.ValidateTableSelector` then `ResolveTableID`), plus required `--row-id` (positive, `PositiveIDFlag`) and `--field` (non-blank, sent verbatim); `--is-bad-data` targets the quarantined rows. `alert`/`warning` take a positive id (`alert/<id>`/`warning/<id>` stripped through `WithArgKinds`) and an optional table selector; without one, `issueKind.tableOf` reads the issue for its `dataset_id`. Every local check exits 2 before `Bootstrap`.
- Output (`send`, `reveal.go`): `-o json|yaml` the server's `{value, event_id}` (`output.WriteRaw`); otherwise the value alone on stdout — `terminal.Sanitize` for a cell, `markdown.PlainText` for a message — and `Reveal recorded as event N` on stderr. A null value prints nothing on stdout and `<target> is null; reveal recorded as event N` on stderr. `-o name` exits 2.
- A 429 comes back from `Reveal` as `*apierror.ThrottleError` with the header's Retry-After; `explainLimit` appends `Try again in N minutes` (or `later`) and the `qctl pii-grant create --table-id <id>` an admin can run, keeping exit 1.

## change-proposal commands (propose a resolution or a rule-param change, edit, submit or withdraw it, then approve, reject or request changes)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `change-proposal edit <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/edit.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Get`, `Edit`; + `alerts`/`warnings` for the target check; `dataset_rules.ParseParamEdit` for `--set-param`/`--unset-param`) | `change_proposal_test.go`, `rule_proposal_test.go` |
| `change-proposal submit <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/lifecycle.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Submit`) | `change_proposal_test.go` |
| `change-proposal withdraw <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/lifecycle.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Withdraw`) | `change_proposal_test.go` |
| `change-proposal approve <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/approve.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Get`, `Approve`; + `alerts` for the relayed-error explainer) | `approve_test.go`, `rule_proposal_test.go` |
| `change-proposal reject <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/decide.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`Reject`) | `approve_test.go` |
| `change-proposal request-changes <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/decide.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`RequestChanges`) | `approve_test.go` |
| `change-proposal rerun-dry-run <id>` | `internal/commands/change_proposal/lifecycle.go` | `change_proposals.NewClient` (`RerunDryRun`) | `rule_proposal_test.go` |

Notes:
- The author and approver verbs of the `change-proposal` resource; the reads are `get change-proposals` and `describe change-proposal`, `alert propose`/`warning propose` create an alert or warning resolution proposal, and `table-rule propose` a rule-param (`dataset_rule_patch`) one. All seven `change-proposal` verbs, both reads and the three `propose` verbs carry `catalog.For("change-proposal")`. None is on the static destructive list: a proposal changes no data until an approver approves it, and `approve` gates on the payload's action instead (below). Only `approve` prompts.
- `alert propose` and `warning propose` run their resolve twin's `resolveInputs` (the verb name in its messages comes from `cmd.Name()`), then `change_proposal.ReadProposeOptions` (`--draft`, `--also-alert`, `--also-warning`, `--depends-on`, a UUID sent as `depends_on_id`, and `--note`, trimmed and sent as `note`, blank exiting 2; any of them with `--dry-run` exits 2). `table-rule propose` reads the same options. The payload is `change_proposals.Document.Payload`: the resolution spec's own `ToRequest`, JSON-encoded with its number literals kept, so a proposal and a resolve can never send different bodies. `--action` is checked against the action's field contract before `Bootstrap` (`ActionDocument`), so unlike `alert resolve --action` an alert action that needs input exits 2. `CheckTarget` then reads the target issue and runs `ValidateAlert` (alert) or `ValidateAction`/`ValidateOffered` (warning, flag/file). `--dry-run` prints `alerts.ProposeSkeleton`/`ProposeFieldNamesSkeleton`/`warnings.ProposeSkeleton`, the resolve skeletons with a footer naming `propose -f`. `-f` runs through `pkgmanifest.Run` one proposal per document; `--also-*` with a file of several documents exits 2 before `Bootstrap`. Default is `submit: true`; `--draft` sends false.
- `change-proposal edit` checks the id, the flag matrix and the `-f` document (exactly one; `change_proposals.ParseDocument`) before `Bootstrap`, then GETs the proposal and sends its `version_id`, so a crossed edit is the server's 409 `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_VERSION_MISMATCH`. A document for another operation or target exits 2 with no PATCH. With neither `-f` nor `--action` it re-sends the stored payload and reads no target. A non-empty `masked_fields` in the proposal response refuses every edit that reuses stored payload values (exit 2, no PATCH), including incremental param edits; an explicit `-f`/`--action` replacement still works. `--also-alert`/`--also-warning` replace the list they name; `--clear-also-alerts`/`--clear-also-warnings` send an empty array for the matching list and conflict with its replacement flag (exit 2 before `Bootstrap`). A list neither replaced nor cleared is sent null (keep). `--depends-on <uuid>` sends `depends_on_id`, `--remove-depends-on` sends it null (clear), and with neither the key is omitted (keep); the two together exit 2. `--note`/`--remove-note` do the same for `note` (`change_proposals.EditClears` names which nil keys are sent as null), and a blank `--note` exits 2. `--submit` is sent only when given. `--set-param`/`--unset-param` (`dataset_rules.ParseParamEdit`) with `-f` or `--action` exit 2 before `Bootstrap`; after the GET, `editRuleParams` refuses them on a proposal of any other operation (exit 2), merges them into the STORED payload's params (`change_proposals.RuleParams`; an unknown `--unset-param` key exits 2) and sends `{params}`, and `editDocument` refuses `-f`/`--action` on a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal (exit 2). `writeParamsEdit` prints the stored-vs-edited `WriteParamsDiff` (a no-op is still sent) and, when the GET's review carries `rule_params_changed`, a `Note:` that the edit did not merge the rule's newer params.
- `rerun-dry-run` is a `newLifecycleCommand` like `submit`: one POST (`change_proposals.Client.RerunDryRun`, op `RerunChangeProposalDryRun`), no body, no prompt.
- `submit` and `withdraw` share `newLifecycleCommand`: a UUID check before `Bootstrap`, one POST, no body. `reject` and `request-changes` share `newDecisionCommand` (`decide.go`): the UUID and a non-blank `--reason` are checked before `Bootstrap` (exit 2, zero requests), then one POST of `{reason}`. Neither prompts; who may decide is the server's call (403 `CHANGE_REVIEW_POLICY_DENIED`, exit 4).
- `approve` GETs the proposal (`Detail`). A `pending_review` read prints `approvePreview` (the header with `DependsOnLine`, `change_proposals.WritePayload`, `WriteReview` and `WriteOverlaps`, the same sections `describe change-proposal` prints) to stderr, next to the prompt, runs `cmdutil.RequireNamedContext` when `cmdutil.IsDestructiveAction` holds for the payload's `action`, then `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`; exit 2 without a terminal, `ErrDeclined` exit 1 on a decline), and POSTs the read's `version_id` and `review.impact_digest`; a `pending_review` read with no `review` (a server that predates impact digests) exits 2 with no POST, since approving there would skip the gate and the prompt. A read in any other state POSTs an empty digest with no preview, gate or prompt: only `pending_review` is approvable, and the server's state refusal, with the fresh proposal, is the answer. It never refuses on validity or blockers itself, and never retries. For a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal the prompt names `its params patch`, and the preview's REVIEW carries the series re-approval prediction (`approve_reapproves_series_rules`). Outcomes: `executed` prints the lead line, `Response:` (the set fields of `execution_result.response`) and `Target <ref> now:` from `execution_result.target_state`, exit 0 — for a `dataset_rule_patch` proposal `change_proposals.RuleOutcome` (outcome, staged suggestion with a `qctl describe rule-suggestion <id> --table-id N` line, application, series rule approval) replaces `Response:`, and the target line is `Target rule/<uuid> now: <state>, params {...}` (`RuleTargetState`); `execution_failed` prints `Execution failed:` (`change_proposals.ExecutionError`), the partial line with `qctl get history --table-id N`, exit 1; a CHANGE_PROPOSAL_* 409 goes through `Explain` (exit 2); a 403 `CHANGE_REVIEW_POLICY_DENIED` (the approver check, before anything ran) passes through untouched; any other API error is passed through `resolveexplain.Alert` (its code-specific arm only when a GET of the target alert succeeds; the action note always) or `resolveexplain.Warning` (a rule PATCH's error through neither), keeps its exit code, and gains a line from a re-read of the proposal (`still pending_review (version N): nothing was applied`, or its new state); a transport error says whether it ran is unknown.
- Every verb wraps the client error in `change_proposals.Explain`, which appends a `qctl describe change-proposal` line per proposal the 409 context names (`active_change_proposal_ids` for `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE`, `change_proposal.id` for `VERSION_MISMATCH`/`STATE_CONFLICT`/`STALE`/`IMPACT_CHANGED`/`BLOCKED`, the last three with the fresh review's reasons or blockers from `context.review`, plus the step that clears a dependency's reason or blocker; all five with a `Now:` line of the proposal's state, version and `allowed_actions`) and keeps the exit code (409 → 2, 403 → 4, 422 → 2). All print `change-proposal/<uuid> <verb> (state: ..., version N)`, a `Next:` line per `allowed_actions` entry (`change_proposals.NextCommand`, the spelling `describe change-proposal`'s NEXT ACTIONS shares) and `Next: qctl describe change-proposal <uuid>` through `WriteResult` (`approve` through its pieces, with its execution lines between); `-o json|yaml` the proposal, `-o name` `change-proposal/<uuid>`.
