| `apply table` | `internal/commands/apply/dataset.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `dataset_test.go` |
| `apply backup-settings` | `internal/commands/apply/backup_settings.go` | `backup_settings.NewClient` | `backup_settings_test.go` |
| `apply destination` | `internal/commands/apply/destination.go` | `destinations.NewClient` | `destination_test.go` |
| `apply cloud-source` | `internal/commands/apply/cloud_source.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `cloud_source_test.go` |
| `apply linked-table` | `internal/commands/apply/linked_table.go` | `linked_tables.NewClient` | `linked_table_test.go` |
| (linked-table via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/linked_table.go` | `linked_tables.NewClient` | `linked_table_test.go` |
| (standard-field via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/concept.go` | `concepts.NewClient` | `concept_test.go`, `internal/concepts/manifest_test.go` |
| (table-series via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/table_series.go` | `table_series.NewClient` | `../root/table_series_write_test.go`, `../root/table_series_frequency_test.go`, `../root/table_series_schedule_test.go`, `internal/table_series/manifest_test.go`, `internal/table_series/frequency_test.go`, `internal/table_series/schedule_test.go` |
| (deliverable via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/deliverable.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`Prepare`, `Apply`, `ExportManifest`) | `../root/deliverable_manifest_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_sheets_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_sheet_inventory_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_binding_preflight_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_apply_failure_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_apply_warnings_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/plan_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/plan_bindings_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/plan_binding_checks_test.go`, `internal/deliverables/apply_bindings_test.go` |
| (table-kind via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/table_kind.go` | `dataset_kinds.NewClient` + `concepts.NewClient` | `table_kind_test.go`, `internal/dataset_kinds/manifest_test.go` |
| (rule via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/rules.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | `rules_test.go` |
| (table-rule via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/table_rules.go`, change review 403 lines in `rule_change_review.go`, duplicate guards in `table_rule_duplicates.go` / `table_rule_pending_duplicates.go`, `--wait` in `table_rule_wait.go` | `dataset_rules.NewClient`, `rule_suggestions.NewClient` (+ `dry_runs`, `notifications`, `alerts` under `--wait`) | `table_rules_test.go`, `rule_change_review_test.go`, `table_rule_duplicates_test.go`, `table_rule_pending_duplicates_test.go`, `table_rule_wait_test.go`, `table_rule_lock_test.go` |
| (table-schema via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/table_schema.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `table_schema_test.go` |
| (rule-review-policy via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/review_policies.go` | `review_policies.NewClient` | `review_policies_test.go` |
| (data-review-policy via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/review_policies.go` | `review_policies.NewClient` | `review_policies_test.go` |
| (change-review-policy via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/review_policies.go` | `review_policies.NewClient` | `change_review_policy_test.go` |
| (notification-preference via generic) | `internal/commands/apply/notification_preferences.go` | `notification_preferences.NewClient` | `notification_preferences_test.go` |

Notes:
- `apply -f` reads `kind` from manifest and dispatches via `genericApply()`.
- Supported kinds: `Table`, `TableKind`, `Destination`, `BackupSettings`, `CloudSource`, `StandardField` (alias `Concept`), `Rule`, `TableRule`, `TableSchema`, `TableSeries`, `Deliverable`, `RuleReviewPolicy`, `DataReviewPolicy`, `ChangeReviewPolicy`, `NotificationPreferences`, `LinkedTable`/`CompositeDataset`. (`DryRunJob` is **not** an `apply -f` kind — launch one with `qctl create dry-run-job`.)
- The accepted kinds come from `apply.genericKinds` (`internal/commands/apply/generic.go`), not a registry: `applyKinds()` sorts its keys for the `apply --help` "Valid kinds" line and for the command's `catalog.Dispatcher` annotation, so help text, dispatch behavior, and the catalog cannot disagree. `qctl diff -f` has its own, deliberately smaller table, `diff.diffKinds` (`internal/commands/diff/diff.go`; its `LinkedTable` entry is `diffLinkedTable` in `linked_table.go`). Both verbs look a manifest's `kind` up through `pkgmanifest.CanonicalKind` (`internal/pkg/manifest/manifest.go`), which maps the `CompositeDataset` alias to `LinkedTable`.
- `apply -f` dispatches `kind: LinkedTable` **or** `kind: CompositeDataset` to `applyLinkedTable` (backend "composite dataset"). The wire `kind` is always `CompositeDataset`.
- `apply linked-table` / `apply -f` accept `--validate-only` (server-side validation, persists nothing). `apply -f` refuses it with exit 2, before any document is applied, when the file holds a document of any other kind `genericKinds` dispatches (`refuseValidateOnlyBesideOtherKinds`, `internal/commands/apply/generic.go`); an unsupported kind or an envelope that will not parse is left to fail on its own line. Distinct from manifest `spec.dry_run` (`max_spine_rows`/`seed` for the dry run a real apply launches). The route answers `200 {valid, errors, warnings}` either way, so `linked_tables.Client.Validate` decodes the verdict: `valid: false` exits 2 listing each error as `<loc>: <msg> (<code>)` (`ValidateResult.Err`), warnings print to stderr as `Warning:` lines, and `-o json` prints the raw answer before exiting. A real apply's `400`/`409` whose `detail` is an issue list renders the same way (`linked_tables.IssuesError`, `internal/linked_tables/issues.go`) and keeps the status's exit code.
- A real `apply linked-table` / `apply -f <linked-table>` prints `linked-table/<name> created|configured|unchanged` (`configured` is the server's `revision_added`), a `revision :` line (release and state after the apply), a `dry run  :` line naming the composite dry run it launched and whether that run auto-enables the revision (`spec.desired_state`), and a `Next:` line: `qctl describe composite-dry-run-job <id>` when a dry run was launched, `qctl get composite-run-jobs --group-id <group>` when the revision is enabled, and `qctl enable linked-table --table <name> --revision <uuid>` for a draft that already passed a dry run (`--revision` because the revision an apply matched by spec hash can be older than the table's latest). The body is decoded into `linked_tables.ApplyResponse`; one with no `result` prints `linked-table/<name> applied`. Warnings print to stderr as `Warning:` lines under every `-o`.
- `apply linked-table` / `apply -f <linked-table>` take `--wait` and `--wait-timeout` (`cmdutil.AddWaitFlags`, registered on `apply` itself too, where it also covers a created table rule -- see the next note): after an apply that launched a composite dry run, `waitForLinkedTableDryRun` polls it through `composite_dry_runs.Client.Wait` (`internal/composite_dry_runs/wait.go`) until its state is neither `created` nor `running` and its `auto_enable_state` is not `pending`, prints each change on stderr, and prints `composite_dry_runs.WriteOutcome` — to stdout, or to stderr under `-o json`, whose stdout stays the server's answer — in place of the apply's own `Next:` line. `composite_dry_runs.Verdict` decides the exit code of a settled dry run: 0 for a `finished` dry run whose `auto_enable_state` is null or `enabled`, 1 otherwise. The wait runs under `cmdutil.StartWait` (`awaitLinkedTableDryRun`), which prints one stderr banner naming `qctl describe composite-dry-run-job <id>` before the progress lines; a timeout (`ErrWaitTimeout`, wrapped in `qerrors.NewTimedOut`) exits 124, and SIGINT/SIGTERM stop the poll through the wait context and exit 130/143. Either way the dry run carries on. An apply that launched no dry run returns at once, and kinds other than `LinkedTable` and `TableRule` under `apply -f` wait for nothing. `linkedTableWaitFlags` refuses `--wait` beside `--validate-only` and `--wait-timeout` without `--wait` with exit 2, from both commands' `RunE` before any document is applied.
- `apply -f <table-rule> --wait` waits for a create's validation dry run (`waitForCreatedTableRule`, `internal/commands/apply/table_rule_wait.go`). Only a 201 in `pending_validation` whose `validation_state` is `queued`, `in_flight` or absent starts a wait; a patch, a 202 and any other 201 print one `--wait: nothing to wait for: ...` line on stderr and keep exit 0. `dataset_rules.WaitForValidation` (`internal/dataset_rules/wait.go`, loop in `internal/pkg/pollwait`) polls the rule detail, reading the validation dry run only when the rule is `pending_validation` with `validation_state: failed`, and `ClassifyValidation` decides: `enabled`, or `pending_validation` with `pending_migration_model_id`, passed (exit 0); `validation_failed`, a killed or stopped run, `validation_state: none`, and `disabled`/`deleted`/`error` exit 1 through `ValidationVerdict`'s `Err*` sentinels; an `error` or `blocked` run with a nonempty summary is inconclusive (exit 1); a finished run that decided nothing, an `error` or `blocked` run without a summary, or a `version_id_mismatch` run, keeps waiting. A timeout (`ErrValidationWaitTimeout`, wrapped in `qerrors.NewTimedOut`) exits 124. The wait runs under `cmdutil.StartWait` (`awaitTableRuleValidation`): one stderr banner naming `dataset_rules.ValidationCheckCommand` (`qctl describe dry-run-job <id>` when the rule names its validation run, else the `get table-rules` line), and SIGINT/SIGTERM stop the poll through the wait context and exit 130/143. Progress goes to stderr when it changes, the outcome to stdout. A failure's reason is the caller's `validation_failed` notification for the rule (not read for a stopped or inconclusive run), then the run's `rule_load_errors`, `error_message`, `summary` and top exceptions, then `Next: qctl describe dry-run-job <id>`. A pass waiting on a draft migration names the unresolved `needs_review_migration` alert whose `migration_model_id` matches -- each listed alert is read, since the listing lacks the field -- with `alerts.YesFlag` on its `apply_migration` line, or falls back to the `get alerts --issue-type needs_review_migration` line.
- `apply linked-table` / `apply -f <linked-table>` retry a `429` from the composite apply route (the converge usually launches a dry run, so it spends the 3-concurrent exploratory cap; an enabled revision, or one that already has a current successful dry run, converges without launching one): up to 5 attempts honouring `Retry-After`, progress on **stderr** so `-o json` stdout stays parseable. `apply -f <table-rule>` retries a `429` too, printing to stdout (stderr under `-o json|yaml`), although atlas declares none on the instantiate route — see `internal/cmdutil/throttle.go`.
- A review policy *manifest* describes one scope — `spec.dataset_id` present = table override, absent = org policy — and PUT replaces omitted fields with backend defaults. Several such documents may share one file like any other kind.
- A `kind: NotificationPreferences` manifest (`applyNotificationPreferences`) carries `spec.email` (type to bool, required; a null value exits 2 before any request, naming the key, via `Manifest.EmailValues`; `{}` restores every role default) and an output-only `status` block. It GETs first and exits 2 before any PUT for a key the GET did not return (`notification_preferences.RequireKnownTypes`), then PUTs the `spec.email` entries that differ from the GET's `defaults` (`notification_preferences.WithoutDefaults`, which `set-preference` also goes through, matching cosmosview's `useNotificationEmailPreferences.ts`), so an entry equal to its default is never stored as an override nobody can see. It always PUTs -- the stored overrides are not readable, so there is nothing to diff against -- and prints `notification-preference/<metadata.name> configured`, or `unchanged` when the PUT answer's `preferences` equal the GET's. `get notification-preferences -o yaml` round-trips: its `spec.email` holds the entries whose effective value differs from the default (`notification_preferences.Overrides`).
- `.py` files rejected with hint to use `submit rules`.
- Multi-document YAML (`---` separators) works for **every** kind and for the typed subcommands, through one shared runner: `applyStream` (`internal/commands/apply/stream.go`) splits the file with `readManifestDocuments` (`documents.go`, whose splitter is `pkgmanifest.SplitDocuments` in `internal/pkg/manifest/documents.go` — shared so `qctl diff -f` counts documents by exactly this rule when it refuses a multi-document file), dispatches per document (so kinds may be mixed in one file), and applies them sequentially in file order. Handlers print their own success output; the runner adds a `<resource>/<name> failed: <err>` line per failure, a trailing `N documents: X succeeded[, Y failed][, Z skipped]` count, and an aggregated `*multiDocError` that wraps the first failure so its exit code survives. A document whose error carries a `*qerrors.Interrupted` (SIGINT or SIGTERM during its `--wait`) is the exception: `applyDocuments` returns that error at once, with no failure line and no summary, and tries no later document, so the run exits 130/143 even after an earlier failure. A **one-document** file gets none of the runner's prose and its handler's error verbatim — single-document behavior is unchanged. Under `-o json|yaml` the runner is **not** silent: whenever the file holds more than one document, its prose (failure lines, `--fail-fast` skips, the summary) goes to **stderr** instead of stdout, so stdout stays one document and the aggregated error still enumerates each failure's cause; a one-document file still prints no runner prose at all.
- Every `applyFunc` returns the document `-o json|yaml` prints for it, and writes its prose through `humanOut` (`cmdutil.ResultWriter`'s human writer: stdout under the table view, stderr under `-o json|yaml`). `applyDocuments` collects the documents and prints them after the run through `output.Printer`, which masks secrets: a one-document file prints its object, and a file of several prints one `output.List` of every document returned, in file order, also when another document failed or a `*qerrors.Interrupted` ended the run. This is kubectl's `apply -o json` shape. `Table`, `Destination` and `CloudSource` return `{name, status, message?, action, id}` and `BackupSettings` `{name, action, id}`; `StandardField` and `TableKind` the API object; `LinkedTable` the server's answer (with `valid: false` under `--validate-only`, beside the error); `Rule` the revision, read back after its PATCH, which answers only `{result}`; `TableRule` the rule (as `--wait` last read it) or the staged suggestion of a 202; the review policies the PUT answer; `TableSchema` `{name, id, action, migration, redirect_post_submit_to, alert_id}`, where `alert_id` is the review alert the redirect names.
- `--fail-fast` is **persistent** on `apply` (like `--yes`), so the typed subcommands see it. It stops at the first failure and prints `<resource>/<name> skipped (--fail-fast)` for every untried document, which the summary counts.
- An empty `-f` file (no documents, or only comments) is a bad-usage error, exit 2.
- Cross-document YAML anchors/aliases no longer resolve: documents are now parsed independently, so `&common` defined in document 1 and aliased with `*common` in document 2 fails document 2 with `yaml: unknown anchor 'common' referenced`. Before this branch only `kind: Rule` decoded the whole file through one shared `yaml.Decoder` and could see such an alias — every other kind read just the first document and never saw one — so this only ever worked for one kind, by accident. The new behavior is the spec-conformant one (kubectl and PyYAML both reject cross-document aliases); a manifest that relied on the old behavior migrates by repeating the block.
- `kind: Table` **update** reads the table first and builds the PATCH on top of the response, and since 2026-09-09 BOTH levels are seeded the same way — a JSON round trip of the read, so an omitted `spec` field keeps its stored value and a key nobody wired up keeps it too. The outer object is `datasets.patchRequestFromExisting`, the nested settings block `settingsPatchFromExisting` (both `internal/datasets/apply.go`), and the manifest's keys are overlaid on top of each. The settings overlay merges RAW JSON KEYS (`overlaySettings`), which is not interchangeable with unmarshalling the manifest onto the seeded struct: `encoding/json` reuses an existing map instead of replacing it, so that form MERGES the stored entries with the manifest's — `value_mapping: {"Y": "yes"}` over a stored `{"N": "no"}` would send both pairs, and `{}` would clear nothing. Slices are reset, so it looks correct on every list and fails on all twelve map fields; `TestEverySettingsManifestFieldIsOverlaid` and `TestPatchClearsSettingsFieldsWrittenEmpty` are the two tripwires. That is not cosmetic at either level: 24 of `api.DataSetPatchRequestSchema`'s 28 keys and 45 of `api.SettingsSchemaOptional`'s 47 carry no `omitempty`, so they serialise even when nil (the exceptions are `settings_model`, `migration_policy`, `data_loading_process` and `compression_type_of_backup_data` on the outer object, `schema_overrides` and `xls_date_mode` on the inner), the backend applies every key the raw body carries, and a null on a nullable column writes SQL NULL. Building the settings block from zero both wiped columns and put `id`/`version_id` on the wire as `0`, which the nested optimistic-concurrency check refused with a 409; the outer object's hand copy left four keys null (`model_labels`, `organization_id`, `destinations_list`, `whodunit_id`) and got away with it only because none of them is a nullable mapped column. Each base drops exactly one key: `schema_overrides` from the settings block, because PATCH writes that column with none of `kind: TableSchema`'s guards; `settings_model` from the outer one, because sending the block submits the nested version check and rewrites every settings column, so it goes on the wire only when the manifest asked for a settings edit. A manifest that asks for one over a read that carried no settings model is refused client-side (exit 1) rather than sent as `id: 0`, which the server would answer with either that mystery 409 or a 200 over a silently dropped edit.
- A `kind: Table` create uses `/datasets/quick` when the manifest has only `metadata.name`, `destination_id`, `database_name`, `table_name`, `backup_settings_id`, and optionally `data_loading_process`; the route uses server defaults for an omitted schema, migration policy, or loading process. Other new-table fields select full create, which requires all three of those fields before reading `/datasets/new`. An existing table accepts omitted schema, migration policy, and loading process as unchanged.
- A `kind: Table` update is planned once before it writes: the plan owns the field changes and the exact seeded PATCH body, so `qctl diff -f` and the apply confirmation cannot describe a body different from the one apply sends. Top-level edits render as `spec.<key>` and settings edits as `spec.settings_model.<key>`. An unchanged manifest is silent under `diff` (exit 0); apply prints `table/<name> unchanged` and sends no PATCH. A changed update prints the changes and confirms unless `--yes` is set; a create remains non-interactive. `-o json|yaml` requires `--yes` for a changed update so confirmation prose cannot corrupt stdout.
- `kind: Deliverable` (`applyDeliverable`) is identified by `metadata.slug`, which `pkgmanifest` requires in place of `metadata.name` as it does for `StandardField` and `TableKind`, and reads `spec.template` relative to the directory of the `-f` file (`manifestDoc.Path`). It prints `deliverable/<slug> created|updated|unchanged`, `updated` for any write to an existing deliverable including a new release or a binding write, then `deliverables.FormatApplyResult`: the created release, the attached template, the created release's anchor report, the `binding writes:` count line, the anchor report of each re-anchoring sheet PATCH, and the plan's `Note:` lines followed by the confirmation notes, which `unchanged` prints too. A plan with `Plan.Removals`, `Plan.MayRemove` or `Plan.MayRemoveCells` goes through `confirmBindingRemovals` first: `cmdutil.RequireNamedContext`, then `refuseUnconfirmedStructuredOutput`, then `--yes` or a TTY y/N prompt listing the removals and `Plan.WriteRequests` (no TTY and no `--yes` exits 2; declining the y/N prompt skips the document, while declining `RequireNamedContext`'s own prompt returns `cmdutil.ErrDeclined`, a failed document, exit 1). After the `unchanged` line, or else before `Client.Apply`, `applyDeliverable` writes `Plan.WriteRowFilterCounts` (the row filter counts and `Warning:` lines `Prepare` recorded) on `humanOut`, unless that prompt already listed them through `Plan.WriteRequests`. `Client.Apply` sends `spec.sheets` and `spec.cells` binding writes (`plan_bindings.go` and `plan_cells.go` plan them, `apply_bindings.go` sends them) one at a time, re-planning them after the revision POST and after a sheet POST or a re-anchoring sheet PATCH. `refuseBindingRequests` refuses, with exit 2 and before any binding write, a binding write to a release that is not a draft, a write other than a DELETE to a release with no template or naming a sheet its template inventory lacks (`templateSheets`; it skips a tree with no inventory, and the copy source or empty tree a release created with `spec.template` plans from, leaving that check to the re-plan after the revision POST), and the DELETE of a sheet an unmanaged cell binding mirrors. Managed mirrors are deleted before sheet writes and recreated afterward when still wanted. When `Client.Apply` returns a result with its error, which it does once the deliverable, the release or a binding was written, `applyDeliverable` prints `FormatApplyResult` of it on `humanOut` before returning the error; a write error past the first request and the template steps comes from `Plan.stopped`, naming the step, the sheet, column or cell of a binding write, and how many completed. `-o json|yaml` print `ApplyResult.Manifest`, which exports the deliverable and the release `spec.release` names from the same post-write revision tree as the confirmation notes (`deliverables.FromAPI` after a failed write or read-back). When nothing was sent, `applyDeliverable` calls `Client.ExportManifest` only under `-o json|yaml` (otherwise it returns `deliverables.FromAPI` without reading the tree).
- `env.go` handles `${VAR}` expansion in manifests.
- Rule apply uses **smart diff** (not simple create/update) — see `docs/patterns.md`.
- TableRule apply has two modes: **patch** (has `metadata.id`) and **instantiate** (has `spec.rule_revision_id` or `spec.rule`). The instantiate identifier may be a full UUID, a SHORT-ID or a slug; `spec.rule` + `spec.release` mirror `kind: DryRunJob`. `internal/commands/apply/table_rules.go` resolves it in place before building the request. **Patch mode accepts the same identifiers**, where they mean a rebind to another revision of the rule's own family.
- `--yes`/`-y` is a **persistent** flag on `apply`, so every subcommand (`table`, `backup-settings`, `destination`, `cloud-source`, `linked-table`) accepts it too — matching what `apply --help` advertises. It skips confirmation prompts; the flows that prompt include a changed `kind: Table` update (through generic apply or the typed `apply table` subcommand), the TableRule instantiate/patch flow, `kind: TableSchema` (`applyTableSchema` confirms the three side effects of a schema change unless `--yes` is given), and a changed `kind: CloudSource` update (through generic apply or the typed `apply cloud-source` subcommand, `approveCloudSourceUpdate`). On the backup-settings, destination and linked-table typed subcommands it remains an accepted no-op.
- TableRule manifests don't require `metadata.name`/`metadata.id` for instantiate (`metadata: {}` is valid); instantiate lets Atlas fill a required field from its unique table concept match and identity-maps other inputs plus undeclared required role fields when `spec.column_mapping` is omitted or empty. It validates explicit mappings against the input/validates/corrects/enriches union minus wildcard and optional fields, allowing produced declarations and unique concept matches to supply missing bindings. `spec.column` (instantiate only) is shorthand for a rule with exactly one distinct required field across that union — `column: booking_number` expands to `column_mapping: {value: booking_number}`; mutually exclusive with `column_mapping` and rejected in patch mode.
- `spec.upgradedFrom` is the instantiate-only, apply-only one-click upgrade key. It accepts a dataset-rule UUID or a server-scoped `r-*` alias, resolves the alias, reads the predecessor, and refuses a cross-table or cross-family pair before POSTing `upgraded_from_dataset_rule_id`. It is deliberately absent from the round-trip `TableRuleSpec`, so re-applying `get table-rule -o yaml` cannot replay a create-time retirement side effect, and `qctl diff -f` remains patch-only rather than pretending to preview that side effect. Apply reports the server outcome instead of promising immediate retirement: an enabled replacement retired the predecessor; a `pending_validation` replacement has not yet done so; a staged replacement has created neither rule nor retirement yet. Atlas retires the predecessor when the replacement becomes enabled, including after a produced-column migration, transfers matching output ownership, and puts the replacement in the predecessor's rule position.
- `spec.produced_fields` is the enrichment key: a list of `{rule_field_name, dataset_field_name, field_schema}` sent on both carriers (instantiate and patch). On patch, `dataset_rules.PreparePatchRevisionFields` (`internal/dataset_rules/produced_fields.go`) is shared by `qctl apply -f` and `qctl diff -f`: it resolves an omitted `field_schema` from the governing revision's `enriched_field_schemas` and validates `spec.params` against that revision's `param_schema`, fetching the revision whenever `NeedsRuleRevision` says either field needs it. Both verbs select the revision through `dataset_rules.PatchRevisionID`, so a rebind is judged against its target and the preview cannot disagree with apply. Every rejection **of the manifest** is exit 2 before the call; the one arm that is not is the missing-revision guard, a plain error reporting a programming mistake no manifest can provoke. On patch a second pass follows, `dataset_rules.ReconcileProducedFieldsWithLive`, called by apply and diff alike: a value equal to the rule's live declarations is dropped from the body (sending it re-merges into the table's draft migration and opens one via `ensure_draft`), a value adding a column is sent, and a drop or retype without an add is sent only for a **paused** (`disabled`) rule, whose backend path replaces declarations directly. For every other state that drop or retype is exit 2, because a drop of an active column read by a linked table gets a 409 at PATCH time, while another drop or retype can pass its dry run but fail promotion. It is a pointer-to-slice on the manifest so that an empty list can be told from an absent key: `produced_fields: []` clears a paused rule's declarations, while omission changes nothing. `get table-rule -o yaml` round-trips the declarations minus the server-owned `generated_by`, id and timestamps (`ProducedFieldsFromResponse`), and `ResolveProducedFields` drops a `generated_by` the manifest states before either body is built, so neither carrier ever sends one.
- TableRule **patch** sends a minimal merge-patch body (only manifest-present keys; built by `dataset_rules.BuildPatchMap`). A 202 has two forms and the response says which: `is_revalidation_candidate: true` means the request changed the rule's revision, column mapping or produced fields (or `params` on a rule in `error`) and is being validated on a candidate snapshot — only `instance_name` reached the live rule; otherwise a draft migration staged `params`/`state` and applied the rest. `dataset_rules.SplitPatchOutcome` branches on that flag rather than on a static request-key table, because `column_mapping_dict` is a trigger and would otherwise be mis-reported as applied. Both are successes with resolution breadcrumbs, exit 0. A **200** gets breadcrumbs too when the response carries `pending_migration_model_id` — the rule's produced columns are sitting in an open draft migration — which is how a **paused** rule's produced-field edit comes back: no candidate can be opened for a rule in `NON_PROMOTABLE_RULE_STATES`, so the patch lands directly and opens that draft itself. `renderPendingMigrationFollowUp` reads the draft id, state and validation evidence off the answer: a `pending_validation` rule needs `validation_state: passed` and `validation_blocks_enable: false` before it advises applying the migration and checking the state; otherwise it advises waiting for validation first. Applying the migration does **not** resume a paused rule; it needs passing validation and live columns before re-applying `spec.state: enabled`.
- TableRule **instantiate** can also return 202: with a draft migration open and a schema-touching rule (produced fields, or a CAF type rewrite), the backend stages the entire instantiation as a suggestion and creates no rule. `dataset_rules.InstantiateRule` returns `*InstantiateOutcome` (`Created` on 201, `Staged` on 202), rendered by `renderStagedInstantiate` — a success with breadcrumbs, exit 0, no `applied now` line.
- `qctl diff -f <file>` (top-level verb, `internal/commands/diff/diff.go` + `concept.go` + `table.go` + `table_rule.go` + `table_schema.go` + `table_series.go` + `deliverable.go` + `linked_table.go`, tests `diff_test.go`, `concept_test.go`, `table_test.go`, `linked_table_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_manifest_test.go` and `../root/table_series_schedule_test.go`) previews a Concept create/update, a Table create/update, a TableRule patch, a TableSchema apply, a TableSeries apply, a Deliverable apply or a LinkedTable apply without applying: field-level old → new plus the exact request body, or, for TableSeries and Deliverable, every request apply would send, in order. For `TableSeries`, `diffTableSeries` runs the same `table_series.Client.Prepare` apply runs; when `Plan.SchedulePreviewable` (a schedule PATCH is planned against an existing series whose frequency does not change) it first POSTs `Plan.ScheduleRequest`'s body to `/table-series/{id}/deadline-schedule/preview` with the stored read's `period_key` (`Client.PreviewDeadlineSchedule`), before printing anything, so a refused preview leaves stdout empty (422 exits 2). It then prints `Plan.WriteRequests` and `table_series.WriteSchedulePreview` (`schedule_display.go`): a `Deadline schedule for period <key> (<tz>), stored → after apply:` header and one `<step> <column>: old → new` line per changed cell of `ScheduleSteps`/`ScheduleEscalations`, or nothing when no cell changes. A create or a frequency change with a schedule PATCH is not previewed and ends with a `Note:` line. Table changes come from the same `datasets.TableApplyPlan` apply executes; a create shows `<absent>` old values and only makes the reads needed to prepare its POST, while an update reports only manifest-authored fields and individual `settings_model` sub-keys. A rebind named by `spec.rule` + `spec.release` (or a slug/SHORT-ID in `spec.rule_revision_id`) is resolved against the rule-revision catalog first, via the same `dataset_rules.ResolvePatchRebind` apply uses, so the preview matches what apply would send — which is why diff needs auth even for a manifest it ends up rejecting. Exit 0 = no differences (silent), exit 1 = differences (silent sentinel `errors.ErrDiffDifferences`) or a runtime failure with stderr output (an unreadable file, a 5xx); kinds outside `diff.diffKinds`, instantiate mode, a `.py` file, a **multi-document file**, a manifest that will not parse, a wrong `apiVersion` and a patch with no patchable fields are all rejected with exit 2, as is every verdict `apply -f` reaches on a patch manifest before its request. Manifest-only checks (`spec.column` in patch mode, a missing `spec.dataset_id`, a server-set `spec.state` — `error`, `pending_validation` or `validation_failed`) live in shared `dataset_rules.ValidatePatchManifest`; revision-dependent checks (non-empty `spec.produced_fields` and `spec.params`) live in shared `dataset_rules.PreparePatchRevisionFields`, so a preview cannot bless a manifest apply then refuses. The multi-document refusal (`genericDiff`, above the dispatch table, counting with `pkgmanifest.SplitDocuments` — the same splitter `apply -f` uses, so the two verbs cannot disagree about how many documents a file holds) is what stops diff previewing document 1 of a file `apply -f` would apply in full: every `diffFunc` re-reads the file and strict-unmarshals it, and `pkgmanifest.StrictUnmarshal` decodes exactly one document. For `TableSchema` it prints the per-field `property: old → new` lines and the exact request body, sharing `datasets.BuildSchemaOverrides` and `datasets.SchemaPatchBody` with apply so a document's preview cannot drift from the request apply would send for it — and, because of that refusal, so can a whole file's. For `LinkedTable`, `diffLinkedTable` first POSTs the manifest to the apply route with `validate_only=true` (`linked_tables.Client.Validate`, whose `valid: false` exits 2 through `ValidateResult.Err` with the warnings on stderr, as `apply linked-table --validate-only` does), then compares it (`linked_tables.CreateChanges`/`UpdateChanges` in `internal/linked_tables/diff.go`) with the latest spec revision of the table named exactly `metadata.name` — a baseline, not the revision apply matches, since apply looks the spec hash up among all of them — finding that table and each source by a `name` filter on `GET /datasets`, never through `ResolveID`, which reads an all-digit name as an id. For `Deliverable`, `diffDeliverable` runs the same `deliverables.Client.Prepare` apply runs and prints `Plan.WriteRequests`: every request in order, the PATCH's `field: old → new` lines and its note that basic fields take effect immediately, then the binding writes `spec.sheets` and `spec.cells` plan (cell DELETEs, sheet DELETEs, then per sheet by name its POST or PATCH and its column DELETEs, PATCHes and POSTs, then cell PATCHes and POSTs by address), then `Plan.WriteRowFilterCounts` (`plan_row_filters.go`: a count per row filter a sheet or cell save sends or keeps, then `Warning:` lines), then the plan's `Note:` lines and the confirmation notes. Ids the server will assign print as `<created-deliverable-id>`, `<uploaded-template-id>`, `<created-revision-id>` and `<created-sheet-binding-id>`, the bindings of a copied release as `<copied-sheet-binding-id>`, `<copied-column-binding-id>` and `<copied-cell-binding-id>`, and diff never uploads `spec.template`. Registered in `root.go`.

## submit commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `submit rules -f <files>` | `internal/commands/submit/rules.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | `rules_test.go` |
| `submit table-kinds -f <files>` | `internal/commands/submit/table_kinds.go` | `dataset_kinds.NewClient` | `table_kinds_test.go` |

Notes:
- `-f` is `StringArrayVar` (multiple files).
- `submit rules`: Only `.py` files accepted; `-f <dir>` expands to the `.py` files directly inside it other than `__init__.py` (`expandRulePaths`, `dirRuleFiles`), a missing path exits 1 before any request, and stray positionals exit 2 naming `-f` (`rulesArgs`). The client caps each file at 500,000 Unicode code points; server preflight also caps it at 500,000 UTF-8 bytes. Flags: `--force`, `--yes`, `--check`.
  - `--check` POSTs the body submit builds (`submitRequest`, shared) to `rule-revisions/check` (`rule_versions.Client.CheckRuleVersion`) and never prompts. Accept: the server's summary line, a `NAME VERSION ACTION` table (plus `REASON` when any rule is skipped; `updated` prints as `updated in place`), then `Check only: nothing was submitted.`, exit 0. A 422 reads `submit would reject this file: …` and goes through `renderSubmitError`, so it exits 2 and `-v` adds the full diff exactly as for submit. 404/405 — a server without the route; an older one answers 405 because the path matches `rule-revisions/{rule_revision_id}`, which has no POST — exits 5 (`ExitAPIIncompatible`) and never falls back to submit. Any other error reads `rule check failed`. Like submit, it stops at the first file that fails, and each file is checked against the catalog as it stands, not as the earlier files would leave it.
  - Revisions that land in `draft` (org policy requires review) get a breadcrumb: `rule/<name>@<release> submitted (state: draft — review required before enable)` + `Next: qctl review request <name> --release <release>`, and a file that added any draft revision ends with one `New to the review flow? Run: qctl guide request-review` line. Best-effort — a state-fetch failure degrades to plain output, never a failed submit.
  - `-v` sends `verbose: true` to the backend; a 422 `ERR_RULE_VERSION_CONFLICT` with `detail.diff` then renders the full code diff (`Full diff:` section) instead of only the compact diff embedded in the backend message. A rejection with a non-empty diff appends the hint `Bump 'release' in RuleMetadata to create a new revision.` Other rejections do not get that hint.
  - `--force` skips (never overwrites) `enabled`/`disabled` revisions whose code changed; skipped rules are absent from the response, so the output carries a standing note saying so.
- `submit table-kinds`: Only `.toml`, `.yaml`, `.yml` files accepted. The client caps each file at 500,000 Unicode code points. Flags: `--yes`.
- Under `-o json|yaml`, `submit rules` prints the server's `{message, added, not_changed}` (`[name, release]` pairs, `[]` when empty, as atlas sends) or, under `--check`, `{message, rules}`; it has no name view. `submit table-kinds` prints `{created, table_kind}` (`submittedTableKind`), `table_kind` being `dataset_kinds.FromAPI`'s manifest, and `table-kind/<uuid>` under `-o name`. A declined file contributes no document, and the List of the files that went through is written even when a later file fails.

| `delete cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/delete/cloud_source.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `cloud_source_test.go` |

| `delete table-kind <slug-or-name-or-id> [--untag-tables]` | `internal/commands/delete/table_kind.go` (+ `table_kind_untag.go` for the cascade) | `dataset_kinds.NewClient` + `datasets.NewClient` | `table_kind_test.go` |
| `delete standard-field <ref>` | `internal/commands/delete/concept.go` | `concepts.NewClient` | `concept_test.go` |
