Notes:
- `delete cloud-source` is soft; `qctl undelete cloud-source` attempts recovery, but current servers reject that request with 404. It needs the admin/ops-only `source:delete` permission, reads `GET .../tiny` for the exact name the server's `?name=` gate requires, and resolves every unresolved alert on the source as a side effect.
- `delete rules -f` takes Python files (not YAML), calls `UnsubmitRuleVersion`. Under `-o json|yaml` it prints the server's `{message, deleted, not_found, skipped}`, each list `[]` when empty, and `rule/<revision-id>` per deleted revision under `-o name`; several files print one List.
- `delete rule-review-policy` / `delete data-review-policy`: a table selector (`--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`) is **required** — only table-level overrides can be deleted (the org-level policy cannot). Both share `resolvePolicyTableFlag` in `delete/rule_review_policy.go`, which wraps `cmdutil.OptionalTableID` only to say *why* the table cannot be omitted; the absent-selector error exits 2 (`ExitBadUsage`).
- `delete change-review-policy` shares that selector handling; the server answers `{deleted: false}` for a table with no override, which exits 3.

## rows commands (the write half of the data plane, and one read)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rows delete-quarantined [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/delete.go` | `datasets` / `cloud_sources` / `stored_items` | `delete_test.go` |
| `rows send-to-quarantine [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/quarantine.go` | `datasets.Client.MoveRowsToQuarantine` (`--row-id`) / `MoveAllRowsToQuarantine` (`--all`) | `quarantine_test.go` |
| `rows reprocess-clean [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/quarantine.go` | `datasets.Client.MoveRowsToQuarantine` (`--row-id`) / `MoveAllRowsToQuarantine` (`--all`) | `quarantine_test.go` |
| `rows reprocess-quarantined [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/reprocess.go` | `datasets` / `cloud_sources` | `reprocess_test.go` |
| `rows hold [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/hold.go` | `datasets` / `cloud_sources` | `hold_test.go` |
| `rows release [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/hold.go` (reporter: `release_result.go`) | `datasets` / `cloud_sources` | `hold_test.go`, `release_result_test.go` |
| `rows reset-hold [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/hold.go` | `datasets` / `cloud_sources` | `hold_test.go` |
| `rows search-replace [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/search_replace.go` | `datasets.Client.SearchAndReplace` / `cloud_sources` | `search_replace_test.go` |
| `rows set-cell [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/set_cell.go` | `datasets.Client.SetCell` | `set_cell_test.go` |
| `rows check-unique [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/check_unique.go` | `datasets.Client.CheckColumnUniqueness` | `check_unique_test.go` |
| `rows ignore-rule [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/ignore.go` | `rule_row_ignores.Client.Create` (+ `dataset_rules.ListAllDatasetRules` for `--rule`) | `ignore_test.go` |
| `rows unignore-rule [table]` | `internal/commands/rows/ignore.go` | `rule_row_ignores.Client.List` + `UnIgnore` (+ `dataset_rules.ListAllDatasetRules` for `--rule`) | `ignore_test.go` |

Notes:
- The source-scoped delete, reprocess and hold routes accept a series source with an explicit period table. `scope.go` retains the selected table as `dataset_id` for all four source routes; ordinary sources omit it. `series_scope_test.go` covers both source selectors, table selectors, the template period and non-member refusals.
- `rows send-to-quarantine` and `rows reprocess-clean` (both selectors) carry `datasets.QuarantineMoveResult` through `quarantine.go` to `quarantine_result.go`: the headline reports `moved`, skipped `already_in_quarantine` rows are reported beside it (on stdout under the table view), and nonzero `not_found` counts warn on stderr. A wholly missing explicit-ID request exits 3 through the error path without a success headline; other successful responses, including zero-move no-ops and empty `--all`, exit 0. Older responses without counts say the server did not return a moved count. Reprocessing output confirms movement only, explains validation/table-policy retention, and offers `rows reprocess-quarantined <table> --row-id <row-id>` for skipped rows without inventing their IDs. Both verbs take an optional `--comment` (`moveCommentFlags` in `quarantine.go`), sent as the request's `comment` through `datasets.MoveComment` on `--row-id` and as a bare `comment` on `--all`; without it the request is unchanged. `--visible-to-sender` sends `comment_is_internal: false` and is refused with exit 2 without `--comment`, with `--all` or with `--shared`, as are a blank comment, one over 10000 characters and more than 500 `--row-id` values with one. `reportMoveComment` in `quarantine_result.go` prints the returned `comment_thread_id` with a `qctl describe comment-thread <id> --table-id <t>` follow-up (no follow-up on `--shared`), or the `--all` form's `notified_user_count`, and warns on stderr when a commented move returns neither. Tests: `internal/commands/rows/quarantine_test.go`, `internal/commands/rows/quarantine_result_test.go` and `internal/datasets/remediation_test.go`.
- `get data`, `download data` and `rows` accept `--shared` for tables shared from another organization (default: owned tables). Shared names resolve through `GetThirdPartyDatasets`; data reads, exports, row lifecycle/edit/uniqueness calls and prompt counts use the generated third-party twins. Shared source narrowing requires `--cloud-source-id` and sends the selected table as `dataset_id`; file deletes read the file's table through `stored_items.Client.GetThirdPartyStoredItem` and use the third-party stored-item route. `--cloud-source` names and rule-ignore writes are refused for third-party rows. Third-party reads/exports refuse `--rule` (use `--dataset-rule-ids`); reads also refuse `--include-comment-threads` and `--include-issues`. Unsupported combinations exit 2 locally.
- `qctl rows *`: the write half of the data plane, plus one read. Twelve of the thirteen verbs write; `check-unique` reads. Scope is the table positional plus optional `--cloud-source`/`--cloud-source-id` or `--file-id`; exactly one selector (`--row-id`, `--signature`, `--status`, `--all`) is required and every unsupported (verb, scope, selector) triple is refused client-side with exit 2 — the matrix lives in the `support` values at the top of each command file. Two verbs have NO selector — `search-replace`, whose `--search` term names the rows, and `check-unique`, whose `--column` list is the whole question — and both say so in the same matrix, with an empty (non-nil) selector list per supported scope, so `checkScopeFlags` still reads their scopes from there; a nil list is how a row spells an unsupported scope, which is why `search-replace`'s `file` entry stays nil and `--file-id` is refused as an unknown flag, and why `check-unique` (whose route takes no `data_source_model_id` at all) leaves both `cloud` and `file` nil and refuses all three scope flags that way. The delete verbs take signatures only. `hold`, `release` and `reset-hold` are one route (`update-keep-in-quarantine`) separated by the `quarantine_hold` enum — `hold`/`release`/`undecided` — so they share one `support` row; `send-to-quarantine` and `reprocess-clean` likewise share one `support` row (`moveSupport`), separated by `hold` vs `undecided` on both of their routes: `move-rows-to-quarantine` for `--row-id`, `move-all-rows-to-quarantine` for `--all`. `qctl delete rows` is redirected here by `runDeleteSubcommand` in `internal/commands/delete/delete.go`.
- Both delete verbs require `--reason`, sent as the wire field `comment` on all ten delete routes (`delete-rows` and `delete-all-bad-rows`, first- and third-party). `deleteReason` (`internal/commands/rows/delete.go`) runs as a `resolveTarget` local check and refuses a missing, blank or over-10000-character value with exit 2 before any request.
- Ten of the twelve writes declare `table,json,yaml` and print their result lines through `cmdutil.ResultWriter`, which moves them to stderr under `-o json|yaml`. The five that report through `reportResult` (`internal/commands/rows/confirm.go`) — both deletes, `reprocess-quarantined`, `hold` and `reset-hold` — print `{affected_rows}`, null when no count came back; `release`, through `reportRelease` (`internal/commands/rows/release_result.go`), prints `{affected_rows, skipped_rows, skipped_row_ids, released_row_ids, approvals}`, the last four null unless the server took the approval path; the two move verbs, through `reportMoveResult` (`internal/commands/rows/quarantine_result.go`), the server's `{moved, already_in_quarantine, not_found}`; `search-replace` the route's `{fields_affected, matched_rows, replaced_rows, skipped_rows}`, the preview's under `--dry-run` or when nothing matches and the write's otherwise; and `ignore-rule` the created ignore records. A selector names no rows, so none of them has a name view. `set-cell` and `unignore-rule` declare `table` alone, so `-o json|yaml|name` exits 2 before any request. `rows check-unique` is the thirteenth verb and the group's only read, so it declares `table,json,yaml` through `cmdutil.WithFormats` (the inventory row is in `internal/commands/root/output_format_test.go`): `-o json`/`-o yaml` emit the route's raw response and everything else gets the verdict lines, while `-o name` is exit 2 because a yes/no carries no id or name and an empty line at exit 0 would read as "unique".
- `rows check-unique` is also the only verb in the group whose EXIT CODE is an answer rather than a status: 0 unique, 1 not unique, on the `qctl diff` contract, through `qerrors.NewSilent` so the reason stays on stdout and stderr stays empty. A real failure exits with its own typed error and a message, which is what keeps a 504 — the route runs under a 30-second server statement timeout — distinguishable from a verdict that also exits 1.
- The loop the two edit verbs and `check-unique` make with `get value-recommendations` — find the rows, preview then replace, fix one cell, pick a value, check the key, see what the write set in motion — is `qctl guide edit-data` (`internal/commands/guide/topics/edit-data.md`); the data page (`internal/resources/pages/data.md`) keeps `guide: inspect-data` and lists both guides in SEE ALSO.
- File row deletes read the file's table during `hydrateScope` (`validateFileTable`) and compare it with the resolved table before confirmation (including `--yes`): owned scope through `stored_items.Client.GetStoredItem`, `--shared` through `stored_items.Client.GetThirdPartyStoredItem` (`GET /third-party/stored-items/{id}`, atlas-ej8r). A mismatch exits 2, and a failed read preserves its error exit code without a delete; a shared 404 adds that a server without the shared file read also answers 404. Both delete routes then send the selected table as `dataset_id`, which atlas refuses with 404 if the file belongs to another table. `file_scope_test.go` covers the matching and mismatched tables for both populations, the read failures and the delete-time refusal.
- Shared machinery lives beside the verbs: `rows.go` (group + the optional table positional), `scope.go` (table/cloud-source/file scope resolution), `selector.go` (the `selectorKind` vocabulary, the exactly-one rule and the per-verb `--row-id` and `--all` populations), `confirm.go` (the destructive-action prompt, `sizedStatusPhrase` and `reportResult`), `release_result.go` (`reportRelease`, `release`'s result).
- Confirmation counts follow the selector rather than the verb name. Explicit id/signature lists are exact. At table scope, `--status passed_validation`, `--status on_hold`, `--status approved`, `--status waiting_for_approval` and `--status held_for_approval` use the count-by-status endpoint and state `N row(s)`, because each bulk selector repeats the counter's status predicate and its `is_deleted_ = FALSE` (`sizeableStatus`, `sizedStatusPhrase`); `reprocess-quarantined --status` suppresses those numbers because that write additionally skips already-queued rows, making the table-wide count only a ceiling. `failed_validation` remains unsized because its bulk selector is `passes_validation_ IS NOT TRUE`, which includes NULL, while the strict count bucket does not. `approved` (then `passed_manual_review`) was in that position too until atlas #783 made its bulk selector `keep_in_quarantine_ IS FALSE`. Narrower scopes and every `--all` form are also unsized. `--yes` never spends a request whose only purpose was to populate a skipped prompt.
- Hold semantics follow atlas #1123/#1125/#1128. `keep_in_quarantine_` records only a person's decision (`on_hold` = TRUE, `approved` = FALSE, NULL undecided); a row the table keeps anyway is `held_for_approval`, which the server derives from the table's `quarantine_rows_until_approved`, its effective data-review policy and the row's `suspected_duplicate_`, and never stores — unless the row is undecided in a non-closed submitted data review, which makes it `waiting_for_approval` (atlas #1522) in the count, the `--status` bucket and `get data`'s Row State. On a table whose effective data-review policy requires review or whose `quarantine_rows_until_approved` is on, `rows release` needs `DATA_REVIEW_APPROVE` and records the caller's approval instead of releasing outright: the server approves each live matching row the caller neither submitted nor edited, skips the rest, releases a row only once it passes the release gate, and returns the others to undecided. `reportRelease` prints the rows released, short of approvals (`1 of 2 approvals`) and approved but still held, plus the skipped count, reason and row IDs (up to ten in prose, all in JSON/YAML), and exits 0 for any 200, including all-skipped results. `hold` and `reset-hold` never take that path. `set-cell` and `search-replace` write `NULLIF(keep_in_quarantine_, FALSE)`, so an edit revokes an approval and never holds a row, and their result lines cannot say which rows lost one. The data page's GOTCHAS (`internal/resources/pages/data.md`) carry the details.
- `rows search-replace` is the group's only verb with a MANDATORY server-side preview and no row selector. Without `--yes` it spends one request with `dry_run: true`, prints `search-replace (dry run): N matching row(s) in <scope>`, and stops at exit 0 with no second request when `N` is 0 — the rule `useSearchAndReplace.ts` enforces in the grid by disabling Replace on a zero count. `--dry-run` stops after that line whatever the count; `--dry-run --yes` is exit 2 rather than a flag that changes nothing. `--yes` writes in exactly one request. `match_mode` is the full 2x2 of `--regex` x `--case-sensitive` (`contains_case_insensitive` / `contains` / `regex_case_insensitive` / `regex`), a deliberate widening of the web UI, whose `deriveMatchMode` lets regex win and can never emit `regex_case_insensitive`. Patterns are never compiled client-side: the server reads them as Python `re`, which Go's RE2 cannot (it has no lookarounds and no backreferences), and refuses a bad one with its own 422 before it reads a row, in one of three shapes: `Invalid regex pattern: ...` when Python cannot compile it, `Regex pattern uses ..., which cannot be translated for PostgreSQL.` when the translation PostgreSQL runs cannot express it — which includes any backreference under `regex_case_insensitive` — and `Regex pattern cannot be run in PostgreSQL (...).` when PostgreSQL cannot compile that translation, as with a nested bounded repeat such as `(?:a{1000}){1000}`. All exit 2, and without `--regex` every non-empty term is valid: the substring modes read it with Excel's wildcards (`*`, `?`, and `~` escaping either or itself) on the match and the replace side alike, so no non-empty `--search` is refused client-side and a whitespace term is sent, and matched, as typed. The result line carries the route's three counts and `fields_affected`, and the follow-up `qctl get data` recipe searches for the REPLACEMENT — omitted entirely when the replacement is empty, since there would be nothing to search for.
- `rows set-cell` writes ONE field to ONE value on the rows `--row-id` names, through `modify-clean-cell`'s `overwrite_value` action — the CLI form of the grid's cell edit, where `search-replace` is the CLI form of its find-and-replace. It is the group's only verb whose prompt carries user text: `confirmAction`'s gerund is built as `Set <field> to "<value>" on`, which is why `confirm.go` lowercases only the first RUNE of a gerund (`lowerLead`) instead of the whole string — `strings.ToLower` named a different value in the refusal than in the question. `--signature`, `--status` and `--all` are registered in order to be refused with a message naming `--row-id`, rather than left to cobra's unknown-flag error; `--all` gets there by declaring `allRefused` with the shared `genericAllRefusal` renderer, so it prints the same sentence as the other two and before `--field`/`--value` are checked. There is no cloud-source or file scope either: `data_source_model_id` changes nothing for `overwrite_value`. The verb's own `--field`/`--value` refusals run through `resolveTarget`'s `localChecks` hook — step 2b of the group's refusal order — so they cost no request, table resolve included. `--value ""` writes an EMPTY STRING and is legal: `new_value` is a required non-nullable `str`, so the route's `Please enter a new value` 422 is unreachable from here. The route answers the literal `{'result': 'ok'}`, so the result line reports no count and says so. The read-back is one `qctl get data <t> --is-bad-data --filter-by-field-name id --filter-by-field-value <id>` line per distinct `--row-id`, under `printFollowUp`'s one heading, and never a filter on the written value: on an `--is-bad-data` read `get_filter_queries` compares an equals filter on a money or percent column with the column's derived `_0x0_` number, so a value that is not a number, the empty string included, is a 400 at exit 2, and a number is compared with a derived value `overwrite_value` never writes. `id` is a reserved `BigInteger` column, so its filter is `id = N` whatever the field's format, and choosing a filter per format would cost a schema GET that set-cell's scope does not otherwise make (qctl-2vtl). `TestSetCell_ResultLineAndRecipe` pins the lines.
- `rows ignore-rule` / `rows unignore-rule` are the CLI surface of `rule-row-ignores`, which no web page calls: suppress ONE table rule on named quarantined rows of ONE cloud source (`ignore-rule`), and remove such an ignore from rows that may since have left quarantine (`unignore-rule`). Their support rows (`cloud: [--row-id]`, `table`/`file` nil) differ only in the row-id population: `ignoreRuleSupport` is `rowIDsQuarantined`, because create validates ids against the bad-rows table, and `unignoreRuleSupport` is `rowIDsIgnored`, because an ignore stays on a row that leaves quarantine and the row keeps its id in the clean table (the two tables share one sequence). There is no table-wide form, because `data_source_model_id` is required on all three routes. `requireCloudSourceScope` runs ahead of `resolveTarget` so a missing narrowing is named at exit 2, rather than answered by `resolveSelector`'s "needs a row selector: (none)". There is deliberately no auto-detect of a table's single source for these writes, though `get rule-ignores`, a read, does auto-detect. `--rule` goes through `cmdutil.FindTableRule`, the strict resolver `table-rule move` shares: an r-* alias, an exact name, a SHORT-ID or a UUID, which must be on this table, with no substring fallback. Row ids are de-duplicated before create, because the route answers one record per id in the body.
- `rows unignore-rule` never takes an ignore id. atlas's `DELETE .../rule-row-ignores/{id}` looks the record up by id alone (`un_ignore_by_id`), with no dataset, org or data-source scoping (qctl-tas). So the verb deletes only records that one scoped listing by rule returned, and `rule_row_ignores.Client.UnIgnore` takes the RECORD rather than an id, sending the record's own `data_source_model_id`.
- For the named rows it handles misses and failures this way:
  - Named rows with no active ignore are warned about and skipped.
  - If none of the rows has one, it exits 3 before any write (the `send-to-quarantine` precedent).
  - A 404 from a DELETE is checked against one fresh scoped listing after the loop (`removeIgnores`). An ignore gone from it was removed by someone else, which is a warning. A row the listing still carries an ignore for (matched by row, so a removed-and-re-created ignore counts) returns its 404. A failed listing returns its own error. This matters because the route's dataset and cloud-source guards answer 404 too, and a table deleted mid-run must not read as "already removed" at exit 0.
  - Any other failure stops the loop with its own exit code and says how many ignores were removed first.
- Both writes print, before the prompt and under `--yes`, the side effect nobody predicts from the verb. `reprocess_bad_rows_for_dataset_rule` queues EVERY quarantined row the rule flags, in every cloud source of the table, on every create (already-ignored pairs included), mirror-loaded tables included since atlas-wo1g removed the fan-out's sync skip. The phantom run that reprocess publishes reads ignores by row id; an upload reads them only on a mirror table or an append table with `key_columns` (atlas-cyd, gated by `Ingestor._steward_carry_source`), matching each line on the ignored row's `initial_signature_`, and carries nothing for a duplicated key (atlas-s19g). So `ignore-rule` says which later uploads keep the ignore (the same key on mirror, the same unchanged line on keyless mirror, the same key in a new version of the same file on keyed append) and that any other upload carrying an ignored row again can flag it again. It names no way to re-apply the ignore after that: an ignore is keyed on a row id, and keyless mirror can re-mint a row's id. `ignore-rule` also says an already-ignored row keeps its existing ignore and reason (create's idempotent early return). `unignore-rule` prints its own reprocess note ("still flags") and says a row the ignore already let through is not re-flagged by that queue, because its `dataset_rule_ids_` no longer carries the rule.
- `--all` is the one selector whose population comes from the **route**, not from the verb: the two delete-all-bad-rows routes and reprocess-all-bad-rows name the quarantine table by construction and take no `is_bad_data` parameter, while `send-to-quarantine --all` and `reprocess-clean --all` take a different route again (`move-all-rows-to-quarantine`) that reads the CLEAN table. Each verb declares that once in `support.all` (`allPopulation` in `selector.go`, the twin of `rowIDPopulation`), which the confirmation prompt reads to name the right half of `describe table`'s `N clean / M quarantined` line, and `refuseAllBadRows` keeps `--all` off a clean-population delete. The move-all route's INTENT still comes from the verb: it takes the by-id route's `quarantine_hold`, and `datasets.Client.MoveAllRowsToQuarantine` always sends it rather than leaving the server's default (`hold`) to decide — `hold` for `send-to-quarantine`, `undecided` for `reprocess-clean`, matching the web UI's Clean Data "Reprocess" over every clean row. The route honors that value from atlas 677b597f5 (#1215) on; an older server ignores the body and parks every row. What a refused `--all` SAYS is declared per verb: a support row with `all: allRefused` must also set `refuseAll` — a verb-specific renderer, or the shared `genericAllRefusal` (which `set-cell` declares), naming the selectors the row accepts at the active scope. `unsupportedSelector` never picks a message by verb name and never substitutes the generic one for a missing declaration; `TestEveryRefusedAllDeclaresARenderer` walks the registered tree (through `registeredSupports`, whose own coverage test fails on an unlisted verb) and fails on an incomplete row.

## share commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `share table <id-or-name>...` | `internal/commands/share/table.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `table_test.go` |

**Notes:**
- `share table` uses the bulk route (`POST /orgs/{org}/datasets/add-user`) even for one table. `--permission owner|editor|read_only` defaults to `read_only`; existing grants keep their level. The frontend uses the member POST for a single new grant; qctl keeps the bulk route's idempotent result partition for one or many tables. External users cannot receive a grant.
- The recipient is addressed by `--to <email>` or `--to-id <uuid>` — the house `--X`/`--X-id` split, as with `--table`/`--table-id`. A single `--user` flag taking either shape was considered and rejected: `--user` is already a persistent flag on root (`init` in `internal/commands/root/root.go`), dead since OAuth but still registered, so a local one would shadow it.
- Selectors are resolved and then deduplicated by table ID in first-selector order. A table selected as both `orders` and `12` is granted once, counted once in the result, and displayed with the first selector's label.
- A 207 is a success at the transport layer — the grants under `granted` are committed — but the command exits 1 so a script sees that some tables were refused.

## table-member commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `table-member set-permission <user-id>` | `internal/commands/table_member/table_member.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `../root/table_members_test.go` |
| `table-member remove <user-id>` | `internal/commands/table_member/table_member.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `../root/table_members_test.go` |
| `table-member leave [table]` | `internal/commands/table_member/table_member.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `../root/table_members_test.go` |

Select the table with `--table <name>` or `--table-id <id>`; `leave` also accepts a positional table. User IDs are full UUIDs from `get table-members`. Each mutation prompts unless `--yes`, and honors the protected-context guard. A level change returns the member; removal returns `{result, dataset_id, user_id}` under JSON/YAML. No name view is offered because a grant needs both table and user IDs.

## email-intake commands (a cloud source's email intake address)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `email-intake rotate <cloud-source-id>` | `internal/commands/email_intake/rotate.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`Get`, `ListSenders`, `Rotate`) | `rotate_test.go` |

Notes:
- The intake's on/off switch is `enable`/`disable email-intake` and its state is on `describe cloud-source`; this group holds only the verb no existing parent fits. `rotate` is annotated `catalog.For("cloud-source")`, so `cloud-source/<id>` is accepted.
- `rotate` reads `GET .../email-intake` first and, before any prompt, exits 2 (`qerrors.NewBadUsagef`) for a source with no address, mirroring the server's 409, and for an intake that is off unless `--no-notify-senders` is given — the server emails the new address whether or not intake is on, and an off intake accepts no mail. Without `--yes` and without a terminal (`cmdutil.CanPrompt`) it exits 2 (`rotateNeedsYes`) right after that read. It then reads `GET .../email-intake/senders` so the prompt can name up to `maxNamedSenders` (10) senders (`senderNotice`); a failed read warns on stderr and the prompt goes on without names. It is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands` and confirms through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`, no shorthand, like `email-sender remove`). The `POST .../email-intake/rotate` body always carries `email_senders` explicitly (`!--no-notify-senders`). Table output is `printRotated` (old and new address); `-o json|yaml` the server's intake; `-o name` `cloud-source/<id>`.

## email-sender commands (who may send files to a cloud source's email intake)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `email-sender add <email>` | `internal/commands/email_sender/add.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`AddSender`; `PreviewWelcome` under `--dry-run`) | `add_test.go` |
| `email-sender remove <email-or-user-id>` | `internal/commands/email_sender/remove.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`ResolveSender`, `RemoveSender`) | `remove_test.go` |
| `email-sender resend-welcome <email-or-user-id>` | `internal/commands/email_sender/resend_welcome.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`ResolveSender`, `ResendWelcome`) | `resend_welcome_test.go` |

Notes:
- The verb half of the `email-sender` resource; the read is `get email-senders`. A noun group like `table-member` and `pii-grant`, not `create`/`delete email-sender`. `--cloud-source-id` is required on every verb and declared with `cmdutil.RefInt` (kind `cloud-source`); `cmdutil.PositiveIDFlag` checks it before `Bootstrap`.
- `add` checks everything before `Bootstrap` (`addParse`): a blank email, `--first-name` or `--last-name` (`cmdutil.RejectBlankFlags`) and `-o name` with `--dry-run` each exit 2. Its positional takes no kind prefix (`cmdutil.WithArgKinds(..., "-")`), and it declares `table|json|yaml|name` with `cmdutil.WithFormats`. `--dry-run` sends `POST .../email-intake/welcome/preview` instead and prints the subject and text body (`addPrintPreview`). A real add prints `addPrintResult` on stdout and `addWarnOutcome` on stderr: the kept-names warning and every welcome outcome but `scheduled`, all at exit 0.
- `remove` and `resend-welcome` resolve the sender through `email_intake.Client.ResolveSender`: after an optional `email-sender/` prefix is stripped, an argument containing `@` matches a listed sender's email case-insensitively and anything else must parse as a UUID (the `email-sender` entry in `cmdutil.refKinds` is `uuidID`); a user id is also looked up in the listing. A malformed id exits 2 and a sender not in the listing — which holds only the source's active senders — exits 3, both before any write. `remove` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands`, like `table-member remove`, and confirms through `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`); `-o json|yaml` prints `{result: removed, cloud_source_id, user_id, email}` (`rmResult`). `resend-welcome` never prompts and prints the outbound email id; `-o json|yaml` the server's `{outbound_email_id}`. Both print `email-sender/<user-id>` under `-o name`.

## copy / merge commands (carry one table's setup into another)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `copy table [table]` | `internal/commands/copy/table.go` (+ `data_sources_file.go` `--data-sources-file` reader, `specs.go` entry checks and request, `series.go` period flags, `copy_secrets.go` missing-secrets refusal, `plan.go` plan and dry run, `result.go` result view, `data_source_error.go` located errors) | `datasets.NewClient` (`CopyOptions`, `Copy`), `table_series.NewClient` (`ResolveID`) | `table_test.go`, `data_sources_file_test.go`, `specs_test.go`, `series_test.go`, `copy_secrets_test.go`, `plan_test.go`, `result_test.go`, `data_source_error_test.go` |
| `copy cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/copy/cloud_source.go` (+ `cloud_source_flags.go` repeatable flag binding, `cloud_source_secrets.go` `--secrets-file` reader and missing-secrets refusal, `cloud_source_plan.go` plan and dry run, `result.go` activation steps) | `cloud_sources.NewClient` (`GetCloudSourceTiny`, `CopyTemplate`, `Copy`) | `cloud_source_test.go`, `cloud_source_flags_test.go`, `cloud_source_secrets_test.go`, `cloud_source_plan_test.go` |
| `merge table-rules [table]` | `internal/commands/merge/table_rules.go` (+ `table_rules_output.go`) | `rule_merges.NewClient` (`Preview`, `Apply`, `Get` under `--wait`) + `dataset_rules.NewClient` (both tables' rules) | `table_rules_test.go` |

**Notes:**
- `copy table` reads `GET /datasets/{id}/copy` first and always sends `include` as an explicit list computed from it (no flag: every option whose `default` or `always` is true; `--include`: those named plus the `always` ones; `--exclude`: the defaults minus those named), so the plan it prints and the body it sends are the same list. `--dry-run` stops after the plan; otherwise `cmdutil.Confirm` (`--yes`), then `POST`.
- `copy table --series <name-or-id> --period-key K` resolves the series in the current organization before the copy-options GET, which then carries `series_id` and `period_key` and returns the series-derived names; the copy POST carries the membership as `series` with `name` and `table_name` null. The server derives the period dates from the key under the series frequency. Local period-key validation, and the refusal of `--name`/`--table-name` with `--series`, precede bootstrap. The text plan and structured dry-run request include the selected membership, and the GET refuses a dry run as the POST would (403, 404, 409, 422 `SERIES_PERIOD_KEY_INVALID`); the derived table name is checked only at the source table's location, so the POST alone checks it at another destination, database or schema.
- `copy cloud-source` reads the original's tiny view, refuses deleted, upload and composite sources and an active source without `--set` (except a `simple_url` source, whose URL is not inherited), then uses the target table's copy template to build the request. `--to-table` is an exact name, including a numeric-looking name; `--to-table-id` is an ID, and omitting both keeps the original's table. The state defaults to the original's; `--name` replaces the template's suggestion. `--set` uses `cloud_sources.ParseCopyFields`, which refuses credential and URL keys and names `--secrets-file`. `--secrets-file` (`-` is stdin) is one YAML mapping keyed by `cloud_sources.CopyRequestSecretKeys`, each value resolved by the `resolveSecret` that `copy table`'s `--data-sources-file` uses (exact `${VAR}` reads the environment). The body uses `BuildCopyBody`, which forwards the template's `s3_access_key` unless the file replaces it. The plan prints first (stderr under structured/name output) and names supplied, kept and missing credentials without values, upload-source disabling only for an active copy, polling only for an active scheduled copy, and cursor reset only when the original has one. An active or disabled copy that lacks any template `secrets_to_resupply` field is then refused with exit 2, before `--dry-run`'s document or the prompt. `--dry-run` copies nothing and prints one `{template, request}` document under `-o json|yaml`, with credential fields removed from the raw template and credential values masked in the request. Secrets and URLs are not inherited. After a copy, `-o json|yaml` prints the response through the output printer, which masks secret fields unless `--allow-plaintext-secrets`; `-o name` prints `cloud-source/<id>`; the default view names the new source, then for a draft that lacks secrets the `get -o yaml`, `apply -f` and `enable` steps that complete it, and otherwise any secrets it lacks and its describe command.
- `--data-sources-file` entries are checked against the GET before the prompt: `source_id` must be one the GET lists, a selector key must be one of THAT source's `distinguishing_fields`, and a copy of an active source that has a location must change at least one of them — the backend's same-files guard compares every active source in the org, the copied one included — except a `simple_url` source, whose URL the GET withholds. Each resulting name must differ from earlier copies and `<new table name> Source`; a later copy of the same source cannot match an earlier active copy's resulting selectors (and, for `simple_url`, its supplied URL). An empty `name`, `state` or secret is refused; an empty value of any other key clears the field, since the backend stores an unset selector or schedule as `""`, never null. Secrets come from the file (exact `${VAR}` via `secrets.ExactEnvRef`); the secret keys are `cloud_sources.CopySecretKeys`. After the plan and before the prompt or `--dry-run`'s document, `refuseMissingSecrets` exits 2 for an active or disabled copy lacking one of its source's `secrets_to_resupply`. The dry-run `request` masks every supplied secret (`maskSecrets`), and a 400 or 5xx located at `[body, data_sources, "N", ...]` is renamed to its file entry by `data_source_error.go`.
- `merge table-rules` previews afresh on every run (each `POST .../rule-merges` stores a new previewed record, and apply refuses a plan that moved with 409), so a `-f` RuleMergeResolution entry names the SOURCE rule and `rule_merges.ToRequest` maps it onto the fresh preview's item id. The positional / `--table` / `--table-id` is the TARGET (the table written, and the one `describe rule-merge` takes); `--from` / `--from-id` is the source. `--wait` polls `GET .../rule-merges/{id}`, which also settles the merge server-side. `waitForRuleMerge` runs that poll under `cmdutil.StartWait`, whose stderr banner names `qctl describe rule-merge <id> --table-id <target-id>`: applied exits 0, partially_applied or failed 1, running out of `--wait-timeout` 124 (`rule_merges.ErrWaitTimeout` wrapped in `qerrors.NewTimedOut`), and SIGINT/SIGTERM 130/143, the last two leaving the merge applying.
- Before an apply, once every conflict is resolved, `merge table-rules` checks that no replacement targets a `pending_validation` rule and that no two items replace one target rule. `--dry-run -f` makes the same check; a bare dry run stays a preview.

## run / kill commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `run ingestion-job` | `internal/commands/run/ingestion_job.go` | `ingestion.NewClient` | `ingestion_job_test.go` |
| `run profiling-job` | `internal/commands/run/profiling_job.go` | `profiling.NewClient` | `profiling_job_test.go` |
| `run sensor-job <cloud-source-id>` | `internal/commands/run/sensor_job.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `sensor_job_test.go` |
| `run rule-run-replay <rule-run-id>` | `internal/commands/run/rule_run_replay.go` | `rule_run_replays.NewClient` | `rule_run_replay_test.go` |
| `run composite-run-job [table]` | `internal/commands/run/composite_run_job.go` | `linked_tables.NewClient` (`LatestRevision` + `RetryBuild`) | `composite_run_job_test.go` |
| `kill ingestion-job` | `internal/commands/kill/ingestion_job.go` | `ingestion.NewClient` | `ingestion_job_test.go` |
| `kill profiling-job` | `internal/commands/kill/profiling_job.go` | `profiling.NewClient` | `profiling_job_test.go` |

Notes:
- Accept IDs as positional args OR `--filter` for bulk operations.
- `--dry-list` previews, `--yes` skips confirmation.
- `run sensor-job` is one exception to the two notes above: its positional is a **cloud source** id, not a job id, and it has no `--filter`, `--dry-list` or `--yes`, because there is no sensor-job listing to select over and the route takes one source. There is no `kill sensor-job` either — stopping a sensor is a whole-source state change, `qctl disable cloud-source`. It reads `GET .../tiny` and refuses, client-side with exit 2, a source type without a sensor (`cloud_sources.RequireSensorEligible`: an upload or composite source) before it refuses a source that is not active (`cloud_sources.RequireActive`).
- `run rule-run-replay` is another: its positional is a **rule run** id (from `describe row`'s `## RUN <id>` evidence headings — there is no rule-run listing), and it has no `--filter`, `--dry-list` or `--yes`. Its only flag is `--max-rows`, validated `> 0` client-side by `cmdutil.PositiveCountFlag` because oapi-codegen dropped the spec's `exclusiveMinimum` and `api.RuleRunReplayLaunchSchema.MaxRows` is a plain `*int`. The launch is not idempotent (a second call starts a second replay), so unlike `create dry-run-job` it does **not** go through `cmdutil.RetryOnThrottle` on a 429; the `*apierror.ThrottleError` carrying the server's `Retry-After` reaches the user instead. There is no `kill rule-run-replay` — the carrier is an ordinary dry-run job. Under `-o json|yaml` it prints the server's `{replay_id, dry_run_job_id, rows_in_evidence, rows_pinned}`; `-o name` prints `rule-run-replay/<replay-id>`, an `intID` kind in `cmdutil.refKinds` that `describe rule-run-replay` takes back.
- `run composite-run-job` is a third: its positional is a **linked table** (id-or-name, or `--table`/`--table-id`), not a job id, because no composite run job exists until the scheduler claims the rebuild's queue message. It reads the table's latest spec revision for its composite spec group, then sends `POST /composite-spec-groups/{group}/retry-build`, which resets the group's build circuit breaker (and resolves its open composite-build incidents and their alerts) and queues a rebuild whether or not the breaker had tripped. Output: `linked-table/<table> rebuild queued`, a `breaker :` line (`was tripped, now reset` / `was not tripped`) and `Next: qctl get composite-run-jobs --group-id <group> --reason operator_retry`. The response's required `run_job_id` is the outbox row id that queues the rebuild, so the default output never prints it; `-o json`/`-o yaml` print the body as sent and `-o name` prints `linked-table/<table-id>`. A group with no enabled revision, or with no active composite source, answers `409` (exit 2). No `--filter`, `--dry-list` or `--yes`, and no `kill composite-run-job`.

## rekey commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rekey linked-table [table] -f <manifest>` | `internal/commands/rekey/linked_table.go` | `linked_tables.Client.Rekey` (+ table and source reads) | `linked_table_test.go` |

- `rekey linked-table` takes the selected linked table by positional id-or-name, `--table`, or `--table-id`, plus a one-document LinkedTable manifest from `-f`. It checks that the manifest's name, coordinates, destination, primary source, identity hash version and (when named) backup settings match the live table, and resolves source names exactly before calling `POST /composite-spec-groups/{group}/rekey`. `spec.release` is required; `spec.dry_run` is refused. It asks for the rebuild and row-annotation consequences to be acknowledged, with `--yes` for scripts. The result is a draft revision; table output prints its new group and the dry-run command, while `-o json|yaml` print the server response and `-o name` prints the table name.

## enable / disable / set-default commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enable cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/enable/cloud_source.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `cloud_source_test.go` |
| `enable email-intake <cloud-source-id>` | `internal/commands/enable/email_intake.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`SetEnabled`) | `email_intake_test.go` |
| `enable rule <name>` | `internal/commands/enable/rule.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | `rule_test.go` |
| `enable linked-table [table]` | `internal/commands/enable/linked_table.go` | `linked_tables.NewClient` (+ `ResolveRevision`) | `linked_table_test.go` |
| `enable deliverable <ref>` | `internal/commands/enable/deliverable.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (+ `ResolveEnableTarget`, `Validate`, `FollowingRuns`, `Enable`) | `../root/deliverable_enable_test.go` |
| `disable cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/disable/cloud_source.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `cloud_source_test.go` |
| `disable email-intake <cloud-source-id>` | `internal/commands/disable/email_intake.go` | `email_intake.NewClient` (`SetEnabled`) | `email_intake_test.go` |
| `disable rule <name>` | `internal/commands/disable/rule.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | `rule_test.go` |
| `set-default rule <name>` | `internal/commands/set_default/rule.go` | `rule_versions.NewClient` | `rule_test.go` |

Notes:
- The `rule` commands use `rule_versions.ResolveRule()` for name resolution, with `--release` to disambiguate multi-release rules.
- `enable cloud-source` / `disable cloud-source` are whole-source state toggles. Both read `GET .../tiny` first and refuse, client-side with exit 2, a deleted source (the server also rejects deleted sources with 404) and a composite source, naming the linked table that owns it. `disable` POSTs to `disable-sensor-cronjob`. `enable` dispatches on the source's type: a sensor-eligible type (`cloud_sources.SensorEligible`) POSTs to `run-sensor-cronjob`, whose most visible side effect (the cronjob) is what it is named after on the server; every other type — an upload source (`qluster_ui`), or a type a later spec adds — goes through `cloud_sources.Client.ActivateCloudSource`, which reads `GET .../data-sources/{id}` for its `version_id` and sends `PATCH .../data-sources/{id}` with `{id, version_id, state: "active"}`. That PATCH needs source:edit where the sensor routes need dataset:ingest:launch and access to the source's table (admin/ops bypass; other permitted roles need a grant that is not read-only), and answers 409 (exit 2) when the source changed between the read and the write.
- `enable email-intake` / `disable email-intake` act on a cloud source's email intake, not its state, which is why they are separate from `enable`/`disable cloud-source`. The positional is the cloud source id (`cloud-source/<id>` stripped by root's `cmdutil.StripKindRefs`, as the commands are annotated `catalog.For("cloud-source")`), checked by `cmdutil.ParseIntArgs` and a positive check before `Bootstrap`. Each sends one `PUT .../data-sources/{id}/email-intake` with `{enabled, auto_send_issues_report}`, the latter null unless `--auto-send-issues-report` was given (`Flags().Changed`). Neither prompts nor is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands`: disabling keeps the address and is undone by the next enable. Table output is `printEnabledIntake` / `printDisabledIntake`; `-o json|yaml` the server's intake (`output.WriteRaw`); `-o name` `cloud-source/<id>`. The address rotation lives in its own noun group, `email-intake rotate`.
- `enable linked-table` takes the linked table positionally or by `--table`/`--table-id` and resolves the revision through `linked_tables.Client.ResolveRevision` (the table's latest revision, or a `--revision` checked against the table's group — see `create composite-dry-run-job`). A revision already `enabled` is reported without the `POST /composite-spec-revisions/{rev}/enable`, because the backend refuses enabled → enabled with a `400`; the command says `already enabled` and exits 0. An enable gate that refuses (the dry-run gate, an inactive composite source, a dependency cycle, sources that no longer resolve) answers `400`, and the command exits 2 with its message. Output: `linked-table/<table> enabled` (or `already enabled`), a `revision :` line (release, or UUID, and state) and, after an enable, `Next: qctl get composite-run-jobs --group-id <group>`; `-o json`/`-o yaml` print the server's answer (for an already-enabled revision, the same three fields built from the revision read), `-o name` prints `linked-table/<table>`. `<table>` is the table as typed: the positional, `--table`, or the `--table-id` number.
- `enable deliverable` resolves the deliverable including the trash, then `deliverables.Client.ResolveEnableTarget` picks the revision: `--release` (an exact label, then a full UUID) or `--revision` (a full UUID or `deliverable-revision/<uuid>`), matched among the deliverable's revisions, or else its only draft. No draft, or several, exits 2 naming `qctl get deliverable-revisions`; no revisions at all exits 3. A revision already `enabled` is reported without the `POST /deliverable-revisions/{id}/enable`, because the backend refuses enabled → enabled with a `409`; the command says `already enabled` and exits 0 — unless the deliverable is in the trash, which exits 2 before any POST, naming `qctl undelete deliverable`, whatever the revision's state (the check comes first, as atlas `enable_revision`'s `require_not_archived` does). Otherwise `FollowingRuns` lists the runs the enable will move (one state-filtered, paged `GET /deliverables/{id}/runs` walk each for `open`, `rendered` and `stale`, keeping `new_period` and `reissue_enabled` runs on another revision), and then the one enable POST is sent. A 422 `REVISION_INVALID` prints each `context.errors` issue as `<loc joined by dots>: <msg>` (`deliverables.FormatIssue`), and a 409 with `context.runs` prints one `run <id>: <reason>` line each; both exit 2 through the `%w` chain. `--dry-run` sends `POST /deliverable-revisions/{id}/validate` instead and never the enable, for an enabled revision and a trashed deliverable too: each warning prints on stderr as a `Warning:` line, an invalid result exits 2 listing its errors, and a valid one that is not enabled lists the runs an enable would move. Output: `deliverable/<slug> release "<release>" enabled`, with `(replaces "<release>")` when another revision was enabled, the moved-runs lines (a rendered run marked `→ stale (revision_superseded; re-render required)`) and `Next: qctl describe deliverable <slug>`. `-o json`/`-o yaml` print the server's revision tree (for an already-enabled revision, the revision read back), or under `--dry-run` the raw `{valid, errors, warnings}`, printed even when it is invalid; `-o name` prints `deliverable-revision/<uuid>` when the command succeeds. Under all three the prose goes to stderr. The command is not destructive: no prompt and no protected-context gate.

## create / inspect commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `create dry-run-job` | `internal/commands/create/dry_run_job.go` | `dry_runs.NewClient` | `dry_run_job_test.go` |
| `create composite-dry-run-job [table]` | `internal/commands/create/composite_dry_run_job.go` | `composite_dry_runs.NewClient` (+ `linked_tables.ResolveRevision`) | `composite_dry_run_job_test.go` |
| `create deliverable-run <deliverable-ref>` | `internal/commands/create/deliverable_run.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`ResolveID` including the trash, `Get`, `AllRuns`, `GetRevision`, `CreateRun`; params through `paramflags.RunParamInput`; `--render` through `deliverable_run.Render`) | `../root/deliverable_create_run_test.go`, `../root/deliverable_run_render_test.go` |
