| `inspect composite-dry-run-job <id>` | `internal/commands/inspect/composite_dry_run_job.go` | `composite_dry_runs.NewClient` | `composite_dry_run_job_test.go` |
| `inspect deliverable <ref>` | `internal/commands/inspect/deliverable.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (+ `ResolveRevision`, `GetRevision`, `Preview`; params through `paramflags.RunParamInput`) | `../root/deliverable_preview_test.go` |

Notes:
- `create` only creates `dry-run-job`, `composite-dry-run-job` and `deliverable-run` imperatively. Guessing any apply-managed
  resource (the catalog's `ClassApply` set — or any of its aliases) returns a
  redirect naming the typed `qctl apply <resource> -f <file.yaml>` when that
  subcommand exists, and `qctl apply -f <file.yaml>   # kind: <Kind>` otherwise.
  The `create --help` `Long` lists the same set, generated from the pages by
  `applyManagedNames` — pinned by `TestCreateLongListsApplyManagedResources`
  and `TestCreateLongMatchesRedirect`.
- `create dry-run-job` retries a 429 from `PUT /orgs/{org}/dry-runs` through
  `cmdutil.RetryOnThrottle` (the same helper `apply`'s TableRule handler uses):
  the backend caps concurrent dry runs per user at both the launch and the
  instantiate endpoint. The retry line is written to **stderr** because this
  command's stdout is the launch response as JSON.
- `create composite-dry-run-job` takes the linked table positionally or by `--table`/`--table-id`, and resolves the revision through `linked_tables.Client.ResolveRevision`: it always reads the table's latest revision (`GET /datasets/{id}/latest-composite-spec-revision`), and a `--revision` naming a different one adds `GET /composite-spec-revisions/{id}` and exits 2 when that revision belongs to another group. `--max-spine-rows` (`cmdutil.PositiveIDFlag`, omitted when unset), `--seed` (sent whenever the flag is given, so `0` is a seed) and `--pin-spine-row` (each `> 0`) are validated before any request. The `PUT /composite-spec-revisions/{rev}/dry-runs` goes through `cmdutil.RetryOnThrottle` with the retry line on stderr. Output: `composite-dry-run-job/<id> created`, a `revision :` line (release, or UUID when there is none, and state) and `Next: qctl describe composite-dry-run-job <id>`; `-o json`/`-o yaml` print the server's answer, `-o name` prints `composite-dry-run-job/<id>`. `--wait` (`--wait-timeout` seconds, default 900) drops the `Next:` line and then waits as `apply linked-table --wait` does, through `waitForCompositeDryRun`: progress on stderr, `composite_dry_runs.WriteOutcome` on stdout — on stderr under `-o json|yaml|name`, whose stdout is unchanged — and `composite_dry_runs.Verdict` for a settled run's exit code. `awaitCompositeDryRun` puts the poll under `cmdutil.StartWait`, so the stderr banner, the 124 timeout and the 130/143 signal exits are the ones `apply linked-table --wait` has.
- `create deliverable-run` reads its parameters as `inspect deliverable` does: `paramflags.RegisterRunParamFlags`/`RunParamInput` own `--params-file` (one YAML scalar mapping, `-` reads stdin), `--period`, their blank checks and their mutual exclusion, all exit 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, and `deliverables.RunParamInput.ForDeliverable` puts `--period` under the deliverable's `period_param` (none exits 2). It resolves the deliverable including the trash and refuses one in the trash with exit 2 naming `qctl undelete deliverable`, as `enable deliverable` does, before listing anything. It always POSTs `run_params_typed` (`deliverables.TypedRunParams`, Python-canonical text) and sends `allow_empty`/`use_enabled_revision` only when their flag is given. Before the POST it lists every run (`AllRuns`: `order_by=id`, `reverse=false`, 500 per page) once: a delivered run whose typed params are `SameRunParamTexts`-equal makes it a re-issue, which skips the required-param check; otherwise it is a new period, and no enabled revision, or a required name of the enabled revision (`RequiredRunParams`) missing or blank, exits 2 with no POST. A re-issue with `--use-enabled-revision` and no enabled revision exits 2 the same way. The server still decides the mode. stderr `Note:` lines name a new period's parameters the enabled release does not use, a `--use-enabled-revision` re-issue's missing enabled-release names, a `--use-enabled-revision` that has no effect on a new period, and each open/rendered/stale/signed_off run already holding the same params. Output: `deliverables.FormatCreatedRun` (`deliverable-run/<id> created`, issue, mode in words, release — the run's revision read after the POST unless it is the enabled one already held, and its id when that read fails — params, `Next: qctl describe deliverable-run <id>`); `-o json|yaml` print the response body through `output.WriteRaw`, `-o name` prints `deliverable-run/<id>`. 409 refusals carry `CreateRun`'s hints. `--render` then requests the run's render through `deliverable_run.Render`, which prints `render requested` only under the table format, so `-o json|yaml|name` stdout stays the create answer; `--wait`/`--wait-timeout` wait as `deliverable-run render --wait` does, and `--wait` without `--render` exits 2 before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`.
- `inspect composite-dry-run-job` prints the examples page's response body as received — `output.WriteIndentedJSON` by default, `output.WriteJSONAsYAML` for `-o yaml` — because `join_values` and `synthesized_values` are data cells and a float64 round trip would round an integer key past 2^53. Pinned by `TestInspectCompositeDryRunJob_PrintsTheBodyAsSent`.
- `inspect dry-run-job` prints the preview response as received — JSON by default (the generated struct, whose `structured_summary` union marshals its raw bytes), YAML via a JSON round trip — so the compact view's `structured_summary` keeps exactly the keys the server sent. Pinned by `TestInspectDryRunJob_CompactStructuredSummaryIsTheWireObject`.
- `inspect dry-run-job --follow` (`followDryRunJob`) is `get dry-run-job --watch` with the states written to stderr: the same REST read first, `dry_runs.IsTerminal`, `dry_runs.Client.Follow` and `Client.Verdict`, so the exit codes match. It prints the preview only after a finished job; any other terminal state prints none and exits 1, where an `error` or `killed` job used to go on to fetch one. `--timeout` is `cmdutil.AddWatchTimeoutFlag`: `0` means no limit, the default is 10m, `QCTL_WATCH_TIMEOUT` overrides that default, and the flag exits 2 without `--follow`.
- `inspect deliverable` selects the enabled or sole revision, or an explicit `--release`/`--revision`, and POSTs its live-data preview. `--params-file` accepts one YAML scalar mapping (`-` reads stdin); the mutually exclusive `--period` supplies the deliverable's period parameter as a string (both through `paramflags.RunParamInput`, shared with `create deliverable-run`). `deliverables.RequiredRunParams` follows the UI's ordered, case-sensitive derivation (period, cells, columns, filename tokens), and missing, null or blank required values exit 2 before the POST. `--max-rows` is 1–1000, default 50, per sheet. Table output shows rows and cells with kept / left out / blocked counts; JSON/YAML print original response bytes and put counts on stderr; name output exits 2. `Preview` decodes row/cell values with `jsonnum`, and a `REVISION_INVALID` refusal uses the same issue renderer and unwrap chain as enable.

## upload / download / undelete commands

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `upload file` | `internal/commands/upload/file.go` (+ `uploader_target.go` for external uploaders, `replaced_file.go` for the replacement check before publish) | `stored_items.NewClient` (`GetReplacedFile`, `PublishStoredItemForIngestion`) | `file_test.go`, `uploader_target_test.go`, `replaced_file_test.go` |
| `upload deliverable-template [<file>]` | `internal/commands/upload/deliverable_template.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`UploadTemplate`, `FinalizeTemplate`; `ResolveID`, `Get` and `ResolveTemplateTarget` for `--deliverable`) | `../root/deliverable_template_test.go` |
| `download file <id>` | `internal/commands/download/file.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` | `file_test.go` |
| `download data <table-id-or-name>` | `internal/commands/download/data.go` | `datasets.NewClient` (+ `cmdutil.ResolveDataRuleIDs` for `--rule`) | `data_test.go`, `data_rule_test.go` |
| `download alert-report [table-id-or-name]` | `internal/commands/download/alert_report.go` | `alerts.NewClient` | `alert_report_test.go` |
| `download issues-report <file-id>` | `internal/commands/download/issues_report.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` (`GetStoredItem`, `PutIssuesReportForDownload`) | `issues_report_test.go` |
| `download deliverable-run <id>` | `internal/commands/download/deliverable_run.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` (`GetRun`, `GetRunDownloadURL`); `filedl.ToFile` | `../root/deliverable_run_download_test.go` |
| `undelete file <id>` | `internal/commands/undelete/file.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` | `file_test.go` |
| `undelete table <id-or-name>` | `internal/commands/undelete/table.go` | `datasets.NewClient` | `table_test.go` |
| `undelete deliverable <ref>` | `internal/commands/undelete/deliverable.go` | `deliverables.NewClient` | `../root/deliverable_lifecycle_test.go` |
| `undelete cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/undelete/cloud_source.go` | `cloud_sources.NewClient` | `cloud_source_test.go` |

Notes:
- `upload file --period <key>` and series routing: `cmdutil.ResolveTableRecord` keeps the table's series and period key, and `cmdutil.ResolveCloudSourceRecord` keeps the source's `table_series_id` and `routing_mode` and accepts a `--cloud-source-id` whose series `--table` belongs to (only `upload file` calls it). `decideUploadTarget` then picks what is sent: no `period_key` for a source in no series (`--period` exits 2); for `uploader_chooses` always one — `--period`, else `--table`'s own period, the two required to agree (`chosenPeriodTarget`); for any other routing mode none, with the file waiting on the source's template table (`routedTarget`). Without a table selector, `--cloud-source-id` plus `--period` chooses the period directly: `resolvePeriodTable` reads `table_series.Client.ListSubmissionPeriods` (upload permission, no admin-only series detail read), matches the exact key and resolves its table by ID before confirmation for callers with table-read permission. With `--cloud-source-id` and neither a table selector nor `--period`, `resolveUpload` asks `inferPeriod` (`internal/commands/upload/inferred_period.go`) for the key the file name names — `table_series.Client.ListUploadable` supplies the series frequency, `table_series.Client.SuggestPeriod` the key — and continues as with that `--period`; any other source without a table exits 2 after the source read. External uploaders instead use the listed dataset ID without reading table settings; with `--table-id` they resolve the source first, then verify its table or series membership through submission periods (`resolveUploaderTarget`). Their table-name selector exits 2 with ID-selector advice because they lack table-list permission. The existing `users/me` read runs first to select this path; it is still fetched only once. An unavailable table name is `(name unavailable)` in the confirmation and an empty string in structured output. An unlisted key exits 2. A listed period with null `dataset_id` is accepted only with `creatable: true`; no table read is made, confirmation and summary name the period, and JSON/YAML use `table_id: 0` and `table: ""`. Creation and its permission checks are left to the stored-item PUT; the series-wide listing can include a key the selected source refuses. There is no post-upload ingestion wait. `cmdutil.ValidatePeriodKey` checks the key's shape before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`. A 409 `PERIOD_LOCKED` (`apierror.CodePeriodLocked`) from the stored-item PUT carries apierror's unlock `Hint:`, and `explainPeriodLocked` adds an `Instead:` line naming `--period` or `--table`, keeping the `%w` chain (exit 2).
- `upload file --delta --delta-cursor <n>` pre-flights the server's delta rules in `checkDeltaUpload`, from the lookups the confirmation block already makes: `cmdutil.ResolveTableRecord` keeps the by-ID `DataSetSchemaFull` (loading process, `settings_model.key_columns`) and `cmdutil.ResolveCloudSourceRecord` keeps the cloud source's `delta_applied_cursor` and `dataset_id`. `refuseIncrementalTable` checks the period's table for an `uploader_chooses` source and otherwise the source's own table; a table found by name (the list row carries neither field) leaves the table rules to the server. For callers with table-read permission, a table resolved from `--cloud-source-id` plus `--period` is read by ID and gets both checks. External uploaders and creatable periods with no table leave these table-setting checks to the stored-item PUT; cursor checks and delta metadata readback still run. `refuseIncrementalCursor` compares the cursor with the source's `delta_applied_cursor` only for a source in no series, and for `uploader_chooses` with the period's entry in `period_delta_cursors`, fetching the full source once when it was found by name or auto-detected; a `latest_unlocked` source is refused outright, and `file_name`/`column` get no client cursor check. After the stored-item PUT, qctl reads the record back and requires both delta fields to match before it uploads any bytes; this is the one extra request, and protects a delta from older servers that silently ignore unknown fields. A plain upload's body carries `"delta_cursor": null` — the generated field has no `omitempty` — which the server reads as absent.
- `download file` writes `Getting download URL...`, the sanitized backend `Note:` and `Downloading to ...` to stderr on both the direct and decrypted paths. Only `Successfully downloaded file to: ...` goes to stdout; an encrypted `--no-wait` exits 0 with empty stdout and the queued-decrypt note on stderr. Its `--output` remains a destination path.
- `download deliverable-run` reads the run's `rendered_file_signature` before minting its URL (each mint records a download audit), resolves the mint's `filename` through `mintDestination`, and streams with `filedl.ToFile`. It compares the saved file's SHA-256 against the observed signature, removes the file on verification failure and exits 1; a concurrent render can cause that failure, so the error advises retrying. An absent or malformed signature after a successful mint exits 1 before transfer. `is_draft` prints a DRAFT note on stderr, including for cancelled runs; only success prints on stdout. Its local `--output` is a destination path, `--force` allows overwriting, and an explicit destination collision exits 2 before any request.
- `download data`, `download alert-report` and `download issues-report` share their delivery half in `data.go`: `exportOptions` owns `--output`/`--force`/`--no-wait`/`--watch-interval`/`--wait-timeout` (`registerFlags`, `validate`), and `queueAndCollect` takes a `queuedJob` (label, the request that queues it) plus `notifications.WaitOptions`. The request returns its `download_handle`, which `queueAndCollect` — not the call site, which cannot know it — puts on the wait; that is what tells two requests of one command on one table apart. `WaitOptions.Producer` is the fallback for a row that carries no handle: answered from `file_operation_action` where there is one, and from the file name's shape on a server before atlas d117e8429. `download file` keeps its own flags because its `--no-wait` governs only the encrypted branch.
- The file wait of `download data`, `download alert-report`, `download issues-report` and `download file`'s encrypted branch is `awaitFile` (`data.go`): `cmdutil.StartWait` writes `<job> queued; waiting for the file, for up to <--wait-timeout>; re-running this command requests the <job> again` to stderr — no resume command, because nothing collects a queued job's file by its handle — SIGINT/SIGTERM end the poll through its context and exit 130/143, and a timeout (`notifications.ErrWaitTimeout`, wrapped in `qerrors.NewTimedOut` by `runWaitLoop`) exits 124 with `re-running this command requests the <job> again` appended; the timeout does not establish the server job's status. `--no-wait` and an unencrypted `download file` print no banner. For `download data`, `download alert-report` and `download issues-report`, `--no-wait` prints `<Job> queued. Re-running without --no-wait requests the <job> again and waits for the file.` on stdout. The `This <job> was already queued; waiting for it to finish...` line `enqueue` prints goes to stderr, so stdout carries only the outcome.
- `download issues-report <file-id>` POSTs `stored_items.Client.PutIssuesReportForDownload` and waits for `notifications.ProducerBrokerIssuesReport`. The announcement carries the file's table and not the file, so a waiting run first reads the stored item (`GetStoredItem`) for `WaitOptions.DatasetID`, the fallback for rows without a handle; an exact `download_handle` match takes precedence over that table so moving the file during the wait does not strand its outcome. `--no-wait` skips that read and costs one request. That producer is the one in `failureProducers` (`internal/notifications/wait.go`): its snapshot and polls list `file_download_failed` with `file_ready_to_download` in one request, a failed row is matched by the ready row's rules (`accepts`, `matchFailedFile`), a ready row wins over a failure seen in the same poll, and the failure ends the wait with a `*notifications.DownloadFailedError` — exit 2 when it carries `broker_report_refusal`, exit 1 when the job failed to render or upload. `explainIssuesReportFailure` appends the `Hint:` to that error and to the route's 409 `BROKER_REPORT_REFUSED`, both through `stored_items.IssuesReportRefusalAdvice` with the verb `download` (`file send-issues-report` passes `send`); the 409 `ERR_QUEUE_KEY_CONFLICT` (`isAlreadyQueued`) is NOT waited for, unlike the other two jobs: it carries no `download_handle`, and every file's report of a table shares the day's name, so a handle-less wait would take another file's refusal or workbook for this one. `queuedJob.queuedAdvice` makes it exit 2, waiting or not, naming the `qctl get notifications` filter the report will appear under. A job that dies before writing either row still reaches the wait as a timeout (124). Deliberate divergence from cosmosview, which offers the action only for a file loaded before and not in trash (`FileHeader.tsx`): qctl sends it for any file and leaves the decision to the server's refusal.
- `upload deliverable-template` takes exactly one of `<file>` and `--template-id` (exit 2 otherwise). Without `--deliverable` it mints the upload URL and finalizes on the organization's routes, attaching nothing; with `--deliverable` (plus `--release` or `--revision`, else the only draft: `ResolveTemplateTarget` picks as `ResolveEnableTarget` does for `enable deliverable`, but a deliverable with no draft advises uploading unattached) it uses the revision's routes, and finalize attaches the template and returns the re-anchor report. `--template-id` finalizes an uploaded template again without sending bytes: the lossy re-run, and attaching an existing template to a draft. Before any request, `readTemplateRequest` checks the flags and `checkTemplateFile` the file (`deliverables.ValidateTemplatePath`: `.xlsx`/`.xlsm`, case-insensitive, read as the server's `splitext` reads the name before any `?`; a directory exits 2, a path `os.Stat` cannot read exits 1), and `resolveTemplateDraft` refuses a non-draft revision with exit 2 before the upload. A 409 `SKELETON_LOSSY` becomes a `*deliverables.TemplateLossyError` (exit 2 through its `*APIError`) listing `deliverables.FidelityLossLines`, and `finalizeTemplateError` appends the `--template-id <id> --acknowledge-lossy` re-run, naming the draft by its slug and `--release` (its label, or its UUID when `terminal.Sanitize` would change the label); an exit-1 or exit-4 finalize failure after an upload gets the same re-run line, with `--acknowledge-lossy` only when it was given. The table view is `deliverables.FormatTemplate` plus a reuse `Note:` (`writeReuseNote`, when a reused template's VBA setting disagrees with the file's extension) and a `Next:` line; `-o json|yaml` print the raw finalize body and `-o name` prints `deliverable-template/<id>`, with the prose on stderr. qctl deliberately does not PATCH the deliverable's `filename_pattern` to the new extension, as the web app's `syncTemplateExtension` does: downloads take the extension from the rendered revision's template, and the pattern belongs to the deliverable.
- `upload file` prints the `ingestion_job_id` that `stored_items.Client.PublishStoredItemForIngestion` returns and ends with `Next: qctl describe ingestion-job <id>`. `get ingestion-jobs` has no id filter, so describe takes the id. A response with no id leaves out both lines (`writeUploadSummary`). Under `-o json|yaml` it prints `uploadedFile` — `{stored_item_id, ingestion_job_id, table_id, table, period_key, routing_pending, cloud_source_id, cloud_source, replaces_stored_item_id}`, `ingestion_job_id`, `period_key` and `cloud_source` null when absent — and `file/<stored-item-id>` under `-o name`; the confirmation block and progress lines go to stderr.
- `upload file` publishes through `publishUpload` (`internal/commands/upload/replaced_file.go`): after the storage PUT it reads `stored_items.Client.GetReplacedFile` and, when the server names a loaded file the upload would replace, `decideReplacement` prints it and picks the `replaces_stored_item_id` `PublishStoredItemForIngestion` sends. `--replaces <file-id>` sends it only when it matches and otherwise refuses with exit 2; without `--yes` `uc.confirmReplacement` asks `Replace <period>'s data with this file?`; under `--yes` alone nothing is sent and a `Note:` names the `--replaces` to pass. A decline or mismatch removes the unpublished record (`withRecordRemoved`, the cleanup `explainPublishFailure` also uses). A publish refused with `REPLACED_FILE_CHANGED` re-reads and decides again, at most `maxReplacementPublishes` publishes. A failed read warns and publishes unconfirmed, or with `--replaces` sends it for the server to check. JSON/YAML add `replaces_stored_item_id`. Deliberate match of cosmosview's upload dialogs (`publishConfirmingReplacement` in `src/common/utils/fileUpload.ts`); `--replaces` and the `--yes` behavior are qctl's own, so a scripted upload never confirms a replacement it did not name. Tests: `replaced_file_test.go`.
- `upload file` exits 1 when the storage transfer fails and attempts signed-upload record cleanup (`handleUploadFailure` in `internal/commands/upload/file.go`). A failed cleanup is reported at normal verbosity with `qctl describe file <id>` and keeps the upload failure’s exit code; only successful cleanup claims the record was removed. Tests: `storage_failure_test.go`.
- `upload file` handles publish failures in `internal/commands/upload/publish_failure.go`: a 422 naming a same-name job in `created`, `pending`, `running` or `requeued` attempts signed-upload record cleanup, reports the outcome, and advises waiting then uploading again with `qctl describe ingestion-job <id>`. Other publish failures keep the file record and name `qctl describe file <id>`. The publish client uses the shared error wrapper so object-shaped 422 responses keep their status and the publish failure determines the exit code even if cleanup fails. Tests: `publish_failure_test.go`.
- `upload file` checks the advisory manual-fix count before its existing confirmation and upload writes (`internal/commands/upload/corrected_row_count.go`, `internal/cloud_sources/corrected_row_count.go`). `cloud_sources.Client.GetCorrectedRowCount` sends PATH's bare file name and the same optional period key as the upload. Positive counts warn only on stderr, including under `--yes` and structured output; failed or invalid checks warn that the count is unavailable and let the upload continue. The bare file name is resolved once for both requests, without directories or an added timestamp. Tests: `corrected_row_count_test.go`.
- `undelete table` prints the server's `{result, dataset_id, name, state, data_sources_recovered}` under `-o json|yaml` and `table/<id>` under `-o name`.
- `undelete cloud-source` reads `GET .../tiny`, requires a deleted source, and posts to `run-sensor-cronjob` whatever the source's type — the endpoint `enable cloud-source` uses for a sensor-eligible type. Current servers reject this recovery path with 404 (exit 3); the command keeps that exit code and explains the limitation without printing success. Older servers may accept it, and `cloudSourceRecoverNote` describes that successful recovery: ACTIVE, alerts still resolved, polling resumed when scheduled.

## file commands (act on one uploaded file)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file set-period <file-id> <period-key>` | `internal/commands/file/set_period.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` (`AssignReportingPeriod`); `datasets.NewClient` (`GetDataset`, for the table name) | `set_period_test.go` |
| `file replace-older <file-id>` | `internal/commands/file/replacement.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` (`AnswerReplacementQuestion`) | `replacement_test.go` |
| `file keep-both <file-id>` | `internal/commands/file/replacement.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` (`AnswerReplacementQuestion`) | `replacement_test.go` |
| `file send-issues-report <file-id>` | `internal/commands/file/send_issues_report.go` | `stored_items.NewClient` (`SendIssuesReportEmail`, `WaitForIssuesReportSend`) | `send_issues_report_test.go` |

Notes:
- `file set-period` is the CLI half of cosmosview's "Choose the month" on the file page. It parses the id (`cmdutil.ParseIntArgs`, positive) and checks the key's shape (`cmdutil.ValidatePeriodKey`) before `cmdutil.Bootstrap`, so a malformed one exits 2 with no request; `file/<id>` is accepted for the id (`WithArgKinds` "file", "-"). It makes one `PUT /stored-items/{id}/reporting-period` with `{period_key}` (including a creatable key, without a table pre-read), then a best-effort `GET /datasets/{dataset_id}` for the table name, skipped under `-o name`; a failed read prints `ID <n> (name unavailable)` and `dataset_name: null`, never an error. `-o json|yaml` print `{id, dataset_id, dataset_name, routing_period_key}`, `-o name` prints `file/<id>`, and the prose moves to stderr. Errors go through `stored_items.wrapAPIError`/`handleError`, so 404 exits 3, 403 exits 4, and 400 and 409 exit 2; `explainSetPeriodRefusal` adds a `Hint:` for `NO_OPEN_ROUTING_ALERT` and `REPORTING_PERIOD_ASSIGNMENT_CONFLICT`, and for `PERIOD_LOCKED` an `Instead:` line under apierror's unlock `Hint:`, and for a `CHANGE_PROPOSAL_REQUIRED` about `alert_resolve` an `Instead:` line saying how to find the file's routing alert and propose `assign_reporting_period` on it. It is not classified as destructive.
- `file replace-older` and `file keep-both` answer the replacement question on the NEW file: one `POST /stored-items/{id}/replacement-question` with `{answer: discard_old|keep_both}` (`newAnswerCommand` in `replacement.go`), after the id is parsed like `set-period`'s. `-o json|yaml` print the answered question as the API returns it, `-o name` prints `file/<id>`, and the prose (naming the older file, then `Next: qctl describe file <older-id>`) moves to stderr. `explainAnswerRefusal` adds a `Hint:` for `REPLACEMENT_QUESTION_SETTLED` and `HELD_FILE_SETTLED` (both 409, exit 2). `file replace-older` is in `cmdutil.destructiveCommands` because it ignores the older file; `keep-both` changes nothing and is not.
- `file send-issues-report` makes one `POST /stored-items/{id}/issues-report-emails` (202 `{result, send_id}`) after the id is parsed like `set-period`'s; every call queues another email. The default output states the send id, `-o json|yaml` print the response, `-o name` prints `file/<id>`, and the prose moves to stderr. `explainSendIssuesReportRefusal` adds a `Hint:` for the three 409s (`BROKER_REPORT_REFUSED`, `BROKER_REPORT_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `BROKER_REPORT_RECIPIENT_REFUSED`, exit 2), keyed on `context.reason` where the code carries one. `--wait` (`cmdutil.AddWaitFlags`) then polls `GET .../issues-report-emails/{send_id}` through `stored_items.Client.WaitForIssuesReportSend` (`pollwait.Run`, clock `SetSendWaitClock`) under `cmdutil.StartWait` until the status leaves `queued`; a 404 is retried until a poll first finds the send, because the POST's row commits after its 202. `sendOutcomeError` turns `skipped` into exit 2 with a `Hint:` from `sendReasonAdvice` (the 409s' advice keyed on the reason) and `failed` into exit 1; a timeout is `ErrSendWaitTimeout`, exit 124. It is not classified as destructive. The `file` group also answers to `files`, the spelling atlas's ops pings use.
- `describe file` makes one extra `GET /stored-items/{id}/replacement-question` (`replacementQuestion` in `internal/commands/describe/file.go`) and shows the result under `status.replacement_question` when there is one; a 403 or 404 on it leaves the field out silently, any other failure with a `warning:` on stderr. `status.submission_comment_thread` comes from the file record itself. An open question prints the `file replace-older` / `file keep-both` commands on stderr, and a thread prints its `describe comment-thread <id> --table-id <id>`.
- `describe file` makes one more extra read, `GET /submissions/files/{id}` (`submissionProgress` in `internal/commands/describe/file.go`, `stored_items.Client.GetSubmissionProgress`), and shows it under `status.submission` through `stored_items.NewFileSubmission`, which keeps the progress's state, outcome, series, period, blocking alert and steps and nothing else; a 403 or 404 leaves it out silently, any other failure with a `warning:` on stderr. Each step naming a `change_proposal` prints `qctl describe change-proposal <id>` on stderr. Tests: `file_submission_test.go`.

## undo / redo commands (walk your eligible changes to one object)

| Command | File | Domain Client | Test File |
|---|---|---|---|
| `undo table [table-id-or-name]` | `internal/commands/undoredo/table.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |
| `undo cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/undoredo/cloud_source.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |
| `undo destination <id>` | `internal/commands/undoredo/destination.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |
| `redo table [table-id-or-name]` | `internal/commands/undoredo/table.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |
| `redo cloud-source <id>` | `internal/commands/undoredo/cloud_source.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |
| `redo destination <id>` | `internal/commands/undoredo/destination.go` | `history.NewClient` | `undo_test.go` |

**Notes:**
- Undo and redo select only the caller's own eligible changes for every role. Six leaves, one implementation. `internal/commands/undoredo` builds each subcommand from a `history.Direction`; only the resolver differs (`cmdutil.ResolveTableID` for the table, `cmdutil.ParseIntArgs` for the two numeric ids). `history.RunFlow` in `internal/history` owns the ordering guarantee — step, then act with those ids — so it lives in one function rather than six.
- The step call is mandatory and there is no `--dry-run`; its message is printed ahead of the confirmation whenever it renders to anything at all, and a message that renders empty is skipped rather than emitted as a blank line. The ids it returns are the optimistic-concurrency token: qctl echoes them so the backend refuses undo or redo if the selected history ids change. An edit that leaves that selection unchanged does not trigger this check.
- `page_type` never appears in the CLI. It is a fact about which screen the browser is on, and the CLI's equivalent is which subcommand was typed: `table` → `DataView`, `cloud-source` → `DataSourceModelEdit`, `destination` → `DestinationEdit`.
- All six annotate `catalog.For("history")` and document onto `internal/resources/pages/history.md`, which keeps the undo semantics in one place instead of triplicated across three pages.

| `guide [topic]` | `internal/commands/guide/guide.go` |

Notes:
- `skill` verbs use `~/.agents/skills/qctl` for Codex; `--legacy` selects `$CODEX_HOME/skills/qctl` or `~/.codex/skills/qctl`. Install the preferred copy, then explicitly uninstall the legacy copy to migrate. Default Codex status reports an existing legacy copy separately.
- `internal/agentskill/notice.go` reports changed managed skills and legacy Codex copies through root's pre-run hook. Notices use stderr and a best-effort 24-hour cache per host under `~/.qctl`; `skill`, help/version and completion commands skip the check.
- `auth logout`: revokes this machine's CLI session on the server before deleting the local credential. It finds the session by matching the credential's `TokenName` — minted with a random suffix by `auth login` (`internal/auth/tokenname.go`) — against `delegations[].name`, because the exchange returns no id and the bearer token is opaque. `--local` skips the server call. It reads every distinct stored copy with `CredentialStore.RetrieveCopies` -- the keyring and the fallback file can hold two logins' tokens, each its own delegation, and the keyring is read even under the plaintext opt-in because `Delete` removes a keyring entry there too -- and runs the revoke once per copy with that copy's own token. A failed revoke keeps the credential so a retry can still authenticate; when only one of two copies failed, `DeleteKeyringCopy` or `DeleteFileCopy` removes just the other. The local delete reports what it could not remove: `CredentialStore.Delete` returns a `*auth.DeleteError` naming which of the keyring and file halves it cleared, and the command turns that into the message and the by-hand remedy. Domain client: `internal/auth/client.go` (`ListDelegations`, `RevokeDelegation`). Tests: `internal/commands/auth/logout_test.go`, and `logout_copies_test.go` for two stored copies.
- `auth sessions --never-used` filters the existing token join to sessions whose tokens all expired without any recorded first use. No new default column or age threshold: `first_used_at` is the earliest token first use, exposed in JSON/YAML and optional `--columns first_used_at`. Missing token rows are skipped with a warning; a failed token lookup makes this filtered invocation fail with the original exit code. Implementation: `session_liveness.go` (`classifyTokenUsage`) and `sessions.go` (`sessionRows`). Tests: `internal/commands/auth/sessions_usage_test.go`, `internal/commands/auth/session_liveness_test.go`.
- `auth sessions`: lists the active CLI delegations returned for the current user and organization, marking the one correlated with the stored credential as current, and reporting for each whether it can still authenticate. That last part is a join qctl computes, not a field the server returns: a delegation carries no expiry column, so the command also calls `ListAPITokens` (`GET /orgs/{org}/users/me/api-tokens`, both `include_*` forced on) and reduces each delegation's tokens to `active`, `expired`, `revoked` or `unknown` in `session_liveness.go`, with EXPIRES the expiry behind that verdict. A failed token lookup leaves every row `unknown` and warns on stderr rather than losing the list; `revoke --expired` makes the opposite call and refuses outright, since on missing information every session looks equally dead. `revoke --expired` sweeps exactly the `expired` and `revoked` rows -- never an `unknown`, never this machine's own -- and reports what it left behind; after successful named or expired revokes it also removes stored copies whose `token_name` belongs to a delegation actually revoked, including a non-current keyring or file credential. Under the plaintext-token opt-in that cleanup still reads the keyring; the argument is optional, and supplying it alongside `--expired`, or neither, is exit 2 before any request. `auth sessions revoke <name-or-id>` revokes every exact-name match, while an ID addresses exactly one delegation (including legacy unnamed sessions); revoking the current session also removes its local credential. The match-and-revoke loop is `revokeSessions` in `sessions.go`, shared with `auth logout` and with login's replacement cleanup; it is now `matchDelegations` plus `revokeEach`, the latter shared with the `--expired` path, and returns the delegations it ATTEMPTED (not every match -- one the loop never reached is one the server certainly still holds) and, separately, how many it really revoked, so `describeRevoke` claims `Revoked` only for the latter — a session that ends between the list and its DELETE (404, or a 200 carrying `success:false`) is a finished outcome at exit 0. Revoking the current session deletes the stored credential on both outcomes and says so — the already-ended path would otherwise log the machine out while printing only `nothing was revoked`. `forgetEndedCopies` reads `RetrieveCopies` and deletes only the copies whose `token_name` is among the matched delegations, so a second credential in the other half, belonging to a session this revoke did not touch, is kept; `describeForgotten` names it and names `auth login` unless `Retrieve` would now return it unexpired. A revoke that fails partway (`revokeFailedError`) reports the count it revoked first and keeps the credential, since which delegation was this machine's is not knowable there. Domain client: `internal/auth/client.go` (`ListDelegations`, `RevokeDelegation`). Test: `internal/commands/auth/sessions_test.go`.
- `auth login` and the ops path of `auth switch-org` share `storeCredential` in `login.go`: it reads every stored copy of the credential it is about to replace through `storedCopies` — both halves via `CredentialStore.RetrieveCopies`, or the fallback file alone under the plaintext opt-in, since `Store` writes nothing else there — stores the new one first (so a local write failure leaves the old one usable rather than stranding both), then `retireReplacedCopies` reads the copies again and runs `retireReplacedCredential` for each one no half holds any more. Reading every copy is what reaches both halves: a keyring write overwrites the keyring's copy even when the fallback file's newer copy is the one `Retrieve` picks, and a refused keyring write replaces the file's copy and removes the keyring's. The copy `Retrieve` picked is reported as the previous session, the other by name and location. Which token authenticates that revoke depends on whether the old one still can — a live credential revokes its own session, while one that is expired locally or rejected by the server is cleaned up by name with the *new* token, since only that reaches a delegation whose own token no longer authenticates. Three cases deliberately revoke nothing: a live copy the second read still returns (it keeps a local handle, and revoking it would break the commands that read it), a replaced `TokenName` equal to the new session's, and an unnamed credential that has expired. A failed cleanup warns on stderr but does not turn a successful login into a failure, and never as two contradictory lines — a partial revoke across several same-named delegations reports itself once. That second read is addressed by the organization the store was addressed with, not by the `organization_id` inside the stored JSON, which `Retrieve` does not backfill. A failed *store* is an error, and `storeFailedError` first revokes the session the exchange had just minted, using that session's own token; `CredentialStore.Store` rolls its fallback-file write back on that path, so the file is not left holding a token whose session is then revoked. `reportStoreResult` is what prints the outcome for both commands. Tests: `internal/commands/auth/store_credential_test.go`, `internal/commands/auth/login_test.go`, `internal/commands/auth/switch_org_test.go`.
- `auth logout --all`: the step to take after a password or MFA reset — it ends **every** session and bearer credential the user holds on that server, on every device and in every organization, not just this machine's. It is a different route from the plain logout above: `POST /orgs/{org}/logout?all_sessions=true` (`RevokeAllSessions` in `internal/auth/client.go`), which atlas-l14.34 opened to bearer callers. `user_id` is never sent — that is the only thing that would narrow the revocation to one organization, so the global promise in the output depends on it, and `RevokeAllSessions` refuses a response whose `scope` is not `global` rather than reporting it as one. The counts printed are the server's `delegations_revoked` and `api_tokens_revoked`, never local state. Afterwards it deletes the stored credential for every organization it can name on that server (`CredentialStore.StoredOrganizations` for the fallback file, plus every organization the config's contexts cache for the same server; the OS keyring cannot be enumerated). `--all` with `--local` is exit 2 before any request. Test: `internal/commands/auth/logout_all_test.go`.
- `guide`: registry + content in `internal/commands/guide/topics.go` (topic list) and `internal/commands/guide/topics/*.md` (embedded via `go:embed`).
- `guide` test files: `internal/commands/guide/guide_test.go`, `internal/commands/guide/topics_test.go`.
- `guide` is registered in `internal/commands/root/root.go` (not a "Parent Command File" itself — it has no subcommands, only topic args).
- `guide` topics: the list is generated into `docs/patterns.md`'s "Guide Topics" block from `internal/commands/guide/topics.go`. Each topic is a `topics.go` entry plus `internal/commands/guide/topics/<name>.md`.
- `diff -f <file>` is a top-level verb, not a subcommand of `apply`. It previews a Concept create/update, a Table create/update, a TableRule patch, a TableSchema apply, a TableSeries apply, a Deliverable apply or a LinkedTable apply without applying it — exit 0 = no differences, 1 = differences, 2 = a rejected input, which includes a multi-document file (diff previews one document; `apply -f` applies them all). Its kind table is `diff.diffKinds`, deliberately smaller than `apply.genericKinds`. Full behavior is described in the apply section's notes; the row above exists so the file map is complete.
- `info` is `config current-context` under another name — `NewInfoCommand` builds the same command and rewrites `Use`/`Short`/`Long`. Registered on root in `internal/commands/root/root.go`, not under `config`.
- `info` / `config current-context` declare `table,json,yaml`. The table view prints the stored context only. `-o json|yaml` prints `{name, server, output, organization{id,name}, organizations[], scope}`, where `scope` is `{server, server_source, organization_id, organization_name, organization_source}` after `--server`, `--org`, `QCTL_SERVER` and `QCTL_ORG`, each source `flag`, `env` or `context`. Every key is always present, with null for a value selected nowhere. The server comes from `config.SelectServer`, the precedence `ResolveServerWithSource` renders its phrase from, and the organization from `cmdutil.ResolveOrganizationWithSource`, the resolver `Bootstrap` uses. So an organization that is selected but does not resolve exits 2 here as it would on the next API command. Tests: `internal/commands/config/current_context_test.go`.
- The other commands in this section that print prose, a script, a page or a diff declare `table` alone, so `-o json|yaml|name` exits 2: `auth login`, `logout`, `sessions revoke`, `switch-org`, the three `cache aliases` verbs, `config use-context`, `delete-context` and `list-contexts`, the five `completion` leaves, `docs`, `guide`, `skill install`/`uninstall`, `diff`, `help` and `version`. `auth me` and `auth sessions` declare the four they render. `TestLocalCommandsDeclareTheirFormats` fails for a runnable command in these groups that declares no set, except `config set-context`, whose own `--output` is the value it stores.
<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: explain-schema-sources -->
- `explain` supports, in addition to the API-backed resources, the manifest kinds served by static offline schemas — the source of truth is `manifestSchemas` in `internal/commands/explain/manifests.go` (`change-review-policy`, `data-review-policy`, `deliverable`, `notification-preference`, `rule-review-policy`, `table-rule`, `table-schema`), reachable as `explain.ManifestSchemaNames()` — plus `composite-dataset`, `linked-table` (`CompositeDatasetApplyManifest`).
<!-- END GENERATED: explain-schema-sources -->
- `api-resources`: cold-start index of every resource → verbs → kind → guide, computed by `catalog.Build(cmd.Root())` walking the annotated cobra tree and joining it with the embedded resource pages. No auth, no API call (offline). `-o json`/`-o yaml` add `verbs`, `schema`, and `summary` alongside the legacy `verb` column, plus a ready-to-run `explain` command on **every** row — every resource has a page, so `explain` is never empty. Ask `schema == ""`, not `explain == ""`, to find the resources with no schema document. Test: `internal/commands/api_resources/api_resources_test.go`. Registered in `internal/commands/root/root.go`.
- There is no hand-written resource registry. Existence is owned by the cobra tree — every command carries a `resource` annotation (`catalog.For`, `catalog.Parent`, or `catalog.Dispatcher`; see `internal/catalog/tree.go`). Semantics are owned by one markdown page per resource under `internal/resources/pages/`. `internal/catalog` (`catalog.Build`/`catalog.Lookup`) joins the two, and is what `api-resources`, `explain`, and the `create` redirect all read from. The `apply --help` kinds line does **not** read from the catalog — as the apply section's notes above describe, it and the command's `catalog.Dispatcher` annotation are both generated independently from `apply.genericKinds`, so they cannot disagree with each other even though the help text itself never calls into `internal/catalog`. The drift tests live in `internal/catalog/*_test.go`: `TestEveryCommandIsAnnotated` (no un-annotated command), `TestEveryResourceHasPage` (no annotation without a page), and `TestDispatcherKindsResolve` (no dispatched kind — `apply -f`/`diff -f` — without exactly one page declaring it).

| `cloud_sources` | `internal/cloud_sources/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard; `sensor_eligibility.go` holds `SensorEligible`, qctl's one copy of atlas's sensor-eligible source types (`SENSOR_ELIGIBLE_SOURCE_TYPES`), which no generated enum carries |

| `composite_dry_runs` | `internal/composite_dry_runs/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | **Returns `(*Client, error)`**; `Get` returns a `Detail` that decodes again, from the body, two fields from `CompositeDryRunJobDetail` (`structured_summary` as the wire object, nil when null; `error_context` with its integers intact); `Examples` returns the body beside the decoded page; `Launch` returns the body beside the decoded ids and forwards a 429's headers; `manifest.go` holds the describe view (`ToManifest`, `ToRawManifest`); `wait.go` holds `Wait` (polls `Get` until `Settled`, clock replaceable with `SetWaitClock`), `Verdict`, `WriteProgress` and `WriteOutcome`, the `--wait` of `create composite-dry-run-job` and `apply linked-table` |
| `linked_tables` | `internal/linked_tables/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | **Returns `(*Client, error)`**; `Apply`/`Validate` post a `LinkedTable` manifest; `revisions.go` holds `LatestRevision`, `GetRevision`, `GetRevisionSpec` (the revision's spec, read from the response body), `GetRevisionDetail` (the spec plus the group context, read the same way), `ResolveRevision` (latest when no UUID is given, otherwise the named revision, checked against the table's group), `EnableRevision` and `RetryBuild` (`POST .../composite-spec-groups/{group}/retry-build`); `diff.go` holds `CreateChanges`/`UpdateChanges`, the comparison behind `diff -f`; `export.go` holds `ExportManifest`, the manifest behind `get linked-table` |

| `rule_families` | `internal/rule_families/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard; `resolve.go` holds `ResolveSlug`, exact family-slug resolution across catalog pages |

| `dataset_rules` | `internal/dataset_rules/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | **Returns `(*Client, error)`**; also `move.go` (`FindRuleInList`, `PlanMove`) behind `table-rule move`; `param_edit.go` (`ParamEdit`, `ParseParamEdit`, `ParseParamValue`, `SameParamValue`) behind `--set-param`/`--unset-param` on `suggestion edit`, `table-rule propose` and `change-proposal edit`; `filter.go` (`GetDatasetRulesByState`) exhausts matching pages, filters lifecycle state and pages the filtered set |
| `rule_row_ignores` | `internal/rule_row_ignores/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); `List`, `Create`, `UnIgnore` over `/datasets/{id}/rule-row-ignores`. `List` ANDs a rule+row filter client-side (atlas lets `row_id` win and drops `dataset_rule_id`); `UnIgnore` takes a listed record, never a bare id, because the DELETE route is unscoped by id (qctl-tas). Errors through `internal/comments` like `datasets` |
| `dataset_kinds` | `internal/dataset_kinds/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard |
| `concepts` | `internal/concepts/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | List/Get, exact-slug apply preparation, create/PATCH, and exhaustive reference resolution; `manifest.go` builds the shared apply/diff plan |
| `table_series` | `internal/table_series/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Unpaginated List/Get with embedded members; `ListSubmissionPeriods` reads the caller's accessible periods for upload; `ListUploadable` (`uploadable.go`) reads series upload targets through the upload-permission endpoint, and `UploadableRows` projects their source IDs, routing modes and selectable periods; `GetDeadlineSchedule` (`schedule.go`) reads the deadline schedule and `PreviewDeadlineSchedule` POSTs a map body to its preview, each sending `period_key` only when one is given, and the schedule PATCH goes through `PatchTableSeriesDeadlineScheduleWithBodyWithResponse` with a map body, never the typed body, whose fields lack omitempty; exact case-sensitive name or full-UUID resolver; `errors.go` aliases `internal/comments` |
| `deliverables` | `internal/deliverables/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | `List` (jsonnum-decoded), `Get`, `ListRevisions`, `GetRevision`, `SheetRows`, `ResolveRevision`, `LatestRun` and `Detail`; `runs.go` holds `ListRuns`, `GetRun` (both jsonnum-decoded), `RunStates` and `RunStateLabel`, and `run_display.go` `RunRows`; `readiness.go` holds `Readiness` (its own 45s deadline, decoded into the `ReadinessSummary`/`Readiness` wire mirror) and `ReadinessBuckets`, and `readiness_display.go` `FormatReadinessSummary` and `WriteReadiness`; `run_detail.go` holds `ParseRunID`, `DescribeRun` (run, deliverable, revision tree, then readiness with `limit=1` unless the run is cancelled; a readiness failure returns the detail with its error) and `FormatRun`; `lifecycle.go` holds `Archive`, `Recover` and `DeleteRevision`; `enable.go` holds `Validate`, `Enable` (a 422 `REVISION_INVALID` becomes a `*RevisionInvalidError` and a 409 listing runs a `*RunsBlockedError`, each unwrapping to the `*APIError`), `FollowingRuns`, `ResolveEnableTarget`, `ResolveTemplateTarget` and `FormatIssue`; `preview.go` holds `Preview`, `FormatPreview` and `WritePreviewCounts`; `run_params.go` holds `ReadRunParams`, `ParseRunParams`, `RequiredRunParams` and `ValidateRequiredRunParams`; `template.go` holds `ValidateTemplatePath`, `TemplateKeepsVBA`, `UploadTemplate` (presigned transfer through `internal/fileul`), `FinalizeTemplate` (a 409 `SKELETON_LOSSY` becomes a `*TemplateLossyError`, unwrapping to the `*APIError`), `FidelityLossLines` and `FormatTemplate`; `column_suggestions.go` holds `AcceptColumnSuggestions`, `ResolveDraftTarget` and `FormatColumnSuggestions`, with nullable hints in `EmptyColumn`; `confirm_columns.go` holds `ConfirmColumns` and `FormatConfirmColumns`; `row_filter_report.go` holds `SheetRowFilterReport` (a sheet binding's row filter report as `RowFilterReportResult`: `row_filters.Report` plus the response body); `plan_row_filters.go` counts the row filters a plan's saves send or keep and holds `WriteRowFilterCount` (one count under its `Row filter <key> on table N:` heading, which `Plan.WriteRowFilterCounts` and `deliverable test-filter -f` both print), `SheetFilterKey` and `SheetTableKey`; `manifest_cells.go` parses `spec.cells` and normalizes A1 references; `plan_cells.go` reconciles cells and `cells_write.go` sends raw POST/PATCH/DELETE requests; `manifest_sheets.go` parses `spec.sheets` (`ParseColumnKey`, `ColumnLetter`, `SheetsFromTree`, `SetSheets`) and `export.go` holds `ExportManifest`; `bindings_write.go` holds the raw sheet and column binding writes (`Create`/`Patch`/`DeleteSheetBinding`, `Create`/`Patch`/`DeleteColumnBinding`; a 422 `BINDING_INVALID` lists its issues), which `plan_bindings.go` plans and `apply_bindings.go` sends; a resolver matching an exact slug or name, a full UUID, or a UUID prefix of 8+ hex digits over a complete listing; `errors.go` aliases `internal/comments` |
| `orgs` | `internal/orgs/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard |
| `review_policies` | `internal/review_policies/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error) |
| `data_reviews` | `internal/data_reviews/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); data-row review routes, shared UUID validation, user-directory display names, and the `internal/comments` object-shaped-422 aliases |
| `rule_reviews` | `internal/rule_reviews/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); also `users.go` directory, `errors.go` APIError, `render.go` describe payload |
| `rule_merges` | `internal/rule_merges/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | **Returns `(*Client, error)`**; `Preview`/`Get`/`Apply` over `/datasets/{target}/rule-merges`, each returning the body as received beside the decoded merge; `Resolution` wraps the generated resolution so `Apply` can send `row_filter: null`; `resolution.go` holds the `-f` document (`RuleMergeResolutionKind`, `ParseResolution`, `ToRequest` keyed by SOURCE rule, `UnresolvedConflicts`, `Skeleton`); `render.go` `RenderMerge`; `wait.go` `Wait`/`Settled`/`WriteProgress`/`Verdict`; `errors.go` the `internal/comments` alias |
| `rule_suggestions` | `internal/rule_suggestions/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | **Returns `(*Client, error)`**; also `errors.go` APIError, `render.go` describe payload |
| `comment_threads` | `internal/comment_threads/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); also `render.go` (summary rows + describe payload). Errors/mentions/transcripts come from `internal/comments` via type aliases |
| `value_mapping` | `internal/value_mapping/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); one method, `Recommend`, over the ORG-scoped `POST /orgs/{org}/value-mapping/recommendation` — which is why it is not a `datasets` method: the table rides in the body, not the path. `RecommendRequest` has no settings-model field on purpose (the view resolves one from `dataset_id`), and `WarningItemID` is `warning_item_id_` on the wire. `errors.go` is the three-line `internal/comments` alias the object-shaped 422 needs |
| `comments` | `internal/comments/` | *(no client)* | Shared home of the unified `Comment` model: `errors.go`, `render.go`, `users.go`. Re-exported by `comment_threads` and `rule_reviews` |
| `user_submitted` | `internal/user_submitted/` | *(no client)* | Shared kernel for `alert submit\|retract` and `warning submit\|retract`: aliases, caps, request builders (`user_submitted.go`), partial-success renderer and exit-code choice (`report.go`) |
| `notifications` | `internal/notifications/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); seven endpoints (list, unread-count, read, mark-all-read, dismiss, dismiss-all, dismiss-by-type). No domain `APIError`: these views raise plain `APIError(404, ...)`, not the object-shaped 422 that `client.LastCapturedError()` exists for, so `apierror.HandleHTTPErrorFromBytes` is enough — and the one route that could 422, `dismiss-by-type` (the type is a path segment), is guarded client-side by `ParseTypes`. `types.go` holds the `notification_type` vocabulary, the `needs_you` subset and `ParseTypes`. `GET /notifications/rule-upgrades` is deliberately **not** wrapped: it is dominated by `get notifications --type rule_upgrade`, which also accepts `--unread` where the alias route does not |
| `slack_config` | `internal/slack_config/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); `Get` and `Put` over `/orgs/{id}/slack/config`, errors through the shared `comments` aliases. `alert_types.go` holds `Change` and `WithAlertTypes`, the per alert type read-modify-write `slack set-alert-type` goes through |
| `history` | `internal/history/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error). Both halves of the ledger: `client.go` reads it (`GetHistory`, behind `get history`), `undo.go`/`flow.go` walk it (`Step`/`Act`/`RunFlow`, behind `undo`/`redo`). `display.go` is the table projection, `enums.go` the filter vocabularies, `msg.go` the terminal rendering of the backend's undo markdown, `errors.go` the `internal/comments` alias — needed by the four POSTs, whose object-shaped 422 the generated parser rejects, and deliberately unused by the listing, which `apiClient.HandleError` already covers |
| `pii_access_requests` | `internal/pii_access_requests/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb, shared)` | Requester `Create`, `List`, `Withdraw`, selecting generated first-party or third-party operations; admin `ListAll`, `Approve`, `Deny` on the first-party `pii-grant-requests` routes; errors through `internal/comments`, 429 headers through `apierror.HandleHTTPErrorWithHeader` |
| `privilege_requests` | `internal/privilege_requests/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); all six `/api/ops/{org}/privilege-*` routes: `Create`, `List`, `Get`, `Approve`, `Reject`, `Audit`. The listing and the audit are platform-wide whichever org is in the path. `Create` can answer 201 already `Approved` (the sole-eligible-ops exemption), so callers read the returned status. `Approve`/`Reject` answer an unknown id with 400, not 404, so only `Get` yields exit 3. `Reject` takes no reason, because the controller discards `rejection_reason`. `vocab.go` holds `StatusNames`/`ActionNames` (sorted wire spellings, pinned to `api.StringEnumValues`), `ParseStatus`/`ParseAction` (case-insensitive, canonical value out, exit 2 otherwise) and `ActionOf` (old/new value → `escalate`/`demote`, anything else `<old>→<new>` verbatim). `errors.go` is the three-line `internal/comments` alias |
| `change_proposals` | `internal/change_proposals/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); `List` and `Get` on `/api/orgs/{org}/change-proposals`, both decoding through `jsonnum.Unmarshal`. `ListPage` is `{items}` with no total. `Get` returns `Detail` (the proposal plus `review`, `impact_at_submit`, `impact_diff`). `write.go` holds `Create` (201), `Edit`, `Submit`, `Withdraw`, `Approve`, `Reject`, `RequestChanges` and `RerunDryRun`, decoding the same way; `render.go` the text sections `describe change-proposal` and `change-proposal approve` share (`WritePayload`, `PayloadManifest`, `WriteReview`, `WriteOverlaps`, `DependsOnLine`, `ExecutionError`, `NextCommand`); `document.go` holds `Document` (`ParseDocument` for one `AlertResolution`/`WarningResolution` manifest, `Payload` from the spec's `ToRequest`, `CreateRequest`) and `RuleParamsRequest`, the `dataset_rule_patch` create body; `rule_render.go` the rule-param sections (`writeRuleImpact`, `ParamsDiff`, `WriteParamsDiff`, `RuleOutcome`, `RuleOutcomeKeys`, `RuleTargetState`; `RuleParams` is in `render.go`). `vocab.go` holds the state and proposable-operation vocabularies pinned to `api.*` constants (`LiveStates`, `IsLive`, `ParseStates`, `ParseOperation`, `ParseID`, `Target`, `TargetKind`, `RuleRef`), `users.go` the directory sweep, `errors.go` the `internal/comments` alias plus `Explain`, the 409 next-step hints, and `IsProposalConflict` |
| `pkg/actionfields` | `internal/pkg/actionfields/actionfields.go` | *(no client)* | Shared validator for a resolve manifest's `spec` against `api.AlertActionFields` / `api.WarningActionFields`: `Present` (supplied means non-nil for a scalar and NON-EMPTY for a list or map, so `ids: []` is refused) and `Validate`, which reports every problem in one exit-2 error. One implementation for both domains, the way `internal/comments` is one implementation of the object-shaped-422 handling |
| `pkg/manifest` | `internal/pkg/manifest/` | *(no client)* | `documents.go` is the `---` splitter `apply -f`, `diff -f` and both `resolve -f` runners share; `stream.go` is `Run`, the sequential per-document runner behind `alert resolve -f` / `warning resolve -f` — one outcome per document, `--fail-fast` (which names every untried document `document N: skipped (--fail-fast)` and counts them in the error, matching `applyStream`'s `reportSkipped`), `ErrDeclined` for a declined prompt (in a MULTI-document file a skip, not a failure: `document N: skipped`, the rest still run, and the run exits 0 — in a ONE-document file an ordinary failure at exit 1, since a one-document `-f` is the manifest spelling of `--action`), and a `StreamError` that wraps the FIRST failure so `qerrors.GetExitCode` still sees its HTTP status; `manifest.go` holds `StrictUnmarshal` |
| `submissions` | `internal/submissions/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard; the MGA broker board reads (`SeriesBoard`, `IntakeBoard`, `SeriesCounts`) and the period waiver (`Waive`, `WithdrawWaiver`), `display.go` for the table rows, board header, count line and one period's `StatusLine`; errors through `internal/comments` |
| `email_intake` | `internal/email_intake/client.go` | `NewClient(base, orgID, verb)` | Standard (no error); the eight org-scoped `/data-sources/{id}/email-intake` routes: `Get`, `SetEnabled`, `Rotate`, `ListSenders`, `AddSender`, `RemoveSender`, `PreviewWelcome`, `ResendWelcome`, plus `ResolveSender` (email or user-id lookup in the sender listing). Errors through `internal/comments` aliases |
